- Changed: The step `ci.d self-test` of both jobs (capture run, jobtime-3, ruling ST-22) runs up to CI_D_SELF_TEST_JOBS self-tests at a time, the driver's own cases among them, by default as many as the runner has processors; each self-test has its standard output, its standard error and its TMPDIR of its own, and the driver prints each script's output and its `seconds` line in the order of the names, so the log and the verdicts read as in a run one at a time; a self-test past CI_D_SCRIPT_LIMIT_S seconds (1200 by default) fails with its name; each self-test starts through perl in a process group of its own with INT and QUIT at their defaults, INT or TERM to the driver ends every self-test that it started, and standard error written after a self-test was printed is printed before the recap; the step `ci.d` still runs one script at a time. The macOS sources move code into functions with no change of behaviour: one table drives the tap mask and the classifier, an owned type holds the tap thread, the foreground poller takes its readers as parameters and its poll step is a function, the foreground state and `emit_metadata_only` move from `macos` into `macos::foreground`, the screen frame cap is the bounded type `macos::screen::FrameRateCap` (`SCREEN_FRAME_CAP` replaces `macos::SCREEN_FRAME_CAP_FPS`, and `ScreenSource::new` takes it), and the stream configuration sets its queue depth of 8 from a named constant; `secure_input_macos::hold_secure_input` returns the status with which the system refuses a hold (`SecureInputRefused`), and a refused hold releases nothing; a timed run that was asked to hold secure input and whose hold the system refuses stops its sources and ends with the error "the system refused the secure-input hold (status <n>)" and no report (a new `DaemonError::SecureInputRefused`); the input source takes the maker of its tap as a value. The check scripts/r3/ci.d/10-r3-layout.sh (agent-setup run, U2.2, rulings CP-102 and CP-103 (1)) also fails when webpki-root-certs, whose licence (CDLA-Permissive-2.0) is not on the list of A0.8, is in the graph that cargo resolves for the workspace on aarch64-apple-darwin, x86_64-apple-darwin, x86_64-pc-windows-msvc or aarch64-pc-windows-msvc, and its self-test holds five fixtures of that check. `WebOrigin::parse` of halvo-schema refuses a host whose last label is a number and that is no four decimal octets (`127.1`, `0x7f000001`, `2130706433`, `0177.0.0.1`, `1.2.3.04`) with the rule `origin_ipv4`, so that it takes the same origins as `WebOrigin::of_address`, which a browser's address goes through; and the value of a stored secret holds at least one byte: `SecretText::parse` replaces `SecretText::new` and refuses an empty value with the rule `empty_secret`, as the reader of `secret_reply` does (agent-setup run, U1.5, contracts.md A7.4 and A6.2).
- Added: A ci.d script's self-test runs alone when its first ten lines hold `# self-test: alone` or when scripts/ci/self-test-alone.txt names it (capture run, jobtime-3), and 12-test writes every Running and Doc-tests line and every test's result line of its cargo test run to target/ci-artifacts/12-test/tests.txt, so that a later script can read which tests ran instead of running a crate's tests again. Tests that fail on fourteen wrong forms of the macOS capture code that passed every test before (a tap callback that ignores secure input, an inverted secure-input byte, an active tap, a re-enable that disables, a stop that leaves the tap thread running, a mask without KeyDown, an inverted self-exclusion, a 60 fps cap, audio in the stream configuration, sequence 0 and 1970 stamps, off sources that emit, a camera event per frame, a frame handler that retains its sample), each held by a required row of the new crates/halvo-capture/src/macos/mutants.rows; a test that compares `secure_input_active` with the session's own reading through a real hold; three grant-needing input tests, ignored by default, under `macos::input::granted_tests`; tests without a grant on the tap's callback, its maker, the hold's status and its release, which a stand-in release counts, and on the two system calls of the production hold, which a test reads by address and three required rows of crates/halvo-capture/src/mutants.rows hold; the hold tests take one lock across processes and give back each hold that they take; and a SAFETY note at each unsafe block of the macOS sources, which now deny `clippy::undocumented_unsafe_blocks`. The crate halvo-connect (agent-setup run, U2.2, A11.2) holds the traits Mail and Calendar and Graph, one Graph-shaped adapter for both, which reads a kept list once per item, offers no other request and holds a token only for the call; its HTTP client is reqwest 0.13.5 with rustls 0.23.46 on the ring provider and the OS trust store. The root Cargo.lock names one package under another licence, webpki-root-certs 1.0.11 (CDLA-Permissive-2.0), which only the wasm32 target uses and no build of Halvo compiles (ruling CP-102). The schema types of the checkpoint list in halvo-schema (agent-setup run, U1.5, contracts.md A7.1 to A7.4, ruling CP-100): the record `AgentCheckpoint` of the kind `agent_checkpoint` with its states `planned`, `made`, `gone` and `left`, the runner body `checkpoint`, the coordinator bodies `list_checkpoints`, over all agents with `workflow` and `agent` null, and `mark_checkpoint`, and their replies, each with one strict reader, golden lines under `tests/golden/r3/` and its names in `tests/golden/names.txt`; and the six kind names of origin own in `halvo_schema::executable::NAMES` and the names file, with a test that every kind name, the family and every field key of the run's `KINDS` stands in `NAMES` but for the names left out, and that `NAMES` names no other kind (ruling CP-101 (b)). Unit 7.1b of the billing run: scripts/r7/ci.d/10-r7-account.sh cleans the packages of account/ before its build and reads the dep-info files that this build wrote for them; it refuses a listed file that, made real on the disk, is a symbolic link, is not there, or lies outside account/ and outside the out directory that the same dep-info file names for the crate's build script, so a path attribute or an include!, include_str! or include_bytes! of a file outside account/ fails, as do a tracked file under target/account and a package of account/ with no dep-info file; its self-test gains a failing fixture for each of the five rules that had none (the record line's name and path, the three rules on the form of a ban line, the manifest path of a local package), real builds of each form in the macos job and includes-inside in both jobs, and in the windows job it leaves 23 of the cases that reach the source reader to the macos job and runs 14: three controls, the cases whose input only Windows spells so, and the cases whose names hold bytes that the tools of the windows runner read in their own way; and halvo-wire's Request has a compile_fail example for a write of each of its four fields.
- Fixed: The macOS capture sources keep two objects that the OS still uses alive: the input tap's port is cleared before the tap is released, so a copy of it can no longer re-enable a released tap, and the screen source keeps its stream output and queue for the life of the process when a start or a stop of its stream does not complete within 5 s or the stop reports an error, after the output gave back its handle of the sink, so a timed run still ends; new tests hold the re-enable on a tap-disabled event (the required row tap-not-reenabled-on-disable), the layout of the tap list entry against CGEventTapInformation, and that `ScreenSource` is not `Send`, and tests without a grant hold the lines that connect the sources to those functions (the tap's reader, options and mask read back from one value, the delegate's message sent to a real stream output, and the process ids given to the selection), each with a required row.
- Added: The driver and native dialog trial of the interface run (U0.4, contracts.md I11.5, ruling RD-42) in `tools/r4-trials/driver/`: a crate with a workspace of its own that starts the trial app with an embedded WebDriver server (`tauri-plugin-webdriver` 0.2.4 or `tauri-plugin-wdio-webdriver` 1.5.1, by cargo feature) and shows the native dialog as `tauri-plugin-dialog` 2.8.4's message dialog or as the system's own (NSAlert, TaskDialogIndirect); `read.sh` prints its reading lines on a Mac, among them, for each form and system, what the app receives on a failed call, a cancel response, an unlisted response and a timeout, read from the sources at the pinned versions; `run.sh` makes its cells on a hosted runner and refuses elsewhere; the dialog readers press controls through the accessibility interface with no posted input event; and `verdict.mjs` computes the verdict lines of `docs/r4/trials/driver.md` and `docs/r4/trials/dialog.md`, failing a dialog form that any outcome but the press on its allowing control can make allow, and refuses, with exit 2, a file with a cell or a reading missing or given twice, a line that is not in the exact form of its kind, a `driven=no` cell with a value, a figure line, a carriage return or a wrong line end. The two files end in `verdict driver hosted_mac=webdriver hosted_win=webdriver` and `verdict dialog hosted_mac=system hosted_win=system driven_mac=yes driven_win=yes`. In halvo-secrets (billing run, 7.7), a read of a macOS build with the feature keychain also reads the file of the file store and moves it once: when the file holds a credential and the Keychain holds none, it adds it to the Keychain (only-create), reads it back with equal bytes, and only then removes the file, which it first claims by one rename to a private name of the form .<name>.<pid>.<n>.claim and compares, and answers `Found`; the next read, write or delete ends a move that was killed after its claim: a claimed file that holds the bytes of the Keychain item is removed, and one beside no item or other bytes is put back under the store name; when a step fails, it keeps the file and answers the new member `Locked::Move` with that step's code, the one member after which the Keychain may hold the item that the add made; a removal that faults, or a claimed file that cannot be named back, answers `Fault`; an add that loses the race for the item reads again once, and a removal that finds the file gone answers what the Keychain holds; with both places full of other bytes every read and delete answers `Locked::OtherStore`, which no operation leaves, and with equal bytes a read finishes the move. The hosted macOS tests show the move, a failed add, a read back that differs, both places full of two credentials of 64 bytes that differ in one byte, and a delete beside a file that cannot be read against the runner's Keychain, through `Store::with_move_fault`, which only builds with debug assertions and the features hosted-store-tests and keychain have; a build without debug assertions stops at a compile_error! when the feature hosted-store-tests is set. The check scripts/r6/ci.d/55-r6-formal.sh of the routing run (U2.1, contracts.md T7.6 and T13.1, rulings SG-33 and SG-35), run in the macos job alone: it fetches tla2tools.jar over https from the address that the script holds and compares its size and SHA-256 with the pin that both the script and formal/r6-spend/README.md hold, runs TLC on formal/r6-spend/Spend.cfg with its eleven invariants, replays the committed traces through the machine of one hold, replays traces of its own of which two must be accepted and 26 refused, and requires each of the two control configurations to break its one invariant; its self-test runs the two controls and a failing fixture for each guard, and formal/r6-spend/README.md, section "The check 55-r6-formal.sh", says what the check holds and what it does not see. The command `route-gate probe --rounds N`, which makes N picks, admissions and spend steps on the release data, with a test that its allocator calls are the same for 1 round and for the 100,000 rounds of the check; the check `scripts/r6/ci.d/70-r6-memory.sh`, which runs the binary that cargo reports five times under measure-peak and holds the bound at or under 16,777,216 bytes in both CI jobs; and `docs/r6/bounds.md` with the peaks and bounds of a development Mac and both hosted runners (routing run, U4.1). The pre-push hook refuses a push whose commits add a line that holds a home path of macOS, Linux or Windows, in any file, unless scripts/home-path-allow.txt names the file and that path with a reason: a made-up path, the public account of a hosting service's runner, or a line that holds no home path and reads as one (capture run, hook-1; the billing run's ruling P7-49 (4)). scripts/check-home-paths.sh holds the predicate with its grammar under --help and its self-test, and the ci.d script 19-home-paths runs both in CI. The integration test formal_traces of the router halvo-route replays every move of the spend model's committed traces (formal/r6-spend/traces.jsonl, 206 traces) through `spend::step` from `Hold::new()` and requires the model's answer, next state, `spent`, `held` and `cost` for each; halvo-route gains its dev-dependency serde_json 1.0.154 (MIT or Apache-2.0) for it, and the new hash of its manifest stands in `PINNED`. The test the_manifest_holds_the_package_and_the_dev_dependencies_alone of tests/no_grant_in_release.rs requires that the manifest holds the tables `[package]` and `[dev-dependencies]` alone (T0.7). A slip of `spend::step` that no committed trace reaches still passes the test: the traces are a draw from the domain of the model, not the whole domain. The fixture server of the workflow run (U2.1): `tools/fixture-server/server.mjs` serves the page face and the API face of each web service on 127.0.0.1, answers every other request with 404 and an empty body (a second request sent in the same write after an upgrade offer gets no answer), sends no interim 100, closes every connection and exits 0 on SIGINT or SIGTERM, and ends when a piped standard input ends; `check-sites.mjs` checks site data files and counts the services per category; `sites/` holds 46 development services over the ten seed categories and the category other; and the check 31-fixture-server, in both CI jobs, runs the tests that `tools/fixture-server/tests.txt` lists at HEAD and the checker over the committed site files. The content floor of halvo-capture (capture run, core-2): `floor::payload` records key classes, clicks by button and scroll, drag and move folds, titles and screen activity with no character, key code, coordinate or pixel; a keystroke value needs the `KeystrokeClearance` that `secure_input::check` gives only for an inactive reading of a key not older than 250 ms, and every floor value needs the keep verdict of `FloorHandle::decide` for its kind (the five switches, the static list, the process tree, a private window, secure input); `SinkHandle::emit_event` and `SinkHandle::emit_continuous` emit floor values beside the old emit; `floor::record::encode` writes the record of a received floor value and hands every captured string to the `TextFilterPoint` that it is given; `floor::record::decode`, which takes a line only when the encoder's own writing of the decoded value gives the same bytes, refuses what the floor refuses (a line above 12,771 bytes, a text above its cap, an empty app, a site that the cut could not keep, an instant finer than its kind's resolution) and returns errors that carry no text and no number of the line; the site's domain cut with the crate psl; one shared list of input transitions in `floor::cases`; the run-mark seam under the feature r2; the check scripts/r1/ci.d/30-core-boundary-proofs.sh with 20 proof lines and their twins, whose name searches read the tracked files of HEAD through git and which runs the token tests of crates/halvo-capture/tests/source_rules.rs (proc-macro2 as a development dependency): at most one `TextFilterPoint` implementor, under src/daemon/, no rename with `as`, no implementor that a macro writes, and `#[path]` and the include macros only at five listed uses; compile-time lines that fail when `KeystrokeClearance` or `Keep` gains `Clone`, `Default` or `DeserializeOwned`, `FoldCount` gains `Default` or `DeserializeOwned`, or `FloorEvent` gains `Default`; seven rows of crates/halvo-capture/src/mutants.rows; the ledger line docs/baseline/ledger.d/core-2.txt. halvo-account (billing run, unit 7.3), the account helper and the app's one path to the backend: halvo-account [--print-url] status | subscribe AMOUNT PERIOD | set-cap AMOUNT PERIOD | cap | manage | delete, a closed grammar in which --print-url belongs to a debug build alone; one Request of halvo-wire for each run, sent with reqwest 0.13.5, rustls and ring over TLS 1.3 at the least with the system's trust store, no certificate file, no proxy and no redirect, 10 s to connect and 30 s for the whole call; answers read through a raw JSON check against the vendored schema with integers written as digits alone; one sign-in in halvo-secrets that subscribe makes only when the store answers that none is stored and that delete removes only after the backend answered deleted or no-such-account and a second read of the store finds the sign-in that it sent, keeping another sign-in (store-changed), an item of another form (store-unreadable) and whatever stands in a store that answers locked or fails; and a debug build that reads HALVO_ACCOUNT_BACKEND and HALVO_ACCOUNT_STORE_DIR alone and keeps no store without the second. A release build does not compile until a unit gives it the store directory of unit 8.2. The gate host of the agent-setup run (U3.2), crates/halvo-agent-gate on the memory store: agent-gate configs makes one approved workflow per seed category, sends create_agent through the store's handler as the interface run's coordinator does and prints one line per category with whether a configuration is stored, the workflow's state, whether its agent is set and the answer of validate, then configs seeds=10 valid=<n> store=memory, where a line counts as valid only with stored=yes, state=approved, agent=set and result=valid; the gate binds a seed's slots with one set_bindings and each origin with add_origin under the way sign_in, as the product's parties send them, takes a seed origin only in the form http://<lower-case letters>.agent.test, writes the slot reply_senders as one text joined by a comma and a space (CP-112), and stops at the first body that the handler refuses. The check 20-r3-policy builds the crates of its second list (halvo-policy, halvo-agent-store and halvo-agent-gate) with all their test targets and the target r3_config of halvo-schema, refuses a second build call that compiles anything, and starts every binary itself from cargo's messages: agent-config presets, agent-gate configs, each of the 19 tests of its first list (policy_table, own_kind_roles, the refusal tests of A5.4 in halvo-policy, halvo-agent-store and halvo-schema, the store's create_agent_stores_the_preset_and_makes_the_state_call and the gate's test every_runner_body_of_an_ended_run_is_refused) in its own target's binary by its name alone, and every test binary of those crates whole, each of the 26 test targets of its second list with exactly one binary, in both jobs; it fails when a tracked file differs from HEAD before or after a cargo command or a run, and does not hold against code of the job that writes a test binary or a tool on the PATH before the check reads them, a test that keeps its name with another body, a required target with its test harness switched off, code that runs before main in a required test file, or a compiler wrapper or a cargo configuration that changes what is built (ruling S-53), nor against a test that stands in no list and is removed from its target, which no list holds without naming every test.
- Fixed: The fixture server of the agent-setup run (tools/fixture-server/variants) exits when its standard input is an anonymous pipe or a stream socket and that input ends or fails (a named FIFO from mkfifo is not read to an end on macOS, so a caller gives an anonymous pipe), so a test that holds the write end ends the server by ending itself, also under a hard kill; the fixture's self-test gives every server it starts such a pipe, has cases for a closed input with a request in flight, an anonymous pipe behind a shell, an input that a reset ends, a hard-killed parent, a null input and its process group or job, and stopServer after a signal, fails when its count of cases differs from the one it names for the system, and no longer sends SIGTERM to its own main server 15 s after the start (U0.1b). In halvo-capture (capture run, core-2), a lost update of the sink's accepted count, a counter written by a load and a store in place of one `fetch_add`, is caught in every run by a test of sink.rs's own text, which also keeps the blocking arm whole and `try_send` at its two places and refuses a timed wait on the emit path and in its helper functions, and the four-emitter test has two keystroke emitters; the doc of `CaptureSource` says that two boxed sources of one zero-sized type count as one source, the case that the code holds.
- Changed: In halvo-secrets (billing run, 7.7), `Store::new` of every macOS build searches the login keychain, also without the feature keychain: a build without it counts the Keychain items of its service and name by their attributes alone, and a read answers the new member `Locked::OtherStore` when one stands, whatever the file holds, so that an unsigned build run after a Developer ID release makes no second account; when the Keychain cannot be searched, a read that finds no file answers the Keychain's `Locked` or `Fault`; in both builds a read that finds neither place full looks at the Keychain once more before it answers `NotStored`. A write and a delete of both builds first read, the move included: a write stores only where that read answers `NotStored`, answers `Occupied` on `Found` and the read's `Locked` or `Fault` otherwise; a delete removes only the item that the read gives and then looks at the other place once more, so it answers `Deleted` or `NotStored` only when that place is empty, `Fault` with code -1 when its removal took effect and the other place holds an item, and `Locked::OtherStore` when it removed nothing and the other place holds one. `Store::in_directory` keeps the file alone, and security-framework is a dependency of every macOS build. In halvo-capture (capture run, core-2), `CaptureError::PermissionDenied` prints its kind in snake_case (`screen_frame`), `secure_input_macos.rs` is the child module `secure_input::macos` and keeps its old path by a re-export, the source context carries a floor handle, the sink's channel carries floor values, which the old receive methods see as an event of their kind, time and id with no app and no payload, `StartGuard` answers a second begin with `AlreadyStarted` for every source, and the capture manifest names the features `Win32_Graphics_Gdi` and `Win32_System_Com`, which it used through windows-capture. The account check 10-r7-account (billing run, unit 7.3) runs cargo deny over the four targets that Halvo is built for, as account/deny.toml now names them: the licence check reads the four targets that Halvo is built for, and the lock names one package of another licence that no build compiles, webpki-root-certs 1.0.11 under CDLA-Permissive-2.0, which the check names on a line of its own and compares with a fixed list, so that a second such package fails it; the check also fails when a tracked file differs from HEAD after cargo ran. Two lists of the script name every case of its self-test, those of unit 7.1b among them: the macos job runs both, and the windows job runs only the first, which holds the cases that end before the first cargo fetch, the cases whose subject differs between the two systems, the controls and the harness cases (rulings P7-55 and P7-71); each job's self-test fails when a case of its list did not run, a case stands on no list, the count of cases left to the macos job is wrong or the job that it read is not the driver's, a verdict that one function gives from the values that the run holds and that harness cases check with values of their own; and the reader of the dep-info files refuses a name that holds a tab or another control character, which it cannot split without doubt: the windows job no longer shows that the script refuses the left-out cases under the shell tools of the windows runner; a tree that such a case stands for is refused by the macos job of the same commit. Both CI jobs end at 90 minutes instead of 60 (capture run, jobtime-4, ruling JB-6), so that a windows job of main that saves its build cache ends inside the limit, which still ends a job that hangs; the bound `ci_job_timeout_min` of docs/baseline/r1-gate-bounds.txt, the pin of the gate's cost check and ADR 0011 read 90.
- Changed: The model of the confirm policy (agent-setup run, U1.3, contracts.md A5.3 and A5.6, ruling CP-35): the first half of P10 holds that a request leaves only while its effect's row is `releasing` and while the run goes on, so a paused request that leaves after the run's end and before `lose` breaks P10, and formal/r3-confirm/README.md says that a request of the person's handback is no effect's and is not on the model's wire. The scan of 40-r6-policy (routing run, U1.2, by the ruling SG-36) takes a hit on scripts/r6/ci.d/80-r6-absent.sh off only while HEAD holds that path as a regular file reached through trees alone whose git blob id is the one that 40-r6-policy.sh names and the work-tree file is that blob, because T13.5 makes 80-r6-absent.sh set --cfg test for its pair; every other hit stays, and a change of 80-r6-absent.sh changes the blob id in the same commit. The changelog row of AGENTS.md asks one line for each type of change that each unit in a pull request makes, so that a joint pull request keeps each unit's own lines and joins no texts (capture run, changelog-1, ruling JB-1 (b)), and says that GitHub's own merge does not apply the union attribute of `CHANGELOG.md`: on 2026-11-12, after pull request #44 merged, GitHub reported three open pull requests as conflicting in that file alone.
- Added: The test `formal_traces` of halvo-policy (agent-setup run, U1.3, contracts.md A5.5 and A5.6) follows every committed trace of the confirm model with `effect_step`, which must accept and refuse each move as the model does and reach the same next state, under 134 sets of values for the digests and effect ids and with derived moves for other classes and counts; the check 30-r3-formal (A5.6, A14.1) refuses a tree whose formal/r3-confirm or script differs from the commit, holds the five configuration files of the model with the seed and the count of the export, runs TLC with P1 to P11 under Confirm.cfg and Rebinds.cfg on every push in both jobs and requires their counts of distinct states, exports the policy table and the traces again and compares them byte for byte with the committed files, runs twelve controls of which each must report its own invariant, and fetches TLC within 60 s; formal/r3-confirm/README.md names what it does not see. The crate halvo-runner (agent-setup run, U4.1, contracts.md A6.1, A6.2, A6.5, A8.6, A11.4): the runner process of one agent run, which reads its supervisor's frames apart from its steps, replays the built-in fill playbook and stores each effect of the workbook edit before and after its replace, ends a run at its limits with the outcomes of A6.5, acts on the three answers of secret_reply, and ends by itself within LINK_END when its link closes. The check scripts/r6/ci.d/80-r6-absent.sh (routing run, U4.3) counts in both CI jobs what would let a model request leave the machine while the owner has not ruled, and prints `absent ruling=<none|other> hosted_picks=<n> hosted_rows=<n> net_crates=<n> entries=<n> grants=<n> test_value=<n>`: it reads from the commit, at their paths as regular files reached through trees alone and with no link, no twin in another letter case and no name outside ASCII at those places, the ruling file crates/halvo-route/data/ruling.txt, which must be the one line ruling=none, the picks of crates/halvo-route/data/policy.tsv and the rows of docs/r6/outcomes/recorded.tsv that name a hosted tier, and the entries of the lists under scripts/r6/egress; it counts the lines of cargo tree for the normal dependencies of halvo-route, halvo-route-gate and halvo-route-wire, on every target and with every feature, that name one of a closed list of 11 packages; it requires the ok lines of no_grant_in_release and pinned_files_hold_their_hashes under the target tests/no_grant_in_release.rs from cargo test of halvo-route in the debug and the release profile; and it builds in its own temporary directory, with --cfg test added to the job's flags, a program that names Ruling::test_hosted, which must stop with E0624 at its own line, and a control that must compile and answer ruling. A git command that fails and a cargo command that leaves the tree changed end the check. It does not hold code that runs in the job before it and writes what it reads (SG-30 (a)), a network package outside the list such as tungstenite, or code that uses std::net, a build or a development dependency, a forged ok line in the release profile with the pin test switched off, which 30-r6-table refuses in the debug profile alone, a data file that release.rs reads from another path, a tracked cargo configuration, which 40-r6-policy refuses, or the text of the probe and the control, which the blob id below and the real path hold because the self-test compiles nothing; a job that turns warnings into errors fails it. (interface run, U0.3) The floor trial in tools/r4-trials/floor/ with its result docs/r4/trials/floor.md, and in docs/r4/page-features.txt an allow line for each web API that the two views call and for 12 of the 47 CSS properties that their style strings set, and 80 deny lines, each with a pattern that finds a use of a feature that reached Safari after 17.0, the floor of macOS 14; list.mjs checks the list, its cases and a view file, and features.mjs checks the lines against the compatibility data. docs/r2/category-parameters.md has a row with the source `open` for each of the ten parameter names that the development services of the fixture server use, one under each seed category after its four capture-layer rows (contracts.md C8.5).
- Fixed: formal/r3-confirm/export.sh (agent-setup run, U1.3) makes its temporary directory under TMPDIR and gives TLC paths inside that directory; on macOS its bare `mktemp -d` ignored TMPDIR and left one directory in the user's temporary directory at every run.
- Fixed: Every public parse of a reply among the agent-setup types of halvo-schema counts the bytes as they arrive, white space outside the value and escapes included (agent-setup run, U1.6, ruling CP-116 (b)): `CheckpointsReply::parse`, `RunnerReply::parse` and the parse of each other reply of a runner or coordinator body refuse more than 261,067 bytes with the rule `reply_bytes` before they read, the parse of each reply frame to the runner refuses more than 65,536 bytes with `frame_length`, and the serde reader of `RunnerReply` counts the text of the value that it is given, as that of `CoordinatorReply` does; serde gives a reader no byte outside the value; a type that `RunnerReply`, `CoordinatorReply` or `ToRunner` names without its bound does not compile.
- Security: The supervisor library starts the runner with its standard error on the null device (contracts.md A6.1, A6.6, ruling CP-121), so nothing that the runner or a process that inherits its standard error writes there reaches a stream of the app, its logs or a file; tests of halvo-agent-client hold it: the runner's standard error is the null device itself, a marker printed in four forms, one of them by a child, reaches no stream of the host, more than 2 MiB written while a confirmation waits do not block the runner, and on macOS a write after the hard kill of the library reaches no standard error of the host.
- Changed: In halvo-sheet (agent-setup run, U2.3b), `prepare` refuses with the cause `workbook`, before any write, a workbook with a relationship marked External whose type the executor or its reader reads as a part (a worksheet, a chart, dialog or macro sheet, a table, a macro project, a pivot part, the office document; the type compared without ASCII case), where it skipped every External relationship and let calamine follow one to an internal path; relationships of other types marked External, hyperlinks among them, stay skipped (the review's D-12, CP-95 (4)). `prepare` also refuses a workbook whose name holds `.halvo-checkpoint-` or `.halvo-prepared-` in any letter case and walks the workbook's path again under the lock. `Folder::new` takes the `Locks` as its second argument, `RunId` and `Sha256` come from halvo-checkpoint, `RunId::parse` answers an `Option`, and `Error::RunId` is gone. halvo-runner (agent-setup run, U2.3b, the join with U4.1 under CP-134) takes the optional argument `--data-dir`, read as the daemon's flag is read, and opens the locks of its file steps under it, or without it under the app's default data directory; its three calls of halvo-sheet take `Folder::new` with the locks and `RunId::parse` as an `Option`, and a file step that finds no data directory answers `step_failed`.
- Added: The crate halvo-checkpoint (agent-setup run, U2.3b; contracts.md A11.3, CP-98 (2)) with no reader of workbooks: the run id, the checkpoint's name `<name>.halvo-checkpoint-<run id><ext>` built in one place, the walk of a path inside a folder that halvo-sheet now uses, the SHA-256 and the bound of a file; `same_file`, the one compare of two files for identity in this crate and halvo-sheet, by the identity that the system gives for an open handle (the device and inode on macOS, the volume's serial number and the 128-bit file id on Windows), with the length and times compared too on a volume whose file id is not stable; `Locks`, a lock file per workbook directory in `agents/locks/` under the data directory that the caller gives, named by the SHA-256 of the directory's identity, which `prepare` of halvo-sheet holds while it writes and `replace` from its compare to its rename, so that two runs that give one data directory lose no edit of one workbook and a `replace` that cannot take it within the wait is refused, and the marks there, one empty `<64 hex>.live` file made before each prepared copy, that keep a live call's copy from the sweep; and `remove`, the one call that removes a named checkpoint, inside the row's folder alone and under that lock, walking the way again under it part by part without following a link and acting only in the directory that it opened, only when no directory on the way is a link, the copy is a regular file whose own entry name is the built name byte for byte, and its length and SHA-256 are the row's (or, for a planned row, the workbook's), moving the copy aside and removing it only when it is still the checked file, an absent copy counting as removed. `prepare` gives the checkpoint's SHA-256, length and name for the list's row (A7.4), carries the workbook's extended attributes and access list (macOS) or its named streams and access list (Windows) to the checkpoint and the prepared copy through the handles that read the workbook and made each file, sweeps the prepared copies of the workbook that a killed run left, each named with the digest of its bytes and with a mark that no live call holds, and leaves and counts one with no mark; and refuses before any write a workbook whose name would make a built name of more than 255 units of the system.
- Changed: The reader of coord_event refuses a run_started row whose trigger is the scheduler and whose slot is null, with the rule no_slot at slot, and the writer refuses to write one.
- Added: The cells of the R4 window and state-sync trials (interface run, U0.2): `tools/r4-trials/window/run.sh` runs a window arm or a sync way on a hosted runner, checks each answer of the trial app against the readers of the system, prints its cell and figure lines and refuses where neither CI nor R4_GUEST is set; `sample.sh` and `sample.ps1` read the figures of the app's process tree, `read.sh` prints the reading lines, and `rule.mjs` gives the figures and verdicts and makes `docs/r4/trials/window.md` and `docs/r4/trials/sync.md` from the raw output. The trial page reports where a sync way stands after 150 ticks of its interval without a change, which is 15 s while it is shown, and reports its first change that steps back in order; the self-check's window run reads the system through readers that it is handed. Each figure line of the R4 window trial (interface run, U0.2b) prints beside its medians the largest `key_bytes` and `show_ms` of the counted runs and how many of them show above the line of 1,000 ms (`max_bytes`, `median_show_ms`, `max_show_ms`, `show_over_1000`); a figure line of the earlier five-key form passes the check, and is kept by `make`, only for the cells of run 43325502435, whose jobs printed it, and `make` writes the new form for any other cells.
- Changed: The R4 trial page's show line (interface run, U0.2) ends in `hide_seen=yes|no`, which says whether the page saw the hide before the show, and the trial app's self-check `launch` runs a binary path that ends in `.mjs` under Node. `tools/r4-trials/window/rule.mjs` (interface run, U0.2b) refuses a window file that does not hold exactly 10 cells for each machine and arm and a sync file without one cell for each machine, way and arm, and `make` refuses a job log with a trial's line that is a `nocell` or `miss` line or a `cell` line that it does not read exactly, a trial's line being one whose first word, after the time stamp and leading white space, is `cell`, `nocell` or `miss` in any letter case, or that names `nocell` or `miss` before `trial=`; it passes over every other line.
- Added: Add the roster, the status function with the case waiting_turn.store and its words entry, the snapshot and its changes, the card's read of Owner::agent_history with each run's committed effects, the type Coordinator with the person's stop of every kind of item and the app's quit in two steps, and the check 20-r4-core.sh to the crate halvo-coord (U2.4).
- Changed: In halvo-coord, the run drive answers state to the stop of a waiting run whose item has not lapsed, the lapse of an item of every kind with the new cause cancelled stops its run in the guard's table first, the run drive gains a stop-all and a kill-all for the quit, a request that ends its run (kind, budget) stops it with that cause, the switch-off sends no second cancel to a run whose item's lapse sent one, the first creation move of a new life raises setup again for each agent of the list whose hold setup stands and that holds no open item, an item that a live run waits on lapses wait_limit_s after its raise and stops its run, a stop marks the guard's table and the board before Runs::cancel, and after the quit's first step a tick kills each stopping run at its grace with nothing stored and sends what the board owes (U2.4).
- Fixed: In halvo-coord, a lost acknowledgement of a ready item, of a run_started, of a hold or of the answer to a check no longer makes the owner refuse every later log of that agent with seq: the schedule's own store reads the owner's events again and logs once more; an item whose answer was stored with a lost acknowledgement gets no second closing row and a second answer takes the stored one, but for the declining choice on a link_account item whose stored answer is done, which stores a row of its own and hands the run declined, a run_started whose acknowledgement was lost is closed with interrupted in the life that finds it instead of letting a second run start, and a debt logs through the base's logger so that its read after seq pays it and an owed run_ended is logged once whatever end of its run the read shows, in either order in which the coordinator learns of a lost acknowledgement; and the second step of a sign-in sends allow to its run only inside the person's press of Allow, once the owner acknowledged that press's row: a press whose row got no acknowledgement decides nothing, a stored allow that the run did not take takes Don't allow with a decline row of its own, a stored decline refuses Allow, and such a step lapses at the wait limit and at the person's Stop with decline to the run first, as an open step does; and a start whose log meets seq decides its steps again from the events that the read brought, so that a switch-off whose acknowledgement was lost holds every later start, by the person, by a ready item, by a tick or from the queue, with hold, nothing stored of the start, no runner and set_state paused sent once (U2.4).
- Added: The crate halvo-derived (workflow run, U5.1): the derived store as a library, one SQLCipher database under derived/ of a data directory, opened under a writer lock in exclusive locking mode with secure delete, a page-cache cap and a progress handler, that keeps each record of a registered kind in one envelope whose kind fixes its retention class; commit_batch with the hashed title token, purge and purge_below with a high-water mark, the recurrence step and the steps and handlers that a host supplies, the typed commit with its origin rule, approve, dismiss and delete with the delete cascade, the state call, expiry by class at every open, the report after each committed transaction, the interface read inside the reply bound and test_host::export_proposals; and the check scripts/r2/ci.d/50-derived.sh in the windows job, which runs the halvo-derived test binaries of the workspace's test build, which 12-test has built before it in the hosted job, against its list and reads cargo tree for a bundled feature and openssl-sys.
- Fixed: In halvo-capture (capture run, win-2), the Windows sources are sound to start and stop: a source that cannot start returns the step that failed with the system's code as an HRESULT, for the hooks of the foreground and window-title sources (`a_foreground_hook_that_the_system_refuses_reports_the_os_code`, `a_title_hook_that_the_system_refuses_reports_the_os_code`) and for the four start steps of the raw-input source, GetModuleHandleW, RegisterClassW, CreateWindowExW and RegisterRawInputDevices (`a_raw_input_source_whose_module_handle_is_refused_reports_the_step_and_code`, `a_raw_input_source_whose_class_is_refused_reports_the_step_and_code`, `a_raw_input_source_whose_window_is_refused_reports_the_step_and_code`, `a_raw_input_source_whose_registration_is_refused_reports_the_step_and_code`), an input source's `shutdown` answers `Timeout` after its deadline of 1 s when its thread does not end (`a_stop_that_timed_out_keeps_the_pump_known_until_it_ends`), and with a full sink a keyboard source's `shutdown` answers Ok inside 2 s (`stop_with_a_full_sink`), and a stop that passes its deadline keeps the thread that still runs, reads as failed and refuses a new `begin`, while dropping a source waits its deadline at most once and not at all after a shutdown that timed out, a dropped source stops capturing (`a_dropped_keyboard_source_stops_capturing`), the screen source also when its stop thread cannot be started, whose session the dropping thread then asks to end (`a_drop_whose_stop_thread_is_always_refused_still_ends_the_session`, `a_dropped_screen_source_whose_stop_thread_is_refused_ends_its_capture`), except a source whose `shutdown` answered neither Ok nor ThreadEnded, which is dropped without a wait and whose registration or hooks the end of the process removes, a second `begin` of the foreground or the keyboard source returns `AlreadyStarted` (`a_second_begin_is_refused_and_health_follows_the_lifecycle`, `a_keyboard_source_refuses_a_second_begin_and_starts_again_after_its_stop`), no window procedure or WinEvent callback waits for the sink, an event carries the time of its OS event, the screen source asks for no minimum update interval when its probe answers that the build has none (`the_settings_choice_passes_the_default_interval_when_the_probe_answers_no`), a closed capture session reads as failed health, the packets and the pending motion sample that a source's hand-off still holds when `shutdown` closes it are counted lost and do not reach the sink, apart from the one packet that the forwarder has already taken (`packets_that_a_stopped_run_held_do_not_arrive_after_a_barrier_drawn_after_the_stop`, `a_frame_pushed_after_a_screen_shutdown_is_counted_lost_and_stays_out_of_the_sink`, `a_timed_out_stop_has_closed_the_hand_off_for_what_the_stuck_pump_pushes_later`), a pump reads up to 4,096 messages that were queued for it at its stop, at its quit message or at its stop event, so that each input event among them is delivered or counted lost, and a message past that bound is neither (`keys_queued_before_a_stop_are_delivered_or_counted`, `the_read_at_a_stop_takes_what_is_queued_and_ends_at_its_bound`, `a_pump_that_ends_at_its_stop_event_reads_what_is_queued`), a screen stop whose thread cannot be started keeps its session and reads as failed until a later stop starts, the drop of a pump handle whose thread was joined posts no quit message, and the drop after a stop that timed out posts once while it still holds the thread's handle (`the_drop_after_a_timeout_posts_while_it_holds_the_thread_handle`).
- Added: In halvo-capture (capture run, win-2), each Windows source hands its packets to a bounded queue of its own whose forwarder thread does the emit and counts what the queue lost (`handoff_counts`), pointer motion collapses into one pending sample sent at most every 100 ms, the screen source takes a process-lifetime MTA reference before its first session (`the_process_mta_reference_is_taken_once_and_holds_an_mta_for_a_new_thread`), the stop deadlines `INPUT_STOP_DEADLINE` and `SCREEN_STOP_DEADLINE`, the bound `STOP_READ_LIMIT` of 4,096 messages that a pump reads at its stop, 26 mutant rows in windows/mutants.rows, and the functional tests `capture_times_follow_the_input_order`, `keys_queued_before_a_stop_are_delivered_or_counted` and, once the clearing rule of RR-windows-04 held (in run 43318100513 on scratch/r1-win-2-1 at ebd8189a, the tree of 04ac8bd1, the control faulted in 17 of 30 launches and the repaired product path in none), `x5_screen_source_stop_and_start_again`, `x12_screen_source_fifteen_start_stop_cycles` and `c8b_window_destroyed_after_the_last_capture_session`.
- Removed: In halvo-capture (capture run, win-2), `windows::screen::exclude_window_from_capture`, which no process could use on its own window, and the `start`, `stop` and `modality` bodies of the Windows sources, which `begin`, `shutdown` and `health` replace.
- Changed: The check 50-windows-functional (capture run, win-2) runs the three tests of known defects 10 to 12 as regular tests, so that its log reads `known_defect=8`, and its self-test runs its fixtures four at a time with a judge that reads each log with shell builtins, and rejects the same 27 failing fixtures.
- Added: Tests of the view contract on both sides (capture run, app-1): one JSON fixture under ui/tests/fixtures/capture/ for each command reply and state, a test that builds the app on Tauri's mock runtime from the real context and compares the reply of each command to the page's argument objects with its fixture, node page tests that feed the same fixtures to the page in a stub DOM with no dependency, page tests that fail when a file under ui/app writes HTML through innerHTML, outerHTML or insertAdjacentHTML, uses web storage, IndexedDB or cookies, or runs text as code through a call of eval, of new Function, or of setTimeout or setInterval whose first argument is a quoted string (the policy's script-src, which holds no unsafe-eval, stops the other routes to text as code in the engine), the check scripts/r1/ci.d/30-app-page-test.sh, which fails when a page test fails or none runs, and the launch flag `halvo --check-page --config-dir DIR --within-s N`, which loads the page in a hidden window with no tray and no capture, prints `page=loaded commands_ok=<n> version=<v>` once the page has the reply of every read command and the version, and exits 1 after N seconds, run on both jobs by scripts/r1/ci.d/32-app-check-page.sh with a bound of 60 s, which prints the first line that it read from the app, with each byte outside letters, digits, space and `=._-` shown as `?`, beside its verdict. The page moves to ui/app/, so the tests under ui/tests/ do not ship, and it reports an error when the inventory reply holds no list of rows.
- Changed: The page and the commands of the app (capture run, app-1) are one file per view and per command module: ui/app/index.html holds markup only, ui/app/style.css the styles, ui/app/ipc.js the one call into the core (`Halvo.ipc.call(name, args)`), ui/app/frame.js the view list (`Halvo.views.add({id, order, heading, mount, show, hide})`, rendered as sections in ascending order, each under a level-2 heading), and ui/app/views/ one file per view; src-tauri/src/commands/ holds the capture commands as one module, its names in commands/names.rs, and one module table from which the invoke handler is built. The seven recorded states of the page are the same on the split page.
- Security: The app's web view (capture run, app-1) has a content security policy with no remote origin and no unsafe keyword, the global Tauri object is off, the window is incognito and a navigation rule refuses every address outside the app's own origin that the runtime puts to it, and it decides from the address's string, so a string that the URL reader refuses is refused (an address that the runtime's own reader refuses is allowed by tauri-runtime-wry 2.12.3 without the rule, is reached only by script in the page, and stays open until a unit closes it at the engine); build.rs declares an app manifest from the command names of src-tauri/src/commands/names.rs, and src-tauri/capabilities/capture.json lets the window main call the capture commands and the core app version command and nothing else. The app's invoke handler refuses a capture command with one text, whatever a capability grants, to a window other than main, and to a call whose web view's address, or whose Origin header when that header is an address, is not of the app's origin (scheme, host, no port, no user name). Tests show each hold: a capability for the window helper or for a made-up label, added at run time or added to the authority of the context before the app is built, reaches none of the eight capture commands; a call on the app's host from another port or with a user name is refused for each of them, and from another port also with no Origin header; and a test that reads the app's sources finds no call that adds a capability at run time or hands one to the context macro. The core app version command stays under the framework's capability check alone, which compares scheme and host and not the port. The page reads the version through that command instead of a script that the app injected, and the permission files that tauri-build generates under src-tauri/permissions/autogenerated/ are committed with LF line ends on every checkout (src-tauri/permissions/.gitattributes), so a build leaves the tree clean also in a Windows clone with core.autocrlf=true.
- Fixed: The app (capture run, app-1) reads its arguments with `std::env::args_os()` and parses them once into a launch type, so an argument that is not valid Unicode, or one that it does not know, prints the usage and exits 64 instead of panicking. A double-click on Delete everything no longer deletes at once: the button stays in place and disabled while armed, the question, which says that copies Halvo made next to the person's own files stay, and the confirmation sit on a second row, the confirmation ignores a click within 500 ms of arming and any click with a click count above 1, and the control disarms after 10 s and when the window hides; a pointer click on the confirmation sends nothing unless a press began on it after arming, or when it lands inside the box that Delete everything had at arming, which the view keeps, and a reply of capture_status, onboarding_state or inventory that arrives while it is armed is drawn by no view until it disarms, when the newest reply of each is drawn in the order of the views (the cap view's question about a lower cap, which sits above it, is not held and still draws when the person presses Save); scripts/r1/ci.d/31-app-page-layout.sh checks in WebKit that a second click at the point of Delete everything sends no delete_all. A double-click on Pause no longer ends with capture running: the toggle sends the action that its label names, ignores a click with a click count above 1 and is held for 1 s after the capture state changes. The storage cap field keeps an edit across status polls until Save, checks the value in GB next to the field (empty, below 1 GB, above 1024 GB) and sends nothing then, asks before a lower cap that deletes capture, with the amount that it deletes, sends no cap while the stored size is not known, and closes that question when the field is edited; a confirmation of Delete everything 10 s or more after arming sends nothing, also when the disarm timer runs late.
- Fixed: The allocation tests of `route-gate probe` (halvo-route-gate, routing run U4.1f) no longer count the test harness's first thread, which makes a few calls of the allocator when it first blocks to wait for the test's thread (up to 3 calls and 208 bytes in the runs on a Mac) and failed `probe::tests::probe_allocates_nothing_per_round` when they fell inside a window (2 of 2,000 runs on a Mac); they still count calls and bytes of every other thread, fail when the test runs on that first thread, and a failed child now prints its own panic text with the values that it saw.
- Added: In halvo-coord, the Clock port gives the local day of an instant (Clock::day), today is the day of one read of now, and the type Stamp holds an instant with its day from one read of the clock; the memory clock keeps a zone, gives the day of any instant in it, and can move its instant alone or on each read (contracts.md I7.1, the agent-setup run's CP-122).
- Fixed: In halvo-coord, every row that the coordinator writes takes its day and its at from one read of the clock, the day being the local day of that instant, so that a row whose two reads fell on both sides of local midnight is no longer refused and no longer keeps every later row of its agent waiting (I7.1, CP-122): the items drive (store, the answers of a confirmation, a handback and a second step, the setup's row), the schedule's drive, whose move reads the clock once and stamps each of its rows and the coordinator's day with that read, so that no row of a move carries the day of the last tick, the raise of the item check and the body of recovery; a read of the clock after the last instant whose nanoseconds an at holds is the clock's failure and stamps no row. The coordinator's flush cuts the waiting rows into bodies by request_bytes as well as by request_records, measured on the body's text (log_text, which halvo-schema's CoordLog reads and writes byte for byte), a row that alone passes request_bytes going as a body of its own; no writer of this head makes rows with an origin wait, since the answer rows that carry one go through store_answer and store_once, so the cut guards the bound for later writers; and the memory owner refuses a body over request_bytes with malformed (I7.2; RD-37 (3); RD-59 CD-3).
- Added: The crate halvo-coord-store with the owner-side handler of coord_log and coord_history, which answers the stored records of a workflow and agent in ascending seq in pages of 64 with the largest stored seq as last, takes a coordinator write alone, stores what the typed writer writes and answers {"stored":n}, and refuses with nothing stored a body that the typed reader refuses, a workflow that is not stored or not the agent's, a record whose at is below 0, a day that is not the local day of its at, and a seq that is not the stored last plus 1, and with the store in memory of the coordinator's records, whose typed commit refuses a record that is no coord_event of a stored workflow, a coord_event that a step returns, and a day after the clock's day or outside its 93 days, whose expiry keeps a row 92 days back and two days ahead of the clock, and whose row read hands out only the rows that an expiry at the read's clock would leave.
- Changed: The dialog trial of the interface run (U0.4b, contracts.md I11.5, rulings RD-49 (h), RD-68 and RD-75): the reading line of each dialog form and system names a file and a line for each of `default`, `cancel` and `close` (`default_file`, `default_line` and so on) in place of one file and line for the three, `read.sh --dialog` prints that form and refuses, naming the file, when one of the 8 lines that it cites or one of the 30 lines, orders of lines and absence that it requires (those that the review and the verifier named) has changed; it reads those texts and does not follow the code, so a change of another line is not seen; `verdict.mjs` refuses the old one, and `docs/r4/trials/dialog.md` holds the new lines with the same verdict line.
- Fixed: In halvo-connect, `CallError::effect()` gives `Effect::NotMade` for an answered status only for 401 and 429 and `Effect::Unknown` for every other status from 400 to 499, as A11.2 of the agent-setup run says, so that a send or a delete answered with such a status is no longer reported as not made (rulings CP-135 (4) and CP-139 (c)); and the fixture server that halvo-connect's tests start gets a piped input whose write end the guard that kills and waits holds, with a preloaded module that ends Node when that input ends, so that the server also ends when the test process is killed hard (unit U2.2b, ruling CP-133 (c)).
- Added: halvo-connect exports `same_address`, which tells without regard to ASCII case whether an address is on the person's list (the account's address or a listed sender) and which `ReplyRule::selects` uses for each of its compares; the runner's own call is not built yet (ruling CP-143 (3) (i)), and `reply_senders`, the reader of the slot `reply_senders`, which cuts its one text at commas, trims ASCII spaces, keeps the order, and refuses the whole slot with `SendersError` for an empty piece, a piece that is no address by the adapter's rule for a sender, or an address twice without regard to ASCII case (unit U2.2b, ruling CP-112).
- Added: Add the second round of the web isolation trial (U0.7b) in tools/r3-trials/web: the relay's list by state with its record of refusals, the agent's start flags and profile settings (the browser's waking of its updater among what they turn off), the policy against WebSockets added by the executor, the reach rows of CP-78, the rows of the ways out under scripts/egress/check.sh, and the round's lines and verdict in docs/r3/trials/web-isolation.md Add the third round of the web isolation trial (U0.7c) in tools/r3-trials/web: the browser started on its debugging pipe under one start lock (posix_spawn with POSIX_SPAWN_CLOEXEC_DEFAULT on macOS, CreateProcessW with a handle list on Windows), the trial's own wire on the pipe with the library's protocol types, the first reply's deadline, the start's probe, the relay's peer check with its record of foreign peers and its bounds, the guard of the relay's port, the checks of a session, the rows pipe_inherit, relay_foreign, relay_port_free, start_probe, org_policy, same_site_pref and same_site_rule, and the third round's lines and verdict in docs/r3/trials/web-isolation.md, the decision whether the text of a browser's policy page names a policy (src/policy.rs, `web-trial --policy-named`), and the control start of an org_ cell (`web-trial --org-control`).
- Changed: A cell line of the web trial names each major version that its runs reported, with its count, and carries relay= when the cell ran through the relay. The web trial's executor drives the browser over its pipe on the trial's own wire and keeps chromiumoxide's connection for the port-form control alone; docs/r3/trials/web-executor.md holds the executor's extras measured again on the pipe; an org_ cell counts only when a start without the setting replied before the policy was written, the job read the value back from the policy, and the browser's own policy page named the setting wherever the page could be read (a page that was read and does not name it holds the cell open as notrun cause=policy_not_seen, and an unread page names its cause on the cell line), and each run line names what the end of the run's own processes found before and after (left_before, left_after); the web trial's relay_port_free row reads each kill as ok, a miss (free_first, tree_left, left_behind) or open (one_pass), from a watch that ends nothing before a cap of 300 s and reads the run before the port, and a call with an open kill and no miss is notrun cause=watch_open; each process that a check counts in port_flag or pipe_holder is classed counts, passing or open by its own state on macOS and named on standard output, beside the counts; the policy page's reader also names a setting whose letters a text reader split and joined, when the value that the job wrote ends them.
- Removed: The web trial's operation that connected to a running browser at a debugging address, the attach cell that used it, and the close of a browser by its address in the driver row (CP-89 (b)).
- Fixed: The control of the web trial's driver row waits until its listener has counted the request that it let through before it kills the driver, so that the browser, which ends with its driver over the pipe, cannot end before the request leaves; every way out of a run of the web trial (the stop after a cell's run, a failed start, a failed cell, INT and TERM, and the end of a kill row's run after its watch) ends, by number and with the creation time read again before the kill, each process that began after the run's start, runs from the browser's own app bundle or install directory and names the run's profile as a whole path, pass after pass until a pass finds none, and leaves every other process alone: the egress check's shell, its runner and its log stream, a process of another program, and a browser that names another profile; a run that leaves such a process is no ok run; each process that a check counts in port_flag or pipe_holder is followed in steps of 5 ms for at most 30 s, classed as one that counts, passes or is open, and named on a line of its own; the policy page's reader names a policy only by its whole name; a process table that could not be read is no longer read as an empty one by the relay's guard, which gave the relay's port up, nor by a check, which then counted nothing; the relay's guard of the web trial ends the run, after a grace and by number and start time, when the executor that started it is gone, because under load a browser whose driver died hard reads its pipe closed and then does not end by itself; the one test that drives that guard with the real process table and the real ask ends a process only through a fence that passes on a call on the test's own stand-in browser, by number and start time, and fails the test at any other call.
- Added: Add halvo-sample with compose, split, build, check-labels, input-copy, leak and mask-layers, the golden script, the development rates and scripts with the manifests of the bodies gen-dev and gen-unseen, which are built at check time below the build directory by scripts/r2/body.sh and are not tracked, and the check 30-sample.
- Changed: `request::decode_reply` refuses, before it reads anything, a reply longer than `REPLY_MAX` (261,120 bytes as given, white space included) with `too_large` and a null id, so that the bound of a reply, envelope included, holds for every caller and not by a count that each caller keeps (workflow run U1.1h; the agent-setup run's ruling CP-153 (5)); scripts/r2/20-schema-tests.txt names its test, so 20-schema fails when that test is renamed, removed, ignored or left unbuilt.
- Added: Five checks of the seam's conformance suite (contracts.md C5.13 item 12), made inside its four cases so that `run` makes them: every store-changed names the current store id, also to an old-id open with a filter; an open_cursor while a cursor is open gets bad-request in five forms and three states and leaves that cursor as it was; an open at the watermark after one shred and after every shred is no error; a filtered read that stops at the byte bound serves every record from its position up to next once, under five filters over two batch files; and a segment keeps one expiry instant within a case. tests/conformance.rs runs 51 wrong servers, proxies over the fixture transport and not copies of it, each of which fails its check by name, and 21-seam reads the five tests by name.
- Added: The router's spend simulation `route-gate spend-sim --seed N [--export FILE]` (routing run, U2.3) runs six cases on fake accounts that move only through `spend::step`, with `admit::fits` on `Room::of` as the admission, and prints one line per case and `spend cases=6 overruns=<n> above_cap=<n> admit_sum=<ok|miss> result=<pass|fail>`; docs/r6/gateway/sequences.jsonl is its export for the seed 1, which the billing run replays, and the test sequences_same compares it with a fresh one; the check 50-r6-spend.sh runs the tests of halvo-route and halvo-route-gate and counts each of the 19 refusal cases and of the tests formal_traces, no_grant_in_release and sequences_same only under its own crate, target and whole test path, when that target's list holds it, its list of ignored tests does not, and a run of the bound tests alone prints its ok line under its target; it prints the seven lines and compares the SHA-256 of the export with that of the file of the commit, which it reads before its first cargo command, and it does not hold code that runs before main in a bound test file or in a target with its harness off, nor the job's environment (a runner for test binaries, a cargo earlier on the PATH); formal_traces requires the six directed traces on lines 2 to 7 and at least 200 drawn traces of twelve moves behind them, and scripts/r6/required-tests.txt names formal_traces and the manifest test of halvo-route. The check 50-r6-spend holds that each bound test is listed, is not ignored, ran and passed under its crate, its target and its whole path, and it does not hold what the body of that test asserts, so a bound test that keeps its name with another body counts.
- Changed: In the router's check 30-r6-table (routing run, U2.3), the self-test takes the number of required tests from scripts/r6/required-tests.txt, and the check holds a floor of the 23 lines that the list holds today: a committed list that lost one of them prints `required dropped=<line>` and ends FAIL with the cause tests, while a list that gains a line passes.
- Added: The crate halvo-learn-store (learning run, U2.1, contracts.md L2.2, L3, L4.1 to L4.4 and L4.6): `LearnHandlers`, which a host puts in front of the agent store's handler for the request types `runner` and `coordinator_write` through the traits `LearnHost` and `Clock`; they pass every request on, store a run's `learn_run` row at `start_run` and the routine's first `learn_routine` row at `create_agent`, serve the learned target in force on a step in the reply of `load_steps` only while the compile number that the run loaded is the agent's newest, hold the admitted proposals of a replay run's `put_playbook` as its candidates and never pass that body on, withdraw the edits of a lower compile number after a compile, and at a run's `finish` make one typed commit with the run's row, the moves of the edits that the run was served and the candidates that the rule keeps, each edit row checked against its source run; they answer the read `list_refinements`, with the empty list where the agent store refuses the agent's load; a host gives one value for its life, and a value makes facts only for a run whose `learn_run` rev 1 it stored and that it saw load, every other run reading as lost (served the compiled steps, each proposal answered `base`, its row left at rev 1); a replay `put_playbook` names the loaded number or the next one and every read for a step is by the loaded number, any other number and a step that the run was not served answering `base`; a later load adds its slot values, kept for a replay run alone; a run's facts go at its answered `finish`, at its `end_run` and at a refused load of it with a name other than `store` and `internal`, under the run's own agent, which the handlers send after a refused body of the run and at each accepted `start_run`, and at most `RUNS_HELD_MAX` (8) runs are held, the first made going first; and they refuse with `length` a recovery whose served step would have more than 2,048 bytes as the step's writer writes it, store no such edit and lay none. A learned target holds at most 2,048 bytes of its written form (`TARGET_BYTES` of halvo-schema, refused by the target's and the `learn_edit` row's parser and writer with the rule `target_bytes`), so that one `learn_edit` row holds at most 2,491 bytes, within the 2,560 of L1.7. The check 20-r5-rule gives each of the 30 tests of its list, the pin test of the `learn_edit` entry among them, the mark built or pending and fails on a test marked built whose line is missing; it fails with the cause `tests` before its first cargo command when a tracked file differs from HEAD (a changed or a staged file, the check's own file among them) or carries an index flag (assumed unchanged or skipped, the check's own file or any other), and a file that git does not track is allowed before the run; it holds the list and the workspace's crates before its first test run, refuses with the cause `tests` a tree that one of its cargo commands changed (a tracked file, HEAD, an untracked file or an index flag), and counts a listed test marked built only under its own target and its whole path as libtest prints them; it does not hold code that runs in the job before its reads (a test binary, a tool earlier on the PATH, code before main in a test file, a target with its harness off), as the routing run's SG-30 says.
- Added: Tests of learn_edit's rows of every state in halvo-schema (learning run, U2.4, contracts.md L1.1, L1.6 and L13; the coordinator's ruling of 2026-11-13 02:32Z (3)): `tests/r5_kinds.rs` runs the provisional, active, reverted and withdrawn rows through `check_record` of the registered `learn_edit` entry and through `LearnEdit::parse` and its writer, sends learn_edit's refusal cases to `LearnEdit::parse`, which must take the example, and requires that both readers refuse, under each of the 15 keys of a row, a scalar value or a site value of each field type that the key's type does not take, under `target` an integer at a locator's value (108 cases), with the generic check's error naming that key and each value first taken under a key of its own type, so that a field type widened or a pair added in the entry fails a test of its own whenever it changes what `check_record` takes; `tests/r5_records.rs` requires that its stand-in of the entry equals the entry of `learning::KINDS`.
- Added: The routine step `LearnStep` of halvo-learn-store (learning run, U5.1, contracts.md L4.5 and L6.1): a `WriterStep` that a host builds with `LearnStep::new(clock)` over its `Clock` and puts after the recurrence step. After each committed batch it reads the bounded summaries and the rows of the family `learn`, counts as an occurrence of a workflow a day with a summary of the workflow's category and whole site value, its source included (or, where the workflow has no site, a summary of the workflow's category with no site and the workflow's app), or a verified replay run of the workflow, one day once, applies `routine_step` of halvo-learn to the newest `learn_routine` row of each workflow and agent, and returns the next row, with `rev` + 1 and `day` today, only where the mark, the period or the last day changed or the newest row is at least 30 days old. It returns no `learn_edit` and no `learn_run` row, and nothing when a `learn_routine` or `learn_run` row cannot be read; it reads no `learn_edit` row.
- Security: Tests of the app's invoke handler on Tauri's mock runtime (capture run, app-1b, src-tauri/tests/page_guard.rs) hold three rules that app-1's line did not name, so the handler answers a capture command only to the web view main of the window main, whose Origin header, when the call has one, is an address of the app's origin, with no user information: a child web view of the window main, at the app's own address, is refused each of the eight capture commands with and without an Origin header, and its delete_all deletes nothing; a call from the web view main whose Origin header is no address (`null`, empty, text that the URL reader refuses, the app's host alone, or bytes that are not UTF-8) is refused each of them; and an address of the app's host with a password and no user name is refused as the Origin header, as the window's own address with no Origin header, and by the navigation rule, and the origin check refuses it for the arm of each system. A fourth test holds that the web view main, moved into another window of the app whose capability grants the eight commands, is refused each of them with and without an Origin header and deletes nothing, and a fifth that a capability for the window helper or for a made-up label handed to the context macro (`tauri::generate_context!` with `capabilities = [...]`) reaches the core app version command and none of the eight capture commands. The child web view and the move need tauri's feature `unstable`, which src-tauri/Cargo.toml enables in the dev-dependency alone, so at this commit no build of the app without its dev-dependencies carries it. On the hosted Windows runner the test binary of this file ended with exit code 0xc0000139 before any test ran; the team's reading of the cause is that tauri-build links the app manifest into the package's own binaries alone, so the file is built on Windows and holds no test there. On Windows these tests do not run, and the refusal of a child web view, of the web view main moved into another window, of an Origin header that is no address and of a password with no user name is held there by the macOS job's tests of the same code alone.
- Changed: The encrypted store's open never makes a database: a missing or empty store.db answers DatabaseMissing, a store key that does not open it answers DatabaseUnreadable, a key-file or database format version above or below the build's answers NewerStore or OlderStore, and a store that another process holds answers Locked before the custody is called (store-2).
- Added: The store's verify entries `measure_store(dir, marker)` and `measure_deletion(dir)`, which read neither HALVO_STORE_DIR nor HALVO_GATE_MARKER and use no default marker, the handle's `unwrap_time`, which `--verify-store` prints as unwrap_ms, a helper that writes a store file durably, a one-time SQLite initialization before the first open, and scripts/r1/ci.d/30-store-ignore-reason.sh, which fails on an ignore attribute without a reason, and two reads of the store's formal checks: every file of a store has mode 0600 and every directory mode 0700 after every operation, and an open that begins and ends while another process holds the store is refused before it asks its custody (store-2).
- Fixed: The store's keyless sweep follows no link and removes only files with the store's segment names, a store.key that is a link is unlinked without a write through it, on macOS a shred syncs the shard directory after it removes a file with std's sync_all on the directory's handle, which asks the drive for a full sync, and an open of the store no longer fails with "no such file" when the lock file is unlinked while it is being opened: the open of writer.lock is tried again; the store reaches each entry of its own through the handle of its directory: it opens segments, a shard, store.key and a segment file in one step that refuses a link, lists and removes through the handle that it walked, so a shard swapped for a link during or after the walk is counted and nothing behind it is removed, and a segment write that finds its shard no longer named by segments, or its partial file's name no longer the file that it wrote, before its publish answers Corrupt, publishes nothing and removes its partial file, which a later sweep removes when that removal fails; a link at store.key or at a segment file answers Corrupt with a text that names no id, a store.key that is a FIFO or another special file answers KeyFileUnparseable and such a segment file Corrupt, both without blocking; and an open that refuses a format version keeps an empty store.db-wal; create tests and clears its directory through the same handle, so a directory that another process puts at the name after that loses no file and the store answers Corrupt before SQLite opens a database at a name that no longer stands for its directory, and create takes any entry at store.key, a link to a missing file among them, as a store (store-2).
- Security: The store opens only when WAL journaling, checkpoint_fullfsync, cipher_log_level NONE, the LibTomCrypt provider of SQLCipher 4.19.0 and the OS entropy hook read back, and on macOS it makes every directory 0700 and every file 0600; every connection of the store, the probe for a holder among them, is opened by one function that reads store.db, store.db-wal, store.db-shm and store.db-journal through the store's handle and refuses a link there (on Windows also a junction or another reparse point) before SQLite opens them by name, so on neither system does SQLite write the log through a link that was there before the open; it opens with SQLITE_OPEN_NOFOLLOW and no URI under the name of the store directory resolved to one with no link in it, so on macOS a link put at store.db after the store's checks is not opened, and a store directory named file:x keeps its own database; on macOS open sets store.db to 0600 on its own descriptor before the connection opens, so its write-ahead log is made 0600; on Windows the store holds each directory of its chain, and store.db until the connection is open, with a handle that shares no delete (store-2).
- Removed: The store's verify entries `verify::run_store_check()` and `verify::run_deletion_check()`, which nothing called since `verify_store()` and `verify_deletion()` run through `measure_store` and `measure_deletion` (store-2).
- Fixed: The workflow lint 17-workflow-lint refuses, each at its first line, a byte of ci.yml other than the line feed and printable ASCII, a key in the explicit form (`?` and a blank), a document marker or directive in column 1, a node that begins with `&`, `*` or `!` (a plain line that begins with one of the three reads so too), and a line that it reads as ending inside a quoted scalar or a flow collection (a block header alone on the next line, and a plain line that begins with a quote or a bracket, read so too) (jobtime-6, rulings JB-8 and JT-4).
- Changed: ADR 0011 says why each CI job's limit is 90 minutes, with the macos job's measured seconds, and names jobtime-4 among the writers of ci.yml (jobtime-6, ruling JB-8).
