- Fixed: scripts/r1-gate/mutation-test.sh (capture run, mutants-2) refuses a row name that stands in two rows, in one rows file or in several, before it applies any row and under `--list` too, and names each place of the name (ruling JB-4 J2); a transitional row of scripts/r1-gate/mutants.rows and the row that takes it over in the rows file beside its file still share their name, a transitional row in another rows file is refused in the run modes, names compare as text, also with a backslash, and a row named malformed is checked like any other. It reads every tracked file named mutants.rows of HEAD from git's list of the tracked paths and refuses one that is a symbolic link, that the copy of HEAD lacks or whose path holds a tab, a newline, a backslash or a quote, in every mode (ruling ST-28 E3). TERM, INT or HUP to the runner, to its process group or to every process below it no longer ends the test that a row runs in the middle of a case, which left a root of the case in the login keychain (inbox point 67): a cargo test runs with the three signals ignored, and the runner sends the test's process group TERM, waits up to 60 s for it and prints `STOPPED mutation:<row> (signal <name>; the running case was let end)`, or kills the test after 60 s and says so. The stop traps are the runner's first commands, and in every mode but --self-test and at every phase, from the first signal on, the runner starts no new step of the run (a row, a test, a build, a gate check, a copy, a read), prints exactly one STOPPED line in one of four forms and no FAIL, PASS, OPEN, NOT-RUN or count line for a step that the signal ended, and exits with 143, 130 or 129; a step whose status says that a signal ended it is never read as an answer, a killed gate check of the control pass or of a measured row and a killed build of a mutant other than a cargo build among them (rulings ST-28 E1 and ST-31).
- Changed: `--strict` of scripts/r1-gate/mutation-test.sh also fails on a row whose state is `required` or `required on <place>` and whose name scripts/r1-gate/mutants.expected lacks, naming the row and its rows file, and on a row that `--budget` left unrun (ruling ST-28 E5), and the count line of every run ends with the number of required rows with no expected name (capture run, mutants-2, inbox point 63 (k)); mutants.expected now names all 140 required rows of the five rows files, the ten of the macOS sources' MA-3 round and the seven of core-2 among the 108 names that it gains, and the runner reads it raw in one pass with the rows, a last line without its line end too. The runner removes its scratch directory, the throwaway copy of HEAD with its build directory, when it ends, on every path but a KILL of the runner itself; `--keep-scratch` keeps it and prints its path, and `--remove-scratch` is accepted and is the default (ruling ST-28 E6).
- Changed: Check 50 (billing run, 4.6) reads a delayed request by contract 10 and says when its rig was slow: a 409 by the whole table of ruling B-34, also in the kill, where any answer is excused only when its body cannot be read (P7-69, P7-76), an id with more than one upstream call as a violation in every attempt, whatever its last answer (contract 10, P7-69), a request that ends with EPIPE is sent again as one that ends with ECONNRESET (B-35), a last answer of the dev proxy's 500 is a request lost in transport whose count goes to the check's log (B-36), a 503 voided, a 502 no-time-left and a 502 of the cut by the bound of src/gateway/timing.ts read from the driver's record (B-37 and the coordinator's answer on the cut), an attempt whose first burst waited longer than the hold's life less the margin does not count while every clause on rows, the mock's log and the cap is still read in it (B-33, B-38), and a wave held until its last reserve was answered, read from the receipts and the refusals, has exactly K hold rows, its streams counted apart (B-37, P7-69); its line gains slow and burst-p50-at-200 and names slow attempts, with the median of each bound, as the cause of a failure without a violation (B-39), a rollover attempt that admitted nothing before the period boundary is drawn again, at most five times for the cell, the line counts the draws in rollover-redrawn and fails with the rig as its cause when the cell has spent its five draws, naming the draws, how many of the cell's runs had counted and the smallest gap, and saying that the cell built no crossing only when none of its attempts built it, while an attempt that admitted before the boundary and lacks the crossing still fails at once (B-15, read by P7-72), and its self-test reads 175 record fixtures of gate/fixtures/concurrency/records/ with no stack. scripts/prerequisites.sh (billing run, F.4) also runs backend/scripts/fetch-licenses.mjs, which fetches from the package registry the licence of each package of backend/pnpm-lock.yaml that the platform does not install, so that the backend's licence check 85 reads every package in a clone that the one command set up. backend/gate/license-allowlist.json holds the owner's seven licences (MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause, ISC, Zlib, Unicode-3.0) in place of eleven, and backend/gate/license-exceptions.json names each of the 29 packages outside them by its exact name and version, in 28 entries with the licence, every chain that brings the package and why it is neither linked nor shipped, in place of five entries, three of them patterns, that covered 26. The gate (ruling P7-68) excepts a package only at a version that an entry names under the entry's licence, refuses an entry with * or without versions and a named version that the lock does not hold, stops at a line of a packages section of the lock that it cannot read, and reads a list of licences in a manifest as AND with each element in parentheses, a list with an element that it cannot read as unread, with the licence cache marked by that reading.
- Added: A workerd test of the backend (billing run, 4.7) in which the promise of the account object's alarm call rejects after the dispatch step has committed, once because the host refuses NaN, which the test passes in place of the step's time, and once after the host's write of the step's time: the gateway gets go, the upstream is called once, the hold settles at the reported usage and the next request gets go; up to 50 ms after each request, the turn that the object's timers get, the hold keeps the dispatch mark, the object is not reset, the host's alarm calls are, for each of the two requests in order, setAlarm for the reserve, setAlarm for the dispatch and deleteAlarm for the settle, and no other call of setAlarm or deleteAlarm, and the app catches each rejection and writes one line for each, alarm-error with the error's name by errorName of src/log.ts (unit 4.8) (ruling B-51). scripts/r7/ci.d/25-r7-backend-licenses (billing run, F.4) runs the backend's check 85 in the macos job: it passes when the allowlist at HEAD is the owner's seven, the exceptions at HEAD name 29 versions by exact name, every package that the pinned pnpm lists from the lock is one that the gate's reader reads, check 85 passes and counts 29 packages outside the owner's list, and after it no tracked file changed and the two lists and the lock are HEAD's bytes. Its self-test runs the gate on the fixtures of backend/gate/fixtures/license-lists/, the reader on the lock forms that pnpm reads and the gate did not, the script itself on stub repositories, and the real pnpm on a committed lock; --help names the gate's limit: it reads licence statements, never licence texts. The wall between backend/ and the app (billing run, F.1b, rulings P7-23, P7-36 and P7-60): scripts/r7/ci.d/24-r7-wall.sh, in both jobs, reads the git index and fails when a file under backend/ climbs, links or follows links out of backend/ and formal/r7-ledger/, or reads the app by a rev:path, by $(git rev-parse --show-toplevel) or, after a variable that it cannot read, a tracked path of the app that backend/ does not track, a variable assigned once from one of five forms naming its directory; when a file of the app (but .md files and the files under docs/ that are no script) names a path that lands under backend/ by any walk, also after a variable, a command substitution or CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR, a dotted path, a glob or a rev:path, or gives the word backend as a directory, a project, a first operand of a listed command, a brace member, a parameter default, a label or a member; when a link points to an absolute path; and when it cannot read the tree (a name that git quotes, an unparsed line, a step that it cannot read). Its exceptions are the 9 files under scripts/r7/ that --help lists, the step of scripts/prerequisites.sh that installs backend/'s packages, read by its structure, and the four files of the wire contract named whole under account/schema/; it passes the 11 named limits of --help: names-v0, computed-path, bare-module, file-or-environment, nul-byte, model-side, absolute-path, both-sides-path, unread-variable, bare-operand, bracket-glob. scripts/r7/ci.d/26-r7-pnpm-step (billing run, F.5) runs the self-test of scripts/prerequisites.sh, with the cases of the pnpm step and of its reader of the lock, in the macos and windows jobs, and fails when the self-test exits other than 0, prints a FAIL line, does not end with its PASS line after at least one case, or leaves a tracked file other than the index holds it; its own self-test rejects six fixtures, among them a copy of the script whose platform package is no longer checked against the lock.
- Fixed: The account object of the backend (billing run, 4.8) catches a rejection of the promise of the host's setAlarm or deleteAlarm call that follows a step, which local workerd gives in place of a throw (unit 4.7), and writes the existing log line alarm-error with the error's name, read once by errorName of src/log.ts, and no other field of the caught value; the step's committed answer, the dispatch mark and the alarm calls stay as they were, and nothing retries, answers an error, writes a row or resets the object, while the awaited deleteAlarm of the object's removal is not caught and reaches its caller as before (contract 1.4, rulings B-48, B-51 and P7-91). The workerd test backend/test/ledger/alarm-rejection.test.ts makes the dispatch step's setAlarm and, by ruling P7-83 (4) (iii), the reserve step's setAlarm and the settle step's deleteAlarm reject, in seven tests, one with a value whose name reads otherwise at its second read, and the same call reject again with a string in the next request; it records every console method, holds the time of each setAlarm against the open hold's expiry, and, with Date.now fixed for both runs, compares every stored value of the object (schema, rows, key-value entries and alarm, the account id alone masked) with the same requests without a rejection, up to 50 ms after each request.
- Security: scripts/prerequisites.sh (billing run, F.5) no longer lets npm fetch the platform package of pnpm unchecked: where it installs pnpm, it downloads the package @pnpm/exe.<platform> of the pinned version from the package registry for the five hosts that the script names (darwin-arm64, darwin-x64, win32-x64, linux-x64 and linux-arm64), checks its sha512 against the integrity value under that package's entry in the first document of backend/pnpm-lock.yaml, and stops with exit 1 before npm runs when the bytes differ, when the script names no platform package for the host or when the lock gives no value; the lock gives a value only when every line of its first document, from its first line, which is ---, to the next line that is exactly --- or to the end of the file, is one of the 23 line forms that pnpm writes there (the first marker and a blank line among them), with no line of more than 1,024 characters, no byte outside printable ASCII and no carriage return other than one at a line's end, no key stands twice, and the package's entry stands under packages: with its resolution line right under it; each refusal names its cause, with the line's number where there is one; npm then installs the pnpm archive and that package offline, with optional packages omitted, no update check and a cache directory that the step makes new and empty under backend/.tools and removes afterwards, so that it fetches nothing more and places no package that the step did not check.
- Removed: backend/scripts/names-v0.sh (billing run, the first joint landing), the script that wrote backend/contract/names/forbidden-names.json once from crates/halvo-capture/src/event.rs of the app and from the kickoff plan, with the two tests of backend/test/contract/names.node.test.ts that ran it; the names file and the two tests that hold it stay.
- Fixed: 20-schema refuses, before cargo runs, a list scripts/r2/20-schema-tests.txt at HEAD that holds a byte other than a printable ASCII character, a space, a tab, a carriage return or a line feed, because the awk of macOS ends a line at a NUL byte and so read a forged line as empty, a line with a carriage return before its end, named by its line number and with no byte of the line printed, and a line whose package is neither halvo-schema-core nor halvo-schema, which it counted before and never checked; the refusals of a line out of form and of a line that stands twice move before cargo too, and the check names a byte fault first, wherever it stands in the list, and then the first faulty line of the list; its rule line names among its limits a tool that a step of the workflow itself puts earlier on the PATH, beside one that code of the job writes there (workflow run, U1.1g; rulings S-53 and S-77).
- Changed: export-names takes the kind names and the family names from halvo_schema::kinds::registry() and hands them to the function that builds the names file beside the NAMES lists, so that the names file no longer depends on a later run's NAMES list holding them, while each later run's own test still requires its list to hold them; tests of tests/golden.rs require the names file to come out byte for byte as tests/golden/names.txt when the later lists leave those names out, require that function to write a kind and a family name that it is handed and neither when it is not, and require every kind and family name of the registry to be a line of the names file (workflow run, U1.1g; contracts.md C1.10; ruling S-77).
- Added: A test of its own for the line role: the_committed_names_file_holds_no_line_role of tests/golden.rs fails when tests/golden/names.txt holds that line, which contracts.md C1.10 keeps out of the file because the routing run's gateway check counts the body keys of its request that the file holds; golden_names failed on such a file before, among its other checks, and still fails on it, by its rule against a line that no rule gives, though it no longer names role among the names that must be absent (workflow run, U1.1g; rulings S-56 (a) and S-77).
- Added: The gate host learn-gate of the learning run (learning run, U2.2, contracts.md L9, L10.1 to L10.4 and L11.1): the crate halvo-learn-gate with its own case reader and page model; a store in memory that keeps the rows of the families agent and learn, each behind its own host calls, with the expiry by day at an open and the delete cascade by workflow; the stub runner, whose compile sends create_agent, a start_run of the mode compile, a load, the put_playbook of the compiled playbook under number 1, finish and set_state to active, and whose replay runs name in their put_playbook the number that the run loaded plus 1, in every arm, with the playbook's steps from step 1; `learn-gate drift` and `learn-gate delete --what workflow` on the memory store, the delete line reading result=ok only when the deleted workflow had learn rows that the cascade takes, the other workflow's learn rows stay the same rows by kind and bytes and its active edit is still served, while it counts no row of the family agent and does not read the workflow record; the test constants_match_targets, which also holds the schema's three bounds of an edit row against the rule's constants and which 20-r5-rule.sh, now a closed list of 42 names with a form for a test in a binary's unit tests, requires as built; and the check 50-r5-drift, which refuses a changed tree before its build and holds, against the trial harness's, the occurrences, drifts, assisted, failed and harm of the arms none and plain of each development case, holds the play with every occurrence restarted against the first in every integer but expired_runs, asks a restart between occurrences to expire more runs in the development set, and asks the held-out set for no difference in expired_runs.
- Fixed: The tests of src-tauri/tests/page_guard.rs (capture run, app-1c, ruling AP-5 W (2)) are built and run in the windows job too: src-tauri/build.rs gives the integration test binaries of the package halvo, on a Windows MSVC target alone, the Common Controls version 6 manifest that tauri-build gives the app's own binaries (src-tauri/windows-tests.manifest, byte for byte the file of tauri-build 2.7.4), by link arguments for tests alone, so the link of halvo.exe does not change; the file loses its build condition and gets back its three Windows-only lines, so the Windows limit at the end of app-1b's Security line above no longer holds.
- Added: The check scripts/r1/ci.d/36-app-guard-tests (capture run, app-1c, the three checks of ruling AP-5) runs in both jobs. It runs src-tauri/tests/page_guard.rs alone and fails unless each of the five tests on its list guard_tests prints ok by name. It fails when one of the five is missing, when a test of the run is ignored or fails, and when a test of the run is neither on that list nor in one of the four modules of the app that the file brings in by a path attribute (app, commands, launch and tray, its list app_modules), so a test in a module of the file itself fails it. It also fails when the run's three counts are not one number (the N of `running N tests`, the number of ok lines and the N passed of the result line), when guard_tests does not hold five names, each once, and when the file is built to zero tests. It fails when `cargo tree -p halvo -e normal --target all` prints tauri, from any source, with the feature unstable or test, which its self-test shows for a copy of the workspace without its resolver line, when tauri.conf.json or a tauri.<platform>.conf.json of the app at HEAD, its name read without regard to case, holds build.features, and when the app holds a tauri configuration in JSON5 or TOML, which it does not read. When a tracked file differs from HEAD it stops before cargo runs and says to commit or stash the change, and it fails when a tracked file differs from HEAD after its cargo runs.
- Fixed: The macOS sources follow the source contract (capture run, macos-2): each answers a second begin with AlreadyStarted, returns from shutdown inside its deadline (1 s for input and foreground, 6 s for screen) with Ok, ThreadEnded or Timeout, or for a screen stop that reports an error the Os answer with its code, and stops on Drop; the screen stop counts its wait from the stop request and waits out a reply that is dropped unanswered before it answers Timeout; the tap thread keeps a stop that comes before its run loop runs; the system-audio and microphone sources answer Started::Off; a window title is read as at most 1,024 UTF-16 units, so an unpaired surrogate no longer panics the foreground thread, and a bundle identifier is read as at most 1,024 UTF-16 units and converted lossily, so such an identifier no longer ends the process; a foreground change is keyed on the pid, so an unbundled app and one bundle id under two pids each give an event, and nothing in front clears the shared value; a failed thread start carries fixed text, and PermissionDenied comes only from the system's own answer that the grant is missing.
- Changed: InputSource::new takes the reader, an InputCounters handle that the caller keeps for the life of the process and a HoldPlan, and the tap thread takes and gives back the secure-input hold of the plan (capture run, macos-2); the three Carbon secure-input calls run inside one lock of the process, and the lock's token can neither be sent nor shared to another thread; a second declaration of a call outside the lock still compiles, and what finds one is the test that counts the reader's calls of the system, which fails for a reader that goes around the lock, and the unit gate's search, which names the calls that take and give back the hold; ForegroundSource::new takes the stamp of the main-run-loop helper; ForegroundReader::get returns an Option of the app in front, a bundle id or executable path capped at 1,024 bytes with its pid; focused_window_title returns a typed result with the title's length; installed_applications takes the home directory; run_sources begins and shuts down the five sources through the contract's members and turns the main run loop for the run.
- Added: macos/run_loop.rs, the helper that turns the main run loop on the process's main thread and makes the one foreground writer, and a title-reader thread whose read makes two Accessibility calls, the focused window and its title, each with a deadline of 400 ms, that asks again for 2,000 ms after "no focused window" and counts title_read_errors (capture run, macos-2).
- Removed: MacosSecureInput, SecureInputViolations::verify_none and SecureInputViolated, which nothing in the product called, and the public function self_exclusion_enforced_in_filter, which answered Ok always and kept nothing out of a screen stream (capture run, macos-2).
- Fixed: The model lock of halvo-cap (workflow run, U0.2b, ruling S-64 point 2) takes its base from the account by the system's call and reads no variable: on macOS the home directory of the account of the process's effective user id by `getpwuid_r`, on Windows the LocalAppData known folder of the process's own token by `SHGetKnownFolderPath`, with the path below the base unchanged, so two processes of one account whose `HOME`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, `APPDATA` or `USERPROFILE` differ meet at one lock file and can no longer load two models at once.
- Changed: In halvo-cap (workflow run, U0.2b), `LockError::NoCacheDir` gives way to `LockError::NoAccountDir { call, code }`, which names the system's call and no variable, for a lookup whose call fails or that gives no directory or no absolute path; an existing lock file is opened for reading alone and made only where it is missing, so a home that became read-only still gives the lock; and the lock's tests stand in the test module of lock.rs and take their locks through a private function in directories of their own, while one test that holds the account's lock runs on a hosted runner alone.
- Fixed: The test `probe::tests::a_window_leaves_out_the_calls_of_the_first_thread` of halvo-route-gate (routing run U4.1f, ruling SG-49) no longer fails when a call of the first thread is under way while the mark moves, and three tests now hold that the windows are compared on the other threads alone, that a test on the first thread is refused at the call, and that the helper refuses a child that prints the line of a pass and fails.
- Added: (capture run, control-2) Protocol v1 between the app and the capture daemon in halvo-control: the frame codec, the ten requests and ten replies with the status that every line of `ctl status` comes from, the delete rule and the two permission forms that the daemon will share, the reserved names run_start and run_end, the transport-neutral client with its deadlines and its unix connector, which checks the socket's directory, the socket and the server's user and, when the app names it, the server's pid, the ctl text types, whose tests put the two record lines of C2.3 into gate-2's four C2 status fixtures and not_followed=0 into gate-2's c2-deleted.txt and read CR LF as LF before they hold the render byte for byte, until gate-3 adds the lines, the count and the attribute, the text table of the view, the threat model crates/halvo-control/SECURITY.md with a row for each request, POLL_MS, INDICATOR_LEASE_MS, STOP_WAIT_MS, SESSION_END_BUDGET_MS and the exit statuses, a scripted daemon under the feature test-support, the three registration points of the later run under the feature r2, the registry row of the billing run's account helper store directory, com.halvo.account, which delete-all leaves and Remove Halvo removes, and, for a source that does not stop, exit status 5, last_stop=source_not_stopped, capture=awaiting_resume, the three status lines of the kept record of the last life (last_stop_kind, last_stop_lost, last_stop_source) and the source reasons writer_gone and item_closed; the module instance_lock on macOS, with the take of a data directory's instance lock (a non-blocking exclusive flock on the directory's own close-on-exec descriptor, tried every TAKE_STEP_MS, 50 ms, for at most TAKE_FOR_MS, 1 s, and released when its holder is dropped) and its read, which never waits and answers free, held or no directory; and REQUEST_DECODE_PEAK_BYTES, 524,288, and REPLY_DECODE_PEAK_BYTES, 33,554,432, the most bytes that one decode call holds live for a frame at each bound, which a test holds with a counting allocator on frames that carry their tree in a body that the decoder reads, with a sweep over the depth and the width of that tree, where the worst frames hold 518,676 and 33,047,177.
- Changed: (capture run, control-2) In halvo-control, ScratchDir::new takes the account lookup in place of a data directory, remove_login_item returns the file's outcome with the sweep's error beside it, the owner of the login item's file is compared with a type that only the process's effective user builds, the Windows folder lookup runs its call through a function that frees the buffer on every path, the golden onboarding JSON holds every spelling of grant, action and request outcome, ctl pause exits 1 with a line on stderr when the reply reads capture=pausing, ctl gives its data directory by one method that applies the default, each onboarding row says on which macOS version its titles were observed, and on Windows the folder lookup is an unsafe function with its condition stated and the token and the registry key are made only by the calls that open them; the delete rule follows ruling PL-8: an armed id against a status that shows none, no id beside a shown id, and no id on an open store or one with no release custody answer the new reply delete_refused, which carries no id and which ctl prints with exit 1 and a line that says this request deleted nothing, already_deleted always names the current store id, and the client and ctl take an already_deleted that names the armed id, or that answers a request with no id, as an error with exit 1; ctl pause and ctl resume exit 0 only when the reply reaches the end that the verb asks for, by the public function reaches_the_end (a pause: desired paused and capture paused; a resume: desired running and capture starting, running, partial, faulted or no_indicator), and the state file was written, and otherwise name the desired and capture values that the service answered on standard error; a reply whose status breaks a rule of the status type is the client error StateDoesNotHoldTogether with the rule's fixed text, which ctl prints after "the capture service reported a state that does not hold together: " with exit 1, and the view's text table gives "Typing is not observed on Windows in this version." only while the Windows keyboard source line reads off and "Screen activity is not recorded." only while the screen source line reads off, and a line of either source that is not off carries its sentence about recording as halvo-control's texts give it; the unix connector keeps the data directory and, on each path that found no daemon, reads its instance lock: free or no directory is unreachable at once, and held makes it try the connect again every 50 ms inside the request's deadline and then answer the client error DataDirInUse, which ctl prints as "the data directory is in use and no connection could be made at <socket path>; capture may be running" with exit 1; by ruling ST-30 the closed list of store_error gains obstructed, a cause of store=unavailable (something that is not the store's own stands at one of its names, or the store directory is not at its name), and the deleted report and ctl's deleted line gain the count not_followed directly after database_left, always present, so that a report without it does not decode.
- Fixed: (capture run, control-2) A scratch directory that holds the data directory, such as the home or a link to it, is refused, and tests now hold what passed with a wrong copy: the order of the two Accessibility steps, a sweep that follows a link to a regular file, a scratch form of the login item's directory that holds the path as given, a locked store with a stale id and a socket directory with a special bit; and the decoder refuses a frame with a JSON key twice, the status refuses a capture value that the states of its required sources contradict, no_indicator beside a fresh lease or a desired state that is not running, faulted beside a lease that is not fresh or a desired state that is not running, awaiting_resume beside a desired state that is not running, and paused beside a desired state that is not paused, the client takes one reply kind for each op, every write of a request and its end get the time that is left before its deadline, a silent scripted daemon answers nothing whatever arrives, the scripted daemon keeps capture=pausing once it reads it, keeps the hold of awaiting_resume through a claim and a pause until a Resume, and restarts on a Resume a stream that the person stopped, ctl prints a peer's text as one bounded line of printable ASCII and an error of the typed client with the exit status of the error reply, and a frame that holds in v an integer from 0 to 18446744073709551615 other than 1 is answered unsupported_version when it starts with { and ends with }, is one JSON object with each key once at every depth, nests no deeper than 127 levels, each object and array counted with the frame's own, and holds no number outside a 64-bit float and no lone surrogate escape, and every other such frame invalid_argument; and the client takes a whole reply frame whose peer keeps the connection: after the frame it waits at most REPLY_END_WAIT_MS, 250 ms, inside the request's deadline, a byte in that wait is an error, and the end of the wait, the end of the stream and any error of the transport give the reply; the instance lock's read and take open the data directory with O_DIRECTORY and O_NONBLOCK, so that a FIFO, a file, a socket or a link to one at its path is an error at once with exit 1 and never waits, and the read of the login item's file takes only the regular file that it looked at, through a handle opened without following a link and without waiting.
- Added: The schema's part of the rows of answered messages (agent-setup run, U1.7, rulings CP-130 (a), CP-135, CP-141 (2), CP-143, CP-153 (6), CP-154): the kinds `agent_reply` and `agent_start` of origin own, kept by default, with their readers `AgentReply` and `AgentStart`, their entries, examples and names; the runner bodies `reply_row` and `load_replies` with the reply `LoadRepliesReply`, held to `RUNNER_REPLY_VALUE_MAX`, 65,455 bytes, as its bytes arrive and at its writer; the `Cause` names `unauthorized`, `not_selected` and `no_start` and the `LogEvent` name `start_accepted`.
- Changed: The record `agent_run` holds the twelve keys of the run's list after `model`, `earlier_page` last, each a count, `yes` or `no`, or null, and the handler of `end_run` writes each as null; the reply of `load` holds the key `start`, the confirmation request the key `cut`, and the readers of `agent_log` and `agent_effect` refuse the new causes outside their rows (agent-setup run, U1.7, rulings CP-135 (2), (5), CP-143 (2), (3) (v), CP-147, CP-151, CP-165 (1)).
- Added: The fixture variants `no_reply` and `amount_changes` of the agent-setup run (U4.2, contracts.md A10.5, A10.7, CP-103 (2)) in the index of tools/fixture-server/variants with their self-test cases: under `no_reply` the first state-changing request after each reset is carried out and recorded and gets no reply, and under `amount_changes` the control host takes `POST /change`, after which the payment frame shows and posts the seed's amount with 1.00 added; and in halvo-runner (U4.2, contracts.md A5.2, A6.3, the coordinator's ruling CP-142) the confirm path behind one port that every request maker implements: the effects proposed after `admit`, the `held` ones asked in one confirmation request, the bound values read again while the request is paused, a `rebind` and a new confirmation with the new values, `begin` with their digest, the request let through once under the lock of the link and `commit` only after its reply, with the port's suite and the tests `refusal_commit_without_reply` and `refusal_changed_on_page`, which run it with a stand-in maker that is test code alone; a later load that gives `confirm` for a `cleared` effect ends every effect of the step that is still `cleared` and asks for them again in one confirmation, with at most two times for one step, a batch counting once (CP-143 (4) (iv), CP-157 E4); a `reject_once` ends its effect alone on every card, the other effects of the call go to their `begin` in their order, and, when no effect of the call is open and no other end came first, the run ends `declined` (CP-174 Part 1); and a Stop that is held when an answer comes is read after the rejected rows and before any approved row (CP-174 Part 2).
- Changed: In halvo-runner (agent-setup run, U4.2, contracts.md A7.4, CP-135 (5)), the runner's log lines count from 2, because the store's handler of `start_run` stores the row 1; and the limit `wall` ends a run whose time is at or above the `wall_s` of the newest load of the configuration (A6.5, CP-135 (3) (iv)); a file edit reads the configuration and the bindings once before `prepare`, which with the bound object and `replace` uses that load, and again before `begin`, which it sends only while `user_files` holds, `decide` gives `run` and the object built again from that load has the row's digest, else it ends the effect and drops the prepared copy, proposes again from that load at most twice for one step and then ends `failed` with `changed`, and it ends `failed` with `changed`, writing nothing, when that load names another source or folder than the read step's (A11.3, CP-135 (3), CP-139 (b)); and the runner reads a `cancel` when a handback result or a `secret_reply` that it waited for has come, and under the lock that lets an effect act after an answered `begin`, where a held `cancel` lets no request through and makes no `replace` (A6.2, CP-135 (4), reading 1).
- Fixed: In halvo-runner's confirm path (agent-setup run, U4.2, ruling CP-157), the request port takes the slots of the request's class from the load that the runner has just made, so that the object built again at a `begin` and the request made again after a `rebind` use the newest load's slots, and a request that does not carry a changed slot ends its effect `rejected` and the run `failed` with `changed` before any `rebind` row (A5.1, A5.2); the row of a `rebind` and every later row of the effect keep the fields of the object whose digest they carry (A7.4); and a `cancel` and the limit `wall` are read before each `propose` of an effect proposed again, as before the first (A6.2, A6.5); before each `begin` the paused request's origin must be an origin of the later load's bindings or the new origin of the confirmation that the person allowed, a fact of the runner's own and never a maker's, else the effect is ended and the run ends `refused` with `origin`, and `admit` is asked with that load's origins (A9.2, CP-159 G1, CP-166 E1); and a file edit holds the load before its `begin` against the sheet of its `prepare`, proposing again for another sheet, and against the read step's source, ending the run `failed` with `changed` for another source (CP-159 G2, G3); the runner stores `commit` only for an answer that the request maker reads as made, from the one success status of its request, and an answer that it does not read as made leaves the effect `releasing` and ends the run `effect_unknown` (CP-159 G4).
- Security: The supervisor library starts the runner with three of its handles or descriptors open in it and no other, the two ends of the link and the null device (agent-setup run, U4.9, contracts.md A6.6): on macOS every other descriptor closes at the runner's exec, and on Windows the library starts the runner by `CreateProcessW` with `PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST` naming those three; tests of halvo-agent-client hold it on each system: a descriptor that the library holds without close-on-exec (macOS), and a handle that it made inheritable and its own standard output and standard error handles (Windows), are not open in the runner. It gives the runner a whole environment from a closed list and nothing else of its own process (ruling CP-160 (1)): `HOME` and `TMPDIR` on macOS, `SystemRoot`, `TEMP`, `TMP` and `LOCALAPPDATA` on Windows, `HOME` and `LOCALAPPDATA` as the library's process has them and the others as the system answers them, and it starts the runner in `/` on macOS and in the system directory on Windows; tests show the names, a marker of the library that reaches no value of the runner, the working directory, a write by a relative name that leaves nothing under the data directory, and on Windows a loopback socket, which the stand-in cannot open without `SystemRoot`. On Windows the runner gets a console of its own that shows no window, which a process that it starts without a console flag of its own shares, so the runner starts attached to no console of the library's process, and `TEMP` and `TMP` come from the LocalAppData known folder of the library's token, so that the library's profile variables do not reach them (ruling CP-164). Two runners started at the same moment from two threads hold no end of each other's link, and a runner whose link ends a helper process also holds is still ended within `LINK_END` and seen as ended, and a runner that outlives its stored `finish` has its link closed `link_end` after it and is ended once twice `link_end` has passed since it (ruling CP-172); the docs of `Config` say that a process that another start of the library's process makes in that moment can hold a runner's link ends, and what bounds the harm. On macOS a test holds that while the library holds a file, both ends of a pipe, a UNIX socket pair, a directory and a kqueue without close-on-exec at 3, 101 to 104, 256 and 5000, the runner's first list of its own descriptors is 0, 1 and 2 (ruling CP-169).
- Added: In halvo-agent-client (agent-setup run, U4.9), the library keeps how each runner ended when it reaps it, as the type `RunnerExit` (the status that it exited with, or on macOS the signal that ended it), so that a refusal, status 2, is told from a runner that the library killed; `RunEnd` and the answer of a start do not carry it yet, because the next unit of src/supervisor.rs adds that. The function `absolute_paths` refuses a start whose runner path, `--browser`, `--model` or data directory is not absolute, naming which (ruling CP-160 (1) (e)); the start calls it from the next unit of src/supervisor.rs on. The docs of `Config` say what the runner gets: its three handles, a standard error that nobody reads, its environment and its working directory.
- Fixed: Tests of halvo-agent-client that lost a race with their own stand-in runner (agent-setup run, U4.3f): the test of a frame that cannot be read waited for a line that the stand-in writes after that frame, which the library ends the runner on at once, and now waits for the end of the run and asserts the runner and its one child gone; eight tests whose stand-in exits by itself after its hello and that need the start to answer ok (start.rs, reap.rs, relay.rs, sigchld.rs, sigchld_kill.rs) now have the stand-in ask for a secret and exit only after the library's reply, which comes after the start has answered, in place of nothing or of a sleep, because on macOS the library reads the runner's line from the process table after it makes the process, and the library's read of the table (proc_pidinfo without the zombie flag, and the crate's public reads) finds no row of a process that has exited, reaped or not, while a read with the flag still finds the one that is not reaped, which the new macOS test the_read_without_the_zombie_flag_finds_no_exited_child_reaped_or_not_and_the_read_with_it_finds_the_unreaped_one shows; sigchld.rs holds the end_run of its run until it has read the mark; and sigchld_kill.rs waits, before its kill, until its own waitid answers that the runner has been reaped, and asserts that it did, because to the library's read a runner that has exited is gone before the system has reaped it, so a gone row is not a reap, and a kill made before the reap ended the group whose number the runner still held.
- Added: The crate halvo-coord-app, the coordinator inside the app on Tauri's mock runtime: one invoke handler for the seven commands of halvo-coord's LIST that answers the page of the window main alone and every refusal as an error object with one of the twelve causes stale, presenter, early, hold, state, budget, queue_full, busy, no_service, unknown_item, unknown_agent and malformed, never refuses agents_snapshot or agents_next, reads an agents_next whose arguments do not fit as one with after 0, answers the stand-in state (seq 0, empty lists, the note note.no_service) while no coordinator serves the page, before the app manages one or before the hub's first tick, and the waiting agents_next, which answers a newer state at once and a change that it waits for after coalesce_ms, each no sooner than coalesce_ms after the last reply with a state, its own after with empty lists after next_wait_s with no change, and closed at a hide or a destroy of the window main, with U0.2's verdict hide on macOS and destroy on Windows; agents_present hands a native confirmation to the presenter of U4.2 once while its dialog is open, and the page's answer to a native item stays presenter; the count of agents under needs_you reaches the sink that an app hands to the hub at each change, with what the tray is to show for it, while no app hands one in yet; and the hooks of the app's start and poller pass (one tick) and of its quit, which takes the coordinator's first step, waits with no move held open until no run is live, at most cancel_grace_s counted from the end of its first step, or from the stop of a run that was stopped before the quit, and at most the time that the system gives, and then takes the second step.
- Added: In halvo-coord-app, the presenter of native confirmations (I4.9): a card whose title and two controls come from the words file by the classes of the request's effects, the mixed entries for more than one class, and whose lines are the item's fields, one line each, a value's pieces joined with nothing; the app's one mapping, in which only the press on the allowing control allows and a failed call, a cancel, an unknown response and a time limit each reject; a presenter that reads the card from a reader that the app hands it, shows it on a thread of its own and shows it once more after an early answer, tested over the coordinator's hub; the dialog in the form system on both systems, the alert of AppKit on macOS and the task dialog on Windows, which a test target builds, reads back and answers by the system's own calls on the hosted runners alone, and never the plugin's message dialog; and the scripted dialog of the walk behind the feature scripted. No build wires a reader to the presenter yet.
- Changed: (capture run, control-2b) In halvo-control, `proto::delete_decision` answers busy to a delete with no id while the store reads opening and shows no id, where it accepted it (contracts.md C2.6, a first value of Pass 28: the open may yet show an id that the person never saw); the scripted daemon of the feature test-support answers the same. The status texts read each source line as off, runs, stopped or may run, from its state and its mark `unstopped` (ruling CT-9): beside the Windows keyboard line and beside a screen line with screen_activity off, `texts().recorded` gives no entry for a line that is stopped and a sentence that the source may still record for a line that failed, or that carries the mark beside stopped or off, where it said that the source runs; `texts().passwords` reads the platform, the guard and the typing source's line, gives a protecting sentence only where the guard is os on macOS or halvo on Windows, or no typing source is on (Windows), and otherwise a sentence that promises nothing, and `passwords` is no longer public; `Texts` gains `typed_ahead`, the sentence of ruling MA-12 H5 on a key pressed before a program or page has reacted, wherever `passwords` is the macOS secure-input sentence (rulings MA-11 (c)(ii) and MA-12 H5); `texts::onboarding_rows(&status)` gives a view the onboarding rows with the screen row's why taken from the same table.
- Added: (capture run, control-2b) In halvo-control, each source's entry of `DaemonStatus` carries the mark `unstopped` (`SourceLine::unstopped`, a required bool on the wire and not printed by ctl) for a source that a stop did not end, and the status type refuses capture paused, no_indicator or awaiting_resume beside a source with the mark (ruling PL-8 (3)(c), contracts.md C2.1 and C2.3); the scripted daemon takes a stop answer for each source (`testing::StopAnswer`), marks a source whose stop answers Timeout or the Os error, reads pausing beside it, and clears the mark when a later ask answers Ok or ThreadEnded; its Pause leaves a source that the person stopped (`reason=user`) as it is until the next Resume, where it read stopped with no reason. The client's error gains `ClientError::ReplyOfAnotherVersion { version }` for a reply of a protocol version other than 1 and `ClientError::ValueUnknownToV1(UnknownSpelling)` for a reply that holds a text outside a closed list of v1 (`ReplyError::UnknownValue` of `decode_reply`), so that a page can tell an older app from a broken reply, which stays `Protocol` (inbox entry 97 (f)); ctl prints the same line for each as before.
- Added: The command `learn-gate routine` and the check `scripts/r5/ci.d/55-r5-routine.sh` (learning run, U5.2, contracts.md L10.4 and L11.1): the command reads the 135 trains of `fixtures/r5/trains/sample.jsonl`, each line of L7.2's form byte for byte, and plays each twice on the memory store, day by day from 1 to 730 until its first day that differs: once with every occurrence as a summary, once with the occurrences from day 120 on as replay runs of the stub runner. It runs `LearnStep` every day, compares the newest routine row's mark and period with the train's events, and prints `routine trains=135 plays=270 same=<n> result=<ok|miss>`; a play that fails or panics is refused with the number of its own train. `--store derived` is refused with `store` while the derived store is not in the build, and the check prints `derived=not-run` for the build's own refusal line alone. Before its build the check refuses a tracked file that differs from HEAD, and a signal ends it with a FAIL line. The usage refusals of `learn-gate` name `routine` among the commands.
- Added: halvo-derived (workflow run, U5.1b): the keyed row read of a handler, `Rows::for_each_under`, which hands out the rows of one kind of its own family under one workflow id by the workflow column that the commit set, under the read rule of DS-4, the held-ahead call of a handler under a coordinator_write, `HandlerCx::held_ahead`, a yes or no whether a row of one kind of its family under one workflow id lies more than two days after the clock's day (S-59 (a)), and tests that a stored row which a later typed reader refuses keeps its workflow column and goes at its day and not before, that two requests of one family never interleave, that the summary read leaves out a summary ahead of the clock, that a handled request reads its workflow and its evidence by one reading of the clock, and that an evidence page holds the conditions of its support rows beside a page of the exact bound.
- Changed: the check 50-derived (workflow run, U5.1b) fails for a test that the halvo-derived test binaries run and its list does not name, ignored or not, with a failing fixture for each, and its list names every test of the crate.
- Changed: docs/privacy-limits.md (workflow run, U5.1b, ruling S-82) states a limit of the derived store: text of a row that it removed or replaced can stay in its encrypted file, with no time limit, until that space is reused, and the mend is the capture run's unit sqlcipher-1, Halvo's own build of the database that zeroes that space.
- Fixed: The test directories of halvo-schema (tests/export_names.rs, golden.rs, r4_golden.rs, r4_names.rs and r5_names.rs; workflows run U1.1i) and of tools/r2-probes (tests/text.rs) are named by the process id, one clock reading taken once for the test process and a counter, made with create_dir, so that a directory of that name that already exists fails the test at once with its path, and removed at drop, with one line on the process's standard error when the removal fails; a failed comparison of tests/r4_golden.rs keeps the file that its message names, and the probes test directory lies under the target directory instead of the system temporary directory.
- Added: A weekly run of CI on main, Sundays at 03:17 UTC (the `schedule` trigger of ci.yml; capture run, cache-1, rulings PL-2 (3), CA-1 and CA-3), runs both jobs with every step on main's newest commit without the Rust build cache: on that event the Rust cache step of each job takes the prefix key `v0-rust-cold`, so it finds no saved Rust build cache and saves none, and an artifact that only a restored Rust build cache holds cannot hide a broken build there; the npm cache of the setup-node step is still restored on that run. Pushes and pull requests keep the prefix key `v0-rust` and save on main alone; their key is unchanged, because after a change of Cargo.lock or of a manifest the step already restores main's newest cache of the job, and it rebuilds what changed and also each cached artifact that was built more than a week before, which the action removes at such a restore, so from a week after a dependency was first built such a job may rebuild most dependencies. The workflow lint 17-workflow-lint holds these lines: it refuses a ci.yml whose block on: does not hold the schedule with its one cron line as written and no other line under it, in which a job's Rust cache step is not exactly its four lines, which names the action on another line, or whose jobs and steps stand in another layout than the one that the lint reads; each refusal that found a line quotes it, and a refusal for a line that is missing names the line to write. No cold job has been measured yet: the first weekly run is the first measure of one against the job limit of 90 minutes.
- Fixed: The workflow lint 17-workflow-lint reads a job's own keys at four blanks alone and refuses a job whose keys stand at another indent, where before a line in the text of a block scalar, such as the text under a job's `name: |`, could stand in for the job's line `timeout-minutes:` and a job with another limit passed (capture run, cache-1, ruling CA-3 E15 (h)).
- Added: The scan for home paths (`scripts/check-home-paths.sh`, run by the pre-push hook and by `scripts/r1/ci.d/19-home-paths.sh`) also reads the message of each commit that it reads and of each annotated tag that a push carries, with the same forms, and the list of allowed paths does not apply to a message; `sh scripts/check-home-paths.sh --stale` names an entry of `scripts/home-path-allow.txt` that allows nothing in HEAD, and 19-home-paths runs it.
- Changed: The two checks of the pre-push hook take their commits from one function, `scripts/commit-range.sh`, which leaves out an exempt commit only when the default branch holds it (or when the clone knows no default branch), in the range mode of the scan for home paths too, and `.githooks/commit-msg` and `.githooks/pre-push` refuse to run when `scripts/check-home-paths.sh` or `scripts/home-path-allow.txt` is missing beside them; the entry of `scripts/home-path-allow.txt` for the made-up account `a` in `crates/halvo-control/src/service.rs`, which allowed nothing, is gone.
- Fixed: scripts/r1-gate/mutation-test.sh (capture run, mutants-3, ruling PL-10 (2)(d)) shields a gate check and the cargo build of the copy, unmutated or of a mutant, that run when a stop comes: such a step runs with TERM, INT and HUP ignored, the runner starts no next step and waits up to 60 s for it to end, then kills it with every process below it, and the STOPPED line names a step that the bound ended (`the running check-store did not end within 60 s and was killed`), so a check that the helper for timed runs ends with its TERM can end and remove what it made before the helper's KILL 5 s later, when the step ends within 5 s; from the first signal on the runner ignores TERM, INT and HUP, so a later signal no longer ends its own reads of the step and cuts the wait for a shielded step or a test short.
- Added: Check 55, metering (billing run, 5.3), reruns S9's command on the real code: the generated orderings and the search of every sequence of up to 4 faults on one interval drive the entries of src/do/entries.ts with the real accounts, ledger and metering modules over test-kit/sql/ against the fake meter, and the check also runs the seven tests of test/metering/ that its line names: an expiry due during a sync wake, stripe-mock's meter events, the two bounds of the sync, and the three that unit 5.4 brought (late-read, unexplained-total and retry-delay). Its line holds 13 tokens, the plan word, five counts and one for each of the seven tests: `PASS metering: plan=whole fake-checks=<n> of <n> orderings=<n> of <n> voided=<n> sequences=<n> settings=<n> of <n> expiry-on-time=passed stripe-mock-accepts=passed refusal-limit=passed send-limit=passed late-read=passed unexplained-total=passed retry-delay=passed`; a test that is skipped, or whose mock cannot start, fails it (ruling B-47), and so does a hold voided at its expiry that is counted or sent (ruling P7-50). Beyond S9 it gives a send a refusal, sends a cancel as two events with seals between them, follows a cancel with a newer active event, reads the stored remainder after every step of a cancelled or deleted account and before the rows are removed, and searches more settings for these paths (38 in all, as `node test-kit/fake-meter/rerun/check.mjs --plan` prints them, ruling P7-74); its port answers the whole minutes that hold the asked range, as the adapter does, and an interval recorded as not billable while the meter holds its event fails the line as a record that disagrees with the provider (ruling P7-77). The model accepts a meter event stamped at or after the subscription's end only before the contract's cut, the earliest cancel time that reached the account object before the seal of the interval it ends (ruling P7-94). The check fails when git cannot say whether a tracked file differs from HEAD, and when the search holds another count of settings than its plan; its self-test runs a changed copy of the product for each rule of the model, the model's own cases for the rules that no such copy reaches first, the plan of the line, and a count of the databases that one worker keeps after a fixed count of worlds, and it says when the two variables narrowed it and how many fixtures it ran (rulings P7-94 and P7-95). Every setting and the draw read one width, READ_WIDTH_SECONDS of the model, and a scenario that states another is refused; a cancelled account that keeps no cancel time, or that is metered and has no cut, fails the line; the count of a cut moved back by an earlier cancel time counts an unsealed interval with a hold, and the generated orderings hold the path (seed 5, ordering 29 under a sync that ignores the earlier time); the guard of tracked files also fails when git answers for a repository that does not track the check's own file; and the line begins plan=whole, or plan=bounded for the run inside check 90, which HALVO_METERING_PLAN=bounded asks for and which is refused unless OUTBOUND_RUN is set (ruling P7-97, F1 to F5, and ruling P7-98).
- Fixed: The metering sync (billing run, 5.3, ruling P7-59) stamps an interval's event with the larger of its start second and the second before its end, so that an interval which a delete or a cancel cuts in the second in which it starts is stamped with its start second, inside its own interval and billing period, and no longer one second before it; check 55 found the defect on the real code at its first run.
- Fixed: The metering sync (billing run, 5.4, ruling P7-77) lets a read made before an interval's late point, the time of its newest send without a definite answer plus the smaller of LATE_READ_SPAN_MS and the uniqueness window less the margin, only acknowledge the interval: a refusal that arrives while a send lacks a definite answer stays pending until a read at or after the late point, past the window of the first attempt no send follows an earlier read, and the delay between tries that change nothing doubles up to RETRY_DELAY_CAP_MS, so that a lost answer and a lagging read no longer close as not billable an interval that the provider holds, while a read's lag plus a call's travel stays under that span; above it a late read can still close such an interval or be followed by a resend that the provider counts again, as before; the close at SEND_LIMIT_MS stays
- Fixed: The accounts module (billing run, 5.5, ruling P7-80) keeps a cancelled subscription cancelled: an event that is not a cancel no longer changes the state, the subscription id, the period, the provider time or the cancel's times of a cancelled account row or kept event (one stamped later than the stored cancel counts in the mark of the Added line below), and a cancel event applies over every state that is not cancelled whatever the provider times, so that no usage is admitted after a cancel of its subscription reaches the account and its cut and write-off stand
- Added: The accounts module and the account object (billing run, 5.5, ruling P7-80, part 8) keep a mark on the account row and on each kept event, the count and newest provider time of the events by which a cancel decided otherwise than the rule of newer (cases M1 and M2), answer overrode on state, kept and none, and log one line cancel-overrides-order after the step of each such event; src/log.ts also names usage-total-unexplained for unit 5.7
- Changed: Check 90, outbound (billing run, 5.5, ruling P7-98), runs check 55 in its bounded plan inside the sandbox: scripts/outbound-hosts.sh sets HALVO_METERING_PLAN=bounded on the nested gate alone, and a nested line of check 55 that reads another plan cannot be counted
- Changed: The account object of the backend (billing run, 4.9) refuses HOLD_LIFE_MS above 30,000 ms (AFTER_CLIENT_GONE_MS) and SYNC_INTERVAL_SECONDS above 86,400 s where it reads its settings, with the line bad-variable and an Error named for the variable, as for a value that does not parse (contract 6), and keeps the host that its constructor made in a field that a test reads; a workerd test holds that after a reserve whose setAlarm the host refuses the dispatch step arms the alarm again at the open hold's expiry and the rejection changes no stored value, no alarm and no field of the instance; and test/contract/wire.node.test.ts, test/metering/stripe-meter-load.node.test.ts and test/ledger/model.node.test.ts each set a test limit of their own (18,000, 19,884 and 19,828 ms, four times the slowest passing time of a test that takes it in the readers' runs of script 21 at loads of 95 to 189), while the walk test of wire.node.test.ts keeps its 60,000 ms and the test of stripe-meter-load whose assertion is a time keeps its 15,000 ms.
- Fixed: The routine step of halvo-learn-store (learning run, U5.3) rests a workflow and agent while its newest routine row is dated one or two days after the clock, so a clock set back by a day or two no longer moves a routine's last day back or shows a stale line for a task just done; the step takes the newest routine row of a pair from one function (the highest rev, then the later day, then the row read last); and a routine or run row that cannot be read stops the step for the workflow that its `workflow` key names alone, or for every workflow where that key cannot be read.
- Added: The needs view keeps one element with the role status, aria-live polite and aria-atomic true, made at its first show and never rebuilt; each addition first takes out what the element holds and then adds all the sentences of one event: of a reply, the say_gone of each item that left, also one that held the focus, but never of a page confirmation that the person answered, then the say_new of each item that came while the focus is outside it, never of a page confirmation; a reply in which an item came or went empties the element also when it adds nothing, and a hide or a page confirmation that opens empties it; from the opening of a page confirmation until the release nothing is added: the view holds its sentences and adds them once, after the close of the last one is delivered and never in the task that closes it, with no timer, first the sentence that each typed line of a card in the list shows if it appeared or changed there during the hold, the same words once and in the order of the list, then the sentences of the items; the release is made at every such close, also when the view holds no items of a hold, as for a confirmation shown again after a failed answer, and then adds the typed lines' sentences alone or nothing; a reply whose only change is that page confirmations which the person answered have gone takes nothing out; the core gives every item but a confirmation the two sentences, say.item.new and say.item.gone with the agent's name, and a confirmation gets them when the composer of the snapshot names the agent with Item::said_by (U2.4b); the words check reads them, and a test holds the agents view's refusal of a click from a key begun on another control. Named limits: nobody has heard whether a sentence that is added after a close, or one that the next addition replaces within 250 ms, is spoken; while a confirmation stays open no other item is added; an item that changes under its id is added nowhere; the agents view's element keeps every sentence and adds while a confirmation is open until its own unit lands; the newest sentences stand until the next addition, a hide or a new load; whether a sentence that the release adds for a typed line is also read as the line's description when the focus returns to the line, so that it is read twice, has been heard by nobody, and the order with the typed lines' sentences first rests on a reading of aria-atomic and on no hearing (contracts.md I3.4, I9.2; rulings RD-29 (c), RD-73 6.4, RD-76 part 2, RD-93 part T4).
- Fixed: The typed line of a setup card no longer lets a later key in after a letter did not fit at the bound, nor pushes the last letter out when a letter is put into its middle: the line is at the bound when it holds 1,024 bytes, or when an input was refused since the last removal while the line held more than 1,020 bytes, and there it changes only by a removal; below the bound a refused input refuses only itself and the next input that fits is taken; a refused input leaves the line as it stood wherever the caret is, with the selection that the input found; the card shows one sentence at a time, named by aria-describedby and added once to the view's status element: param_typed.full at the bound, until a removal, and param_typed.too_long after a refusal below the bound, until an input that only removes or until the answer is sent, which an input that is taken does not take away. Named limits: an input of more than 4 bytes at once on a line of 1,020 bytes or fewer (a flag or an emoji from a picker, a word that an input method commits, a paste) is refused whole and sets no mark, so an input that fits is taken after it, with param_typed.too_long standing; on a line of 1,021 to 1,023 bytes after any refusal a letter that fits is refused until a removal (contracts.md I3.5; rulings RD-32 (b), RD-76 part 1).
- Changed: An answer of many letters given at once to the typed line, such as a paste, that would take it past 1,024 bytes is refused whole and the line stays as it stood, where the view cut it at a whole letter before; between a compositionstart and its compositionend the view changes nothing in the line, refuses nothing and shows no sentence, and at each compositionend the line as it then stands is one input, held against the line and the selection kept when that composition began, taken if it fits, also when its spelling passed the bound on the way, and else put back as a refusal; the send takes the line that the view kept and never the field; a typed line is unsettled while a composition is open in it and after any end of one, taken, refused or cancelled, until the page receives a pointerdown, a pointerup, a pointercancel, a keyup or a keydown that is no repeat, or until a click on its card is stopped; a press on a control of a card sends nothing when a typed line of that card was unsettled at the press's first event, a pointer press's pointerdown, which reads before it counts and is kept to the click, or a click with no press of its own, or is unsettled at its click; the stopped click ends the state that an end left, so the next press sends the kept line, and it puts the focus on the typed line that stopped it, with its text and selection unchanged, and adds nothing to the status element; each parameter of an item carries the keys full and too_long, the sentences of a typed one, and the words check reads them. Named limits: no input method was run in either engine; the rule is held in Chromium by the protocol's composition calls and in both engines by composition events that a script makes in each engine's order; in the Mac's own web view and with a real input method on either system it is held by no run; a stopped press shows no sentence of its own, and the focus goes to the line; a press is stopped once after a composition that ended with no pointer or key event after it, as a word chosen from the system's list with its pointer or a dictated word; a press that moves no focus leaves an open composition open, and every such press is stopped until it ends; if an engine delivers the end after the click that brought it, two presses are stopped; text of a composition that ended because the focus left the line is taken as the engine left it, and the next press sends it; two presses with no events of their own send at the second, however close; an event that reaches the page between an end and the first event of the press that brought it lets that press send; a press that began on a control and was let go off it stops nothing later; an end in one card leaves its line unsettled through a press on another card; that a pointer press reaches the page before the composition ends is shown in Chromium by the protocol alone; when a page confirmation opens over an open composition, Chromium by the protocol ends it, and one that does not fit is refused, while one that fits, the Mac's own web view and a real input method were run by nobody (contracts.md I3.5; rulings RD-76 part 4, RD-93 parts T2 and T3).
- Changed: In halvo-coord and the coordinator's schedule model (interface run, U2.3b, rulings RD-5, RD-35 and RD-38), every move that reads the hold `check` of an agent, the person's answer to a check among them, lapses the agent's open `ready` item with the new cause `check` and raises the item `check` in the same body, so that no state holds an open `ready` item beside an unanswered check, and the lapse stores no `slot_skipped`; `OtherLapse` gains `check` and the new names type `ItemLapseCause` holds the seven causes of an `item_lapsed` row; both trace files of formal/r4-coordinator are exported again under one stamp, and gate-record.txt is made again for it.
- Added: The web executor `halvo-web` (crates/halvo-web, U2.1 of the agent-setup run, contracts.md A11.1): the agent's browser started on its debugging pipe as a direct child of the executor, never on a port, with the whole environment and working directory of A6.1 (CP-160 (1)) and its standard streams on the null device (CP-156 (2)); every start of a child in the crate under one start lock, which the crate's clippy.toml holds against the starts of std and tokio, posix_spawn, posix_spawnp and fork of libc, the six CreateProcess functions of windows-sys and the driver's client types that connect by an address; a SOCKS5 relay that starts with an empty list, refuses a host outside it before any lookup, refuses a peer that is not at 127.0.0.1 or not one process of the browser's tree and records refusals apart, and under the system's names refuses a tunnel whose answer holds a loopback, link-local, unspecified, multicast, broadcast or private address, an IPv4 address inside a mapped, compatible, translated, 64:ff9b::/96 or 6to4 IPv6 one read as IPv4, with a record of its own by host, port and class, unless the caller marked the entry as a bound loopback literal; a guard that holds the relay's port until the browser's tree is gone and ends the run by CP-148 when the executor is gone, following members born later and orphans that bear the run's mark, with its grace counted from an unbroken absence of the executor, asking and ending only a member that bears the run's mark at that read, writing each member that it left into its record at the act, and keeping the port for such members at most 300 s after the last marked member is gone; on Windows a row with the executor's number, no creation time and the guard's own program name counts as the executor, and the guard refuses an executor creation time of 0; a failed start that ends the run's own processes, those that name its profile, began after the start and run a program inside the browser's bundle, and reports what it left, with no words for a person (CP-165); the operations of A11.1 that the web isolation trial held (open, snapshot with stale refs and no value for a credential field, resolve, click, fill, select, press, upload, read, the request rule, the session's export and import, the recorder, the stop), with the request rule armed at the start with no origin in the mode that fails and a mode that holds each state-changing request until the runner lets it through once by the executor's own id, after which every other is failed until the next arm, and with no read of a request's body; the policy line against WebSockets of A4.2 added to every document and script from the start in every mode, the executor's own storage page among them, a response that cannot carry it being failed, and only a redirect with a Location line, a 204 and a 304 passed on with no line; without the handback mode, the compile allowance and the list one hop at a time, which CP-167 (2) gives to the planner; the executor's own flags, among them `--breakpad-dump-location=<profile>/Crashpad` beside `--disable-breakpad`, so that a crash's dump stands in the profile and not in the crash place of the account's own browser; `web-probe`, which runs the nine steps of A13.2 and prints `probe ok=9 of=9`; and `web-rows` with the rows of first starts, a driver killed during the start, `relay_foreign` (another account on macOS and on Windows), the six rules of the start-to-stop check with a control, `block_frame_post` (a state-changing request that a page's frame sends is refused), `hold_release` (a held request let through once while a second one is failed) and `ws_allowed` (no WebSocket handshake from a document, a frame or a worker, against `rows/ws-listener.mjs`), and the crash row of CP-165 (2) on hosted runners alone, with one reading under the egress check of scripts/egress, whose cell reads notrun unless the control's dump stands at a place that is no report place of the system, the reading with the means shows its dump inside the profile and its crash handler is gone, and reads miss when the stop left a directory or the reading removed a file outside the profile that stood before the row; the listing reads only the places that the row names (the profile and its temporary directory, the account's crash places, the browsers' helper caches, the system's temporary directory and its report places), 12 levels deep below each, follows no link, and takes neither a removal nor a dump in the system's temporary directory as the row's, and an unread process table or unread arguments of the browser read as handler=unread, which is notrun; on macOS the start resolves the profile's path once, before its flags, so the crash handler's database names the profile in the form that the run's mark reads.
- Added: Item 3 of the browser profile trial (U0.3) runs the browser under scripts/egress/check.sh with the list scripts/r3/egress/fixtures.txt of A14.3, the web trial prints the browser on its kill lines, and tools/r3-trials/web/hosted/ tracks the hosted harness of the org_ cells, its policy-page reader and the collector, the harness refusing with one line before any privileged call unless GITHUB_ACTIONS, RUNNER_ENVIRONMENT and ImageOS show a GitHub-hosted runner (ruling CP-133 (c), (i)).
- Changed: Items 1 and 2 and the task shape of item 4 of the profile trial start the browser as the product does after U0.7c (the debugging pipe, the agent's settings, U0.7b's flags and the relay); item 3's browser runs the same start without the relay, and the steps shape of item 4 runs the web trial's start of U0.7c; docs/r3/trials/browser-profile.md holds its second round with the flag and pref lines, the trials' fixture server reads a pipe that its Server holds and is killed and waited for on drop, and every unsafe block of both trials carries a SAFETY note under a denied clippy lint.
- Fixed: The profile trial builds again against the web trial's library, whose StartOptions lost debug_port in U0.7c.
- Added: halvo-sample writes the key seed_category on each line of the script manifest and check-labels knows a seed-category step by it, with `FAIL seed` for a range of a seed episode whose step is marked false and `FAIL link` for a stream directory that is a symbolic link; the fixture-server reader keeps D12 as compose does, the compose pairs and the reader's fixtures stand in one list, tools/fixture-server/site-cases.jsonl, and 30-sample checks the modes of its inputs and builds the split scripts again from the rates file and the site files (U2.2b).
- Changed: leak names a file of a HIT line by the PATH argument and its number in the walk (`<path>#<n>`), refuses a PATH argument that is a symbolic link with `path` and a body whose manifest, day file or label file is a link with `body` or `tier-files`, and gives only the argument as the detail of those refusals; check-labels refuses a manifest, day file or label file that is a link; build refuses a script file of another suffix, with a dot name or that is no file with `script`, so 30-sample hands it every entry of a scripts directory, and build and split name a script file in a refusal by its place, `SCRIPT <n>`, never by its name (U2.2b).
- Fixed: check-labels names the later row of every overlapping pair of ranges, also where the farthest-reaching range lies between them, and build counts `seq` only for a record that it writes (U2.2b).
- Added: Set D of the seam's conformance suite, one batch-file text whose `at` steps back by 999 ms from a scroll pointer to the keystroke after it (C4.7), and reads of D at the end of the case `filters` (C5.13 items 3 and 8, U1.2e): the read with no filter, which requires the keystroke's `at` to lie exactly 999 ms below the pointer's, as a `load` that moves every `at` by a whole number of seconds keeps it (C5.13 item 2), and five ranged reads, each from the zero position and from the pointer's position: from D's first `at` to the pointer's `at`, the same with the kinds filter `keystroke`, to 1 ns after the keystroke's `at`, from the keystroke's `at`, and from 1 ns after it. A server fails `filters` that ends a ranged read at the first record whose `at` is at or after the end of the range, or after it by less than 999 ms, also only under a kinds filter of one name or only from a position other than zero, that ends it when the first line of a Batch has an `at` at or after the end, that drops a record whose `at` is below one that it served, or the store's first record when its `at` is the range's start, that finds a range's first line by a binary search over `at` or checks the start only until the first `at` at or after it, or that serves an entry of D at a wrong position or with a wrong record; so do a `load` that rounds the step away and one that breaks a line of D. The tests `conformance_filters_step_back` (23 wrong servers and three that pass) and `conformance_set_d_holds_its_step_back_when_served` hold the reads and the data, and 21-seam reads both by name.
- Changed: The matcher of crates/halvo-seam/tests/suite_controls.rs takes an expectation only when it stands in the failure and its text after its last `: ` is not blank and equals the failure's text after the failure's last `: `, so an expectation that names a place, a check or a part of the reason alone is refused; every expectation names its failure's whole last part, the six of IgnoreKinds, IgnoreRange, NextFalls and FilteredOpenServesFromTheStart that named only `check filtered_stops: ` or a filter among them, and the matcher's own test, which 21-seam reads by name, holds what it refuses (inbox entry 76 (k), U1.2e).
- Changed: scripts/r1-gate/mutation-test.sh --apply-only (capture run, mutants-4, ruling MS-1) compiles the unmutated copy and a row on a file that cargo builds with cargo check of the package and target of the build (lib.sh gate_type_check beside gate_build), keeps cargo build for a row on a file named build.rs or on a path with a part named halvo-sqlcipher, and prints `applies and type-checks` for a type-checked row; the full run still builds the mutant of every row that it starts and alone counts a row as caught. On one macos-15 runner the apply-only phase fell from 908 s to 302 s. scripts/r1/ci.d/30-mutants.sh prints the seconds of each phase and how many rows the budget run ran, less those whose line says that the mutant does not build, and its rule line, the runner usage, scripts/r1-gate/README.md and docs/verify/store-shred.md say what each run proves: a pull request and a push type-check a cargo mutant, a push to main builds and runs the rows within its budget, and the whole list is built and run against its verdicts only in a full run on the measuring Mac.
