- Added: Script 21 of the macos job (billing run, 4.11) runs check 50's records after the backend's tests from the commit's objects: it notes the commit before the tests, reads the test list from it after them, exports backend/ at the commit into a directory under TMPDIR and runs records.mjs there, so a commit that breaks a record fails the job; it requires each record of the commit (good-<name>.json or bad-<name>.json directly in the records directory) to have its own line as expected once, by name, with no problem line, no other line and the summary once as the last line, and it refuses a tracked file that differs from the commit or carries an index flag before the node tests, after them and after the records' run; by ruling P7-101 and its Addition 1 it trusts backend/node_modules, the tools on the PATH, code that a node test starts or arranges to run after the tests end (it can write the export, the lines under the artifacts and backend/node_modules), the clone's .git, the index against a change that is staged and put back, and records.mjs as the commit holds it.
- Changed: The model trial of the agent-setup run (U0.5, taken over by U0.6 under ruling CP-77): fits.py refuses a cell line whose file_sha256 is not its model's weights line and a run number that stands twice, with fixtures for both and for three changed copies that its tests let pass; fetch.sh takes uname and the cmake path into variables, so the strict shellcheck pass prints nothing for it; the README says that a bound read again can stand one 8 MiB step below the file's, and model-cells.md says that one run of ten answered 2 tokens where the others answered 29.
- Added: The driver trial of the agent-setup run (U0.6, contracts.md A13.6): tools/r3-trials/drive/run.sh runs one cell, a compile run in which a small model drives the fixture's bill or order pages through the cdp library under the cap of halvo-cap, three replays of its playbook with no model and a recovery run on changed_page_2, or one write run of the reply bodies, and prints the attempt, drive and reply lines; verdict.py applies the rule of A13.6 to the lines of docs/r3/trials/driver.md; the prompts of A9.5 and docs/r3/model-settings.toml with the starting values of A12.5; docs/r3/trials/driver.md with the cells of mac and hosted-win and the verdict driver=none path=B writer=none, which by ruling CP-179 stands as far as its three mended rules go, because at the mended commit each model's first cell missed by the cap of 32 asks; control.sh with a stand-in child that walks the tasks with no model, and rebuild.py, which makes the block again from the raw files; by ruling CP-177 the harness hands back by its own reading of a credential field before it asks the model; by ruling CP-179 no reply ends a compile for being given before, a run is bounded at 2,000 s, one state-changing request leaves per run, the harness's words of a request stand in src/words.rs, a reply's body is its text after the model's thinking, and the fixture tools/fixture-server/variants gains the variant slow_charge for the control.
- Fixed: The account backend's stand-in under account/stub/ (billing run, 7.5b): a request head whose lines end in a bare LF is answered and recorded; a raw file or a body file that cannot be opened, EMFILE among the causes, ends that one connection with the line raw-open or body-open, and the process goes on, because requests.jsonl and the raw file of each open connection stay open; for a client that pipelines and does not read, the stand-in reads no further request once the answers it holds reach the socket's write buffer mark, and reads on when the system has taken the held answers into its buffers; a start that is refused for the authority's file writes no requests.jsonl into the record directory; a request that is still open when the stand-in stops gets the line incomplete; each record directory that a test of the stand-in makes is removed when its test file ends, and 20-r7-stub.sh fails when the tests leave one in their temp directory and removes that directory at its end; a connection that the stand-in ends, also at the stop, is closed in stages, so that a client that reads late inside the idle limit still gets its answers, and a close with answers unwritten, past BODY_CAP, or at a stop while the peer has not ended its side writes the line forced-close (rulings P7-64 and P7-70); from the begin of its stop the stand-in accepts no new connection and writes no 100 Continue, and a second SIGINT or SIGTERM joins the stop that runs instead of ending the process (ruling P7-103).
- Added: The account backend's stand-in under account/stub/ (billing run, 7.5b) answers an HTTP/1.1 request that expects 100-continue and announces a body with 100 Continue before it reads the body, as contract 1.4 asks.
- Changed: Give each CI job a 120-minute timeout in place of 90 minutes (capture run, jobtime-9, ruling PR-31 item 1), because the macos job reckons about 5,340 s on a normal runner after landing 43, and 5,700 to 5,780 s by projection on landing 41's slow attempt, against the old limit of 5,400 s, with the bound ci_job_timeout_min, the pin of check-cost.sh, ADR 0011 and scripts/ci/README.md.
- Changed: halvo-schema takes a coord_log body of up to 20 records and 16,384 bytes (interface run, U5.1c, ruling RD-73 7.1 and 7.2), and the store of halvo-coord-store takes such a body, the rows of a confirmation of up to 20 effects; halvo-coord's limits stay 8 records and 4,096 bytes until U2.4f raises them, the schema's bounds are never below them, and a golden file holds the largest body of a confirmation.
- Added: The record coord_event of halvo-schema takes the cause sign_in_refused in hold_set and hold_cleared, each naming the run whose refused sign-in it answers (interface run, U5.1c, ruling RD-73 4.2 and 4.5), and the cause answer_lost of the lapse of a confirmation (ruling RD-84 item 5), and the store of halvo-coord-store stores and reads such rows, before any unit writes them; its typed reader also takes a row with the nullable keys defer_launch and defer_ticks_failed (ruling RD-86 item 3) in any place and, null, on every event, set on run_ended alone, while the writer writes them after origin on run_ended; the store commits such a row only where both are null until the registry's entry lists them.
- Fixed: The wall between backend/ and the app (scripts/r7/ci.d/24-r7-wall.sh, billing run, F.1c) keeps the exception of account/schema/ for the four files of the wire contract also in its reading from the top, so account/schema/vendored.txt and account/schema/vendor.sh may name backend/contract/schema/v1.json and the other three files by their paths under backend/, as contract 8 has them do; the same line in another file of the app stays refused.
- Changed: The wall between backend/ and the app (scripts/r7/ci.d/24-r7-wall.sh, billing run, F.1c) refuses, in its reading from the top in backend/, a tracked directory of the app spelled in full with at least one slash, as a listing of an app directory whose files are then read or a cp -R of a crate directory after cd .. && cd .., and a glob whose part before its first glob character is a tracked directory of the app; and it refuses, on either side, a line that it reads (one that git grep hands on) and that holds a run of more than 64 strings joined by a comma, ").join(" or "/", whose chains it would read in time that grows with the square of the strings, while a line of many strings in shorter runs is read whole. It names in --help four more limits, each held by a passing fixture, a part of a path held in a variable, text that is no code moving the tracking of a case block, an unquoted glob right after a closing quote and a backend glob whose first part is a glob, and two readings that refuse an honest arm, a one-line case with a glob pattern and an esac in a string, so that it states the 15 named limits of --help: names-v0, computed-path, bare-module, file-or-environment, nul-byte, model-side, absolute-path, both-sides-path, unread-variable, bare-operand, bracket-glob, name-in-a-variable, case-in-text, quote-before-glob, backend-glob.
- Added: The interface run's crates halvo-coord, halvo-coord-app and halvo-coord-store hold a clippy.toml that disallows Command::new, spawn, output and status of std::process (interface run, U4.3, rulings RD-67 2.3 and RD-105), with an expect of the lint in the two tests of words_same.rs that start coord-words; check 20-r4-core runs clippy with that lint forbidden over the library, binary and example targets of the three crates with all their features in both jobs, and in the windows job refuses a cfg name outside its closed list, a dollar sign in a cfg predicate, a rustc-cfg line, another or a changed clippy configuration, a package inside the three, a tracked cargo configuration and a flags variable that lowers the lint, fails on tauri resolved with unstable or test in a normal build (RD-70 E12), on a start of a child in the named spellings that it reads in the crates that the app links and in the three crates but the two named limits, each as its committed statement, and on a tracked file that its test run changed.
- Added: The store's delete tail `EncryptedStore::delete_store_dir(store_dir, roots)`, the `DeleteReport` of delete-all with its count `not_followed`, the variant `StoreError::Obstructed` with its closed lists of store names and of what stood there, `KeyFile::at(path)` and a key-file label in the custody's wrap, `create.pending`, the kill table of the store with its interposer store/testdata/crashpoint.c, and scripts/r1/ci.d/31-store-full-volume.sh, which fills a disk image and runs delete-all in three scenarios (store-3).
- Changed: Delete-all runs the root step first through one opened handle of the roots directory, destroying every root file there only when that directory holds the store's own root, and its report reads the root as destroyed, left or unnamed, with Ok never for a root left; the delete tail, with its lock held, with nothing at the store name or after it removed a link there, destroys every root file and partial root file under the roots directory that it is given, named or not, and reads destroyed only a root that its key file or a made pending mark names; a later delete-all on a handle whose root read destroyed opens no roots path again; the tail runs no sweep on a roots path that holds a parent part or a link below the directory that it shares with the store path; delete-all seals the handle and removes the key file, the database files and the segments through the store's handle, every step attempted and no link followed; the macOS custody of a build without the feature secure-enclave is a root file under the roots directory that the caller passes; a root and store.key are published by a hard link that replaces nothing; the key file is version 2 with a namespace; the writer lock on macOS is a flock on the store directory; on macOS SQLite opens the database by the volume name of the store's directory, and the store looks through its handle once SQLite holds its files; open and create answer a code of SQLite by one table after a look; no error text holds a path or a segment id; `--verify-store` prints prompt=none (store-3).
- Removed: The login-keychain code of the development custody, the lock file writer.lock on macOS with its retry, and PROMPT_BOUND_MS (store-3).
- Fixed: A store.key or a root file of mode 0400 is removed by delete-all, two creates on one directory or under one root id no longer both succeed, and a create killed after its root and its pending mark that reads made leaves a root that the next create, open or delete tail destroys, while a mark that reads owed destroys nothing; a partial root file, store.key.tmp, a root file whose name stands in upper case, a root file whose zero-fill fails under a second name and a directory that cannot be removed are counted as left, and the tail on a store directory that it cannot open runs no root step and reports the root left and the roots not listed, and the tail whose lock fails for a reason other than a holder destroys the named root alone; on a handle that delete-all finished every call but delete-all answers Sealed, size_bytes among them, and delete-all answers its kept report and touches nothing; a key file version past 32 bits answers NewerStore or OlderStore, and on Windows the tail on a store path with a part that ends in a dot or a space removes nothing there and answers DeleteIncomplete; the root step sets a root file that it cannot open to mode 0600 by its name before it opens it, and every later tail names a root file that no open reaches; the delete removes a second name at store.key.tmp without writing through it and counts an entry there that stays; the root reads destroyed only when the call removed, at the root's own name, a regular file that it met at its own look and never one that only its listing saw that is not empty and, off Windows, of the root file's length, and any other entry there is removed with no write through it; on Windows a create or an open at a store path or with a roots path of which a part ends in a dot or a space answers Io(InvalidInput) in place of acting on the trimmed sibling, and the tail builds the verbatim form of such a path from its parts; the root step takes as its witness only a file at the root's own name that can be the root's file, and removes a second name at a root name that cannot be a root file without writing through it (store-3).
- Fixed: the self-test cases store/hup-ends-the-verify-child, cost/hup-ends-the-child and cost/hup-ends-the-child-under-dash, and cost/term-ends-the-child and cost/term-ends-the-child-under-dash, which share their function, of scripts/r1-gate (capture run, gate-2b, rulings MS-2 (d) and MS-3) start their check with HUP and TERM at their defaults and unblocked, so a caller that ignores or blocks HUP or TERM (as nohup ignores HUP) no longer makes them fail, wait on their child's events, with a guard of 120 s against a stall: they send their signal once the check's child runs and the check watches it, and read the check's end by its exit, with no count of polls; the store case finds its store directory after --store-dir, and a path in a pgrep pattern is quoted; they fail when a process of the check is left, when the child ran to its own end, or on another exit than that of the trap.
- Added: the self-test cases store/hup-waits-for-a-slow-verify-child and cost/hup-waits-for-a-slow-child of scripts/r1-gate (capture run, gate-2b, ruling MS-3): a check that is sent HUP while its child, load-standin in its deaf mode, ignores TERM ends only after that child has ended.
- Added: The ci.d driver (capture run, driver-1) fails the run when a script of the step `ci.d` or the step `ci.d self-test` as a whole changes a tracked file, moves HEAD or changes the git settings of the checkout, and names the script or the step; it ends what a script leaves of its process group; it ends a script of either step that runs longer than CI_D_SCRIPT_LIMIT_S seconds (1200 by default) with its whole process group, gives each script a TMPDIR of its own and removes it after the script, refuses a line of scripts/ci/self-test-alone.txt that names a script without a reason, and its own self-test holds a case for each rule that scripts/ci/README.md states of the driver, but for seven that are held by reading: a process in a group of its own at the time limit and at a signal, the sum of the waits at the end of the step `ci.d self-test`, the `one of` form of a change of the git settings, that the lane line does not touch the step `ci.d self-test`, that a script of the first part never finds the test list that 12-test writes in the same run, the conditions of the free lane, and when a script names the alone part. A ci.d script names the free lane of the step `ci.d` by the line `# lane: free`, or the alone part by `# lane: alone`, among its first ten lines, and the driver refuses any other `# lane:` line; `CI_D_CHECK_JOBS` bounds the free lane; fourteen checks whose verdicts rest on a reading of this machine, on tests with deadlines, or on a setting of the whole machine name the alone part; and the step Processor of each job of the workflow prints the runner's processor model and count before the step `ci.d` (capture run, jobtime-5).
- Changed: The step `ci.d` starts each script through perl in a process group of its own with INT and QUIT at their defaults, as the step `ci.d self-test` does; INT, TERM and HUP to the driver end the running scripts in both steps; a blank line and a line whose first word starts with `#` in scripts/ci/self-test-alone.txt are comments; the driver removes its own temporary directory when it exits (capture run, driver-1). The step `ci.d` runs its scripts in two parts: first the scripts that name no lane one after the other and the free scripts beside them, then the alone scripts one after the other with nothing beside them; it prints each script's output, standard error and `seconds` line when the script and every script before it have ended, and a tracked file that changes while scripts run beside each other names them all; the README of scripts/ci states what the rule on tracked files does not hold, and an own case holds each rule that it states of the driver, but for the seven held by reading that driver-1's line names (capture run, jobtime-5).
- Fixed: The time limit of the step `ci.d self-test` ends the self-test's whole process group, not its shell alone, and a scripts/ci/self-test-alone.txt that is a directory fails the step instead of reading as an empty list (capture run, driver-1).
- Added: The move `not_made` in the model of the confirm policy (agent-setup run, U1.8 (a), contracts.md A5.2, A5.3 and A5.6, rulings CP-135 (4), CP-139 (c), CP-144 and CP-194): the step relation takes it from `releasing` alone, with the cause `not_sent`, `not_replaced`, `unauthorized` or `throttled` where the cause can be true for the class, to the final state `not_made`; in the system the runner alone makes it, while the run goes on, and P4 and P11 hold that no move leaves `not_made`, that its cause is true (no request left, or the reply answered that the `replace` was not made, 401 or 429) and, from what the run's end records, that no move of a run's end makes it; P9 holds that an effect is committed only after a reply that its maker reads as made; formal/r3-confirm/traces.jsonl holds one directed trace for each cause and one in which the moves of a run's end never make it; the test `formal_traces` of halvo-policy checks those moves against the row of A5.2, and `step_traces` gives `effect_step` no `not_made` move and no move after an accepted one, until U1.8 (b) gives `effect_step` the move.
- Fixed: route-gate import refuses with `source` and its line number a line of a block whose first bytes, after spaces and tabs alone and compared without ASCII letter case, are a known word of that block (`attempt` and `verdict` in the driver block, `bound` and `verdict` in the cells block) and that does not start with that word at its first byte, and a line of the driver block whose words, divided by spaces and tabs, have `limits` first and later `task=bill` or `task=order` beside a verdict with no driver, or `task=reply` beside a verdict with no writer, each compared without ASCII letter case; still passed over are a line hidden by a byte other than a space or a tab or by a letter outside ASCII, a `limits` line in another form, and a value typed by hand (routing run, U0.2b, contract T5.4, ruling SG-63).
- Added: `LINES_HELD_MAX` of halvo-learn-store, 4,096: the most `step_ok`, `step_failed` and `recovered` lines that the learning handlers hold for one run. A `log` body that would take a run past it drops what they hold of the run, which then reads as a lost run, and a line that they hold already does not count again (contracts.md L11.2, L4.2; ruling LM-51). `halvo_learn_store::newest_routine`, the one function for the newest `learn_routine` row of a workflow and agent, public with its signature unchanged for every reader of the rows (contracts.md L1.1, L4.5; ruling LM-79, item 2; U2.5).
- Fixed: The learning handlers of halvo-learn-store: a row that its parser refuses stops, in the workflow that its workflow key names, or in every workflow where that key cannot be read, what rests on its kind and nothing else, through one result of each kind's read, and a body that the agent store answered gets the agent store's reply whatever the learning part could read or store; an error of the agent store at the handlers' own `load` of a run is still answered with that error, but at `end_run`, where the agent store's reply is returned; `list_refinements` alone answers `store` (contracts.md L4.2, L2.2; ruling LM-58 (a), (b), (d)). An accepted `start_run` stores the pair's `learn_routine` rev 1 where a read that succeeded holds no row of it, and its newest row again where that row is at least 30 days old and holds the mark `none`, so a routine whose row a stop behind `create_agent`, a refused commit or the bound left missing is watched again (contracts.md L4.3; rulings S-69 and LM-57). A `start_run` that arrives again for a lost run whose start this value still holds stores nothing, and one that arrives at `RUNS_HELD_MAX` for a run that it holds drops no other run (contracts.md L4.3, L4.6; ruling LM-57 (e), (i)). A step whose `load_steps` replies to a run carried different edits gets no clean use in that run, so an edit never becomes active on a use that is in doubt (contracts.md L3.7; ruling LM-60). An `end_run` that the agent store accepts for a run with facts makes the commit of a failed `finish`, which reverts a provisional edit whose step logged a counted failure, so, unless that commit is left out or refused, or the handlers' own `load` before it is refused (the run then reads as a lost run), a provisional target of the newest compile number whose step logged a counted failure before the person stopped the run is not served again (contracts.md L4.3; ruling LM-61). `list_refinements` takes the pair's newest routine row by the one rule of `rows.rs` that the step uses, so of two rows of one rev it shows the mark of the row of the later day (contracts.md L1.1; ruling LM-56 (f); U2.5).
- Added: `learn-gate privacy --data-dir DIR` (learning run, U3.1, contracts.md L11.4) prints six lines `privacy check=<name> result=<ok|miss|not-run>`, and the check scripts/r5/ci.d/60-r5-privacy.sh runs it with a temporary directory of its own beside the two delete commands, in both jobs, and prints `privacy checks=6 ok=<n> miss=<n> not-run=<n>` directly before its last line whenever the privacy output is six lines of their form, and not on a refused command, on another number of lines or on a failure before the play. In this pull request `learn_deleted` checks the delete of the gate's own test store in memory (its own cascade), not the app's delete, and reads no file: after that cascade no learned row names the first seeded workflow by its kind's workflow key, holds its id as a JSON string or holds its marker, and the second workflow's rows are the same rows, by kind and bytes, with none added. `learn_outside` searches only the directory that the command is given, to which nothing of the command writes in this build, and, in the process's temporary directory, each entry whose name was not at its top before the seed, at every depth, and nothing else on the computer; the search looks for the markers' bytes in names, in regular files and in the target texts of links, follows no link below those places, reads no extended attribute (on Windows, no alternate data stream) and no other encoding, and counts an entry that cannot be read as holding a marker. `learn_listed`, `learn_scrub`, `learn_delete_all` and `learn_expired` read not-run until the derived store and the capture run's delete-all tool are built.
- Changed: the two usage refusals of learn-gate name privacy among the commands (`commands: drift, delete, routine, privacy`), and the crate doc of learn-gate says that privacy runs and that soak, model and the product link of drift answer the refusal usage (learning run, U3.1).
- Changed: The test of crates/halvo-schema/tests/golden.rs that held tests/golden/names.txt free of the line role, now the_committed_names_file_holds_none_of_the_five_gateway_keys, holds the file free of all five gateway keys of contracts.md C1.10 (effort, max_output_tokens, messages, role and content) through a helper that takes the file's path, and a_copy_of_the_names_file_with_one_gateway_key_added_fails_the_check_for_that_key runs that helper on a scratch copy of the file with each key added and requires the failure each time, and both stand on scripts/r2/20-schema-tests.txt, so that 20-schema fails when either is renamed, ignored or left unbuilt; tests/later_kinds.rs says in one line that its entry AGENT_RUN, its example row and its row of OTHER_STATES stay frozen on the form of the agent-setup run's record agent_run from before that run's U1.7, whose form is that run's (contracts.md C3.7; workflows run U1.1j).
- Changed: scripts/egress/check.sh (workflow run, U0.1b, contracts.md C26.4) starts the search for a free run directory at one more than the highest number of a directory already under target/egress, read with a shell pattern, in place of trying each number from 1 with its own mkdir; a name that is no number, a name with a leading zero, a number of 9000000000000000000 or more (one case arm reads it; sh arithmetic wraps only above 9223372036854775807) and a file are passed over, the run still claims its directory with mkdir and takes the next number where a run beside it took that one, and a path at that number that is no directory is still refused. On a hosted windows runner whose restored build cache held about 170 run directories, the search from 1 cost about 4 s of each run of the check (CI-seconds run, attempt a01). The part search of scripts/egress/test.sh, which the sandbox and wfp-rules parts also run, holds twelve cases in a copied root, each with the run number that it takes and the numbers that it tries.
- Changed: (capture run, control-2c) In halvo-control, the status type refuses capture running beside a source set with no required source, and the scripted daemon of the feature test-support reads such a set as faulted with capture_reason no_source after a claim and after a Resume, where both read running (ruling PL-18, contracts.md C2.3); `proto::delete_decision` takes the daemon's fact `Life` and the capture value and answers busy whenever capture reads pausing, and `DeleteDecision::Ending` in a life that is ending, before the store and the ids are read, where it accepted such a delete (ruling PL-17 (b), the review's case told-04); the scripted daemon answers by `proto::admission` and a flag of its own, `set_life`: in a life that is ending a Pause persists desired=paused and answers the status as it stands, a Resume answers the status as it stands and writes nothing, a claim starts no source, and the Stop request and every other mutation answer the busy of an ending life, behind the unsupported_op of the other permission form and behind the busy of a mutation in flight, which the Pause, the Resume, the Stop and every other mutation answer before the life is read, while the claim and the two reads are served (ruling PL-17 (c) to (e), the review's cases mark-01 and mark-02); the password sentence of the guard halvo on Windows gains the team's default words of ruling W3-4 (e) on the mouse events that the registration hands the keyboard source, after the sentence on UI Automation (contracts.md C10.6); `DataDir::is_provisioned` returns `Result<bool, EntryAtDataDir>`: a link at the data directory's name whose target is away answers `LinkLeadsNowhere` and an entry there that is no directory `NotADirectory`, where both read as not provisioned, a first run; the read and the take of the instance lock give a link whose target is away an error that carries `LinkLeadsNowhere`, where the read answered `NoDirectory` and ctl said not running, and an entry that is no directory an error that carries `NotADirectory` (ruling ST-36 (e)); SECURITY.md names a link to a directory at the data directory's path (ruling ST-36 (l)); the mark `ending` of `ErrorReply` is a private field that only `ErrorReply::busy_of_an_ending_life` sets, read by `ErrorReply::ending()`, with `ErrorReply::new(code, message)` for every other error reply, and its `code` is private too, read by `ErrorReply::code()`, so that no value holds the mark beside another code (ruling CT-14 K2); the scripted daemon's `set_life(Life::Ending)` makes its capture read pausing with no reason, for the status held and for every status set or changed after it, in either order of `set_life` and `set_status`, and `set_life(Life::Going)` changes no status (contracts.md C2.3; the review of ruling PL-17; ruling CT-14 K3); the scripted Pause writes desired=paused also while capture reads pausing, where it wrote nothing then (ruling CT-13 (c), PL-17 (g)); the texts of `EntryAtDataDir` read "the data directory's path meets a link that leads nowhere" and "the data directory's path meets an entry that is no directory", which hold for the directory's own name and for the nearest name that stands above a missing directory (ruling CT-13); a first run is answered only for an absence: a stat of the state file that is refused, a folder on the path that cannot be read and a link whose target may not be reached for permission answer the third cause `EntryAtDataDir::CannotBeRead`, "the data directory or a folder on its path cannot be read", and an open of the lock that is refused for permission carries it with its kind kept, where each read as not provisioned, a first run (ruling CT-14 K4); `DataDir::is_provisioned` reads the state file's name without following a link: no entry there is a first run, any entry there (a link that leads nowhere among them) is Ok(true), and any other refused read is `CannotBeRead`, where a link that led nowhere read as a first run (ruling CT-15 G2).
- Added: (capture run, control-2c) In halvo-control, `proto::Life`, `proto::admission` with `proto::Admission`, the mark `ending` of `ErrorReply` (on the wire only when true, refused beside a code other than busy) with `ErrorReply::busy_of_an_ending_life`, the client error `ClientError::ServiceEnding`, and ctl's exit 75 for that busy with a line on standard error that names what the verb did not do (`ctl_text::ending_line`) (ruling PL-17 (a), (h); contracts.md C2.1 and C2.5); the client error `ClientError::AlreadyDeletedToThisRequest(AlreadyDeleted)` for an already_deleted that answers no request of the call (to another verb, to a delete with no id, or naming the armed id), where the client gave `UnexpectedReply` with the kind string; ctl prints the same line as before (inbox entry 111 (d)); `paths::check_data_dir` with `DataDirCheck` and `DataDirFault` (macOS): on the instance lock's own descriptor, or on one of its own when no lock is held, the data directory's owner must be this user, a directory at its own name whose permission bits are not 0700 is set to 0700 (`DataDirCheck::Own { set_to_0700 }`), a directory reached through a link at its last name is never narrowed and fails when its mode gives group or others access, a parent that is a link is no fault, and `DataDirCheck::resolved_line` gives the line `data_dir_resolved=<folder>` read from the descriptor for a linked directory, as one line in which each control character and U+2028 and U+2029 read "?" (ruling ST-36 (b), (c), (f); ruling CT-14 K5); `paths::EntryAtDataDir` and `paths::entry_cause` (ruling ST-36 (e)); the open of the instance lock brings its path to the whole form of a `DataDir` first, by the function that `DataDir` itself uses, and refuses a relative path or a part `..` with an error of kind InvalidInput (ruling CT-14 K1), does not follow a link at the data directory's last name and follows it only on a second open, which records that the directory was reached through a link, so that the check reads no name after the take; a missing name below a parent link whose target is away also answers `LinkLeadsNowhere` (the review of ruling ST-36); `DataDirFault::OwnOnAVolumeThatIgnoresOwnership` and `DataDirFault::LinkedToAVolumeThatIgnoresOwnership` for a data directory on a volume whose flags (fstatfs on the descriptor) carry MNT_IGNORE_OWNERSHIP, refused in both branches before the owner is read and before any change, and `DataDirFault::ModeDidNotTake` when the mode that the check set to 0700 reads otherwise afterwards (ruling CT-15 G1).
- Fixed: (capture run, control-2c) In halvo-control, ctl prints `halvo-capture: the roots of this custody cannot be listed; a root may survive` on standard error for a deleted reply with root_destroyed=unknown, with exit 0, where it printed no line (contracts.md C2.6; ruling CT-12 N6); the scripted daemon's Pause keeps a marked source line that reads off as off, with the mark as its stop answer leaves it, where it made the line stopped and the Pause answered internal; a test holds that the status type takes the three kinds of status of ruling CT-11 and that the client and the texts answer them.
- Fixed: Check 50 (backend/stress/) runs the first-burst wait of a request, and with it slow and burst-p50-at-200, from the request's first send, the least send time that its record holds; it reads a last answer of 500 as the dev proxy's only when its body is at most 4,096 characters, holds no gateway marker, and is the proxy's error line followed by frames that each name a file of the miniflare package by a file URL with no . or .. segment, plain or percent-encoded, and nothing after the frame's closing parenthesis, while a 500 that the driver sends again is read as before, by its gateway marker alone; the driver keeps 4,097 characters of an answer that is not a stream; a wave run that held its wave in no attempt fails with a sentence that gives the state of the first hold that ended before the last reserve of the wave was answered and by how much, or that no reserve was answered; and the rig's sentence gives the count of the rollover cell's runs that had counted when the draw was needed. Its records hold, for each status whose body a reading parses (400, 409, 413, 500 with JSON or with the proxy's text, and 502), a body cut by the kill and one cut outside it; gate/fixtures/concurrency/records.mjs refuses a record whose expect holds a key that its kind does not read, an attempts run with such a key or with no key, an attempts expect with no key, an empty list under cases, chunks, cells, attempts, attemptsByRun, attemptsByCell or expect.runs, a chunk list of empty chunks, a main-guard argv1 that the guard's table lacks, a blank expected text and a key written twice in one object, each with a fixture under gate/fixtures/concurrency/refused/ that names its cause; and scripts/r7/ci.d/21-r7-backend-test.sh reads the refused pass's lines (unit 4.10).
- Added: The coordinator's model Confirm4 holds the second step of a sign-in, with the run's request `Bind`, a press of Allow and of Don't allow, a store that loses its reply, the three answers of `Runs::answer_bind`, the Stop, the wait limit, the quit and the kill, and states L11: the runner receives `allow` only inside a press of Allow on that step while the newest row tried for it is an acknowledged `allow`; Schedule holds a switch-off whose `hold_set` lost its acknowledgement before a start (ruling RD-52 (a)); both trace files are exported again and `formal_traces` replays the new lines through the items drive and an owner that loses its reply, and a kill line through `recover_step` alone; L11 counts every Bind from the move, reads every `answer_bind` of every move and no word of the coordinator's answer, and keys a stored row by the item that it names, and Confirm4 has its own bound `Confirm4-bound.cfg` (three requests in all), from which the confirm walks are drawn (U1.2c).
- Changed: scripts/r4/ci.d/30-r4-formal.sh holds every Confirm4 configuration at eleven invariants, L1 to L11, prints `invariants=11` for it, its long self-test refuses a Confirm4.cfg without L11, it refuses every line of a configuration file that is not `CONSTANT` with a constant of the model and a value, `INIT Init`, `NEXT Next`, `INVARIANT` with a name, or in Schedule `CONSTRAINT Bound` and `VIEW View`, and it breaks three control copies, the third L11 with a coordinator that sends `allow` after a lost row (U1.2c).
- Added: halvo-coord gives each item a step mark (I3.4, RD-87), which a setup item raises at each refusal of its body by name; `agents_answer` and `agents_setup_typed` carry it and an answer of a step that is no longer current is refused with `state`; the command `agents_shown` takes the page's report that it opened a page confirmation, and an answer arms in its own move `arm_ms` after that report (RD-80 part 5) (U2.4b).
- Changed: halvo-coord's switch-off stores its hold first and stops nothing until the hold is known stored, by its acknowledgement or by one read, and where a move's read first shows a stored switch-off beside a live run, an answer to a handback item stops that run first and the run gets no answer, an answer to a second step stops it first and sends no allow, and an answer to a confirmation, on the page or native, goes to the run first and the rest of the switch-off follows in the same move, a named limit (RD-52 (b)); the items drive's lapses at the person's Stop, at the switch-off and at the wait limits hold the board's stop before the cancel (RD-48 (a)); a decline after a stored done that the run did not take stores its own row for stuck and a sign-in's first step (RD-61 (d)); the row of `agents_answer` lists `hold`, `budget` and `queue_full` and that of `agents_setup_typed` `no_service`; a confirm request with more effects than `request_records` ends its run with `kind` (RD-73 7.4); a confirmation's lapse that stops its run holds the stop, and its row where its clock read answers, in memory, then cancels, then sends its decision only after a cancel that answered success or `ended`, and stores its row last, and a handback's time-out and a second step's lapse hold the stop in the guard's table first (RD-83); the switch-off takes the agent's queued start out of the queue (RD-52 (d)); a tick raises `ready` until the agent has a clean run, one that ended `completed` or `nothing_to_do`, and a skip with no slot holds until a later slot opens (RD-67 part 5, RD-73 1.2); the schedule and confirmation models and their traces follow (U2.4b). The needs view sends with each `agents_answer` and each `agents_setup_typed` the step mark of the item from which the pressed card was drawn, so that an answer counts for the question that the person saw (the page harness's sample view sends it too), and a set-up's chain of calls stops at the first reply that holds an error (RD-73 5.1) (U3.10).
- Fixed: halvo-coord hands a handback answer to the run in the move of the press whose read shows its row, after a store whose reply was lost (RD-52 (c)), and a press after one with no acknowledgement logs its own row before any kept row's result goes to the run (RD-61 (c)) (U2.4b).
- Changed: learn-gate routine refuses a sample line whose kind is not the kind of its train's number (1 to 100 same, 101 to 150 stop, 151 to 200 shift); opens the store and its list of summaries on every day from 1 to 730 before that day's batch, a day on which no routine row is left reading as one that differs, so that the routine line reaches the step's 30-day rewrite; and takes the panic hook for its plays, so that a play that panics writes nothing by itself and the one REFUSED line names the train and the message and place of the first play in place order that panicked. 50-r5-drift.sh and 55-r5-routine.sh remove the built learn-gate at its two names under the build directory, build it, take the one name that then exists (none or both is the cause build) and run that file directly, and fail with the cause stderr when a play that ended with status 0 or 1 wrote on standard error; 50-r5-drift.sh prints set=dev or set=heldout before the lines of each set, and falls back to the memory store only for the build's own refusal of the derived store (status 2, nothing on standard output and that one line on standard error, byte for byte with its final LF), any other refusal of the derived call failing with refused. The root .gitignore ignores /fixtures/r5/cases/heldout/ (the learning run's rulings LM-64, LM-66 to LM-70, LM-75 and LM-79, unit U2.7).
- Added: halvo-agent-client's RunEnd carries the runner's exit and the library's record of a forced end (kill, link_end or after_finish, with whether the browser was among it), the reap's answer carries that record with the path reap, a refused start names the relative path, the missing value of the runner's list or the runner's exit, and reap removes the profile directories that runs left (U4.6).
- Changed: halvo-agent-client's Supervisor::start returns StartError and answers started for a run id of its table or of a standing mark before it reads the other paths and values; reap checks each left profile against the run table just before it removes it and holds the profiles folder by a handle that follows no link; on Windows end_all reads the runner's own exit first; and the library takes a bind only once after a done handback of a sign-in reason, ends a run whose runner sends a frame out of turn after its stored finish or after bound=no, drops an open bind at a cancel, and gives up an add_origin after three replies that cannot be read (U4.6, CP-193).
- Removed: halvo-agent-client's Info.zombie, a field that the table's read never set true, and its filters (CP-193 item 5).
- Fixed: The tests of the account backend's stand-in under account/stub/ (billing run, 7.5t) wait, for at most 2,000 ms, for the answer or the fact that a step needs in place of a fixed 100 or 200 ms, so a slow runner no longer fails "after the peer resets the connection behind an answer" (main's run 43666146769), a failed test prints the record's lines and the bytes its client read, and a new test holds that the stop has begun, by its record, once a client has read the answer to POST /stop, and tests of cap.test.mjs pin the record's lines and the raw file's count for a peer that ends, stays silent or resets at BODY_CAP, and the sentences of account/stub/README.md on forced-close, BODY_CAP, the peer's end and its reset say what those tests hold and name each test.
- Added: The store owner of halvo-capture and the gate's timed run on it (capture run, daemon-1), under `crates/halvo-capture/src/daemon/` in test builds and in macOS builds with debug assertions: the store port of contracts.md C11.2 (`MaintainStore` with `expire_due`, `evict_to` and `usage`, `CaptureStore` with `write_batch` and `volume_free_bytes`, a segment id of the version 4 form minted once per batch, a `SegmentWrite` that no code outside the daemon can fill, and no per-event operation), a model store for the tests and a port that keeps nothing; the owner thread, which handles the waiting commands before each received event, starts in the discarding state, draws the barrier of a pause and of a resume from the sink's writer when it handles the command, and receives and discards what the sink holds before it draws the barrier of a resume, and discards an event whose emit began before it handled the resume, whenever its send lands, by a resume mark that every emit of the sink reads before it can block (`SinkWriter::resume_mark`, `Received::began_before`); batches of at most 512 events, 5 s of age on the monotonic clock, one UTC capture day and a payload buffer of 1,048,576 bytes allocated once and wiped after each write; `StoredText::filtered`, the one constructor of stored text and the one implementor of the text filter point, with R1's one filter, the byte cap of 1,024 bytes for the app id and the title and 253 for the site domain; the exclusion recheck on every received event; a failing drain that keeps its batch and its segment id, retries from 1 s to 60 s, holds at most 2,560 events and 2,097,152 record bytes and counts the rest as lost, drops a refused batch or one that fails the same way three times, reports a new cause each time the fault changes, and tries no write under a free-space floor of 16,777,216 bytes; expiry at open and on a 60 s deadline of the monotonic clock with each forward step of the wall clock taken only after it has stood for 600 s, eviction after each flush and again at each deadline after a fault, healthy maintenance reported only when no job's last call failed, one chunk of 64 segments per loop pass; counters for the life of the process with a snapshot that keeps received = persisted + discarded + lost + pending; seven required rows of `crates/halvo-capture/src/daemon/mutants.rows`; the ledger lines of `docs/baseline/ledger.d/daemon-1.txt`; and the ci.d checks 22-daemon-no-dead-allow (no allowance of dead or unused code or of warnings, no test-only product module, also under any cfg that names test or as r#name, one platform condition under src/daemon/, every file there named by exactly one plain mod line, and no mod in a macro) and 23-daemon-port-only (no file under src/daemon/ names the encrypted store, the segment store or rusqlite, a path to the module store in any spelling, a glob of the crate root, the module's name at the head of a path, a path built from a macro metavariable or an alias of the crate root), in both jobs, each reading every file of src/daemon/ but mutants.rows whatever its name and through a link, and failing with the path when the listing fails; the source rules of tests/source_rules.rs read every file of their directories but a closed list of data files, and two proof lines of 30-core-boundary-proofs, built in the macos job, hold the daemon's port closed to code outside it. The timed run, `--timed-run SECONDS --store-dir DIR [--hold-secure-input] [--switch NAME=on|off]...`, resumes the owner over a port that keeps nothing, starts the sources that the switches turn on (no screen source by default), lets the owner receive what the sink took in the window for up to 2 s (DRAIN_BOUND), pauses it, stops the sources and settles within 5 s, prints the report of contracts.md C5.1 (a count line for each kind, the sink's counts of each kind, `rss_peak_bytes`, the failed sources and, with the hold, its six lines), and exits 0, 1 with a loss line on standard error when the last settle finds events held or lost since the last acknowledged write, more events lost than before it, or no answer, 2 on SIGINT or SIGTERM, or 3 when a source failed; `daemon/signal.rs` installs the signal flag and the mask 0o077 on macOS before any mode and reads the process's own peak. The owner's snapshot counts the events lost since the last acknowledged write and the first and the last capture instant of every lost event, `BatchRejected` carries the dropped batch's instants and its fault, a failed try counts toward the drop only when it was due, and a sealed batch is given up at its first answer. Scripted sources serve the daemon's tests; under the feature `r2` stand the module line, one call per owner pass, `append_run_mark` and the run mark's key among the per-kind entries; `mutants.rows` holds eight rows. The compiler holds the store's boundary: 30-core-boundary-proofs holds eight proof lines that fail to build when a path, a glob, a re-export or a macro names the store's database type, key file or segment files outside the store, and fails when the lib's test build reads a file of the daemon that its product build does not read and that is no test support. Checks 22 and 23 read text and are a second layer; the help of each, `--help`, names their limits.
- Removed: `daemon::CaptureDaemon` of halvo-capture, which appended every received event to the store one by one; `daemon::DaemonError`, `daemon::TimedRunReport` and `daemon::timed_run` keep their names (capture run, daemon-1).
- Changed: The halvo-capture command (capture run, daemon-1) reads its arguments with `args_os` and prints usage and exits 64 for any line that it refuses: `--timed-run` requires `--store-dir DIR`, an absolute directory clear of the data directory that holds no store.key, takes SECONDS from 1 to 604800 and `--switch` once for each name, and with `--verify-store` and `--verify-deletion` exists only in builds with debug assertions, so a release build prints usage and exits 64 for each; it reads no HALVO_ variable: the timed run takes its directory and its switches from its flags, and `--verify-store` and `--verify-deletion` call store-2's `measure_store(DIR, MARKER)` and `measure_deletion(DIR)`. `daemon::TimedRunReport` has no `Default` and no `seconds`. In halvo-capture the store's database type, its key file, the modules expiry, segment and verify and `DEFAULT_RETENTION` are visible inside `crate::store` alone. Check 22 also refuses an allowance of dead code, of an unused lint or of warnings that covers the daemon from crates/halvo-capture/src/lib.rs, from a lints table of the crate's or the workspace's manifest, or from a file that sets the compiler's flags (ruling DM-2). The sink queue that the store owner of halvo-capture drains holds 1,024 events in place of 2,048 (ruling DM-9), so that 2,000,000 window-title records grow the owner by at most 8,388,608 bytes on a hosted Mac; the ledger's sink_queue line is 4,194,304 bytes, and the owner still holds at most 2,560 events while its drain fails; and the growth test of the store owner (ruling DM-10), 2,000,000 window-title records that may grow the owner's resident size by at most 8,388,608 bytes, runs in the release build of the lib's tests in the macos job by check 26-daemon-owner-growth and is ignored in the debug build of 12-test; it also prints the heap's bytes in use over every malloc zone and in the default zone and the physical footprint, at ready and after Settle, which are printed and not held. At a write the store owner counts toward its three tries only SQLite's primary codes 11 and 26 and the code 0 of a failed mint of the segment id, beside a refused and a sealed write; every other primary code but 13 (no space), the codes 10, 5 and 6 and one that this build does not know among them, is of the class of Io, is held, never counts and names the kind io (ruling DM-12 A8; ruling PR-30 PR4; ruling PR-31 item 3).
- Added: crates/halvo-test-dir, the one maker of the scratch directories of tests, a dev-dependency alone: it names a directory from the process id and a counter of its own process, makes it by create_dir, which fails when the name stands, and then takes the next number; and the lexical check scripts/r3/ci.d/15-r3-test-dirs.sh, which fails when a tracked test makes a scratch directory in one of the forms of its rule line outside its list of known places, and whose --help names the forms that it does not read (U2.5).
- Fixed: halvo-sheet's test helper scratch (src/testing.rs), the helpers of halvo-checkpoint's tests and halvo-agent-client's tests/common/mod.rs take their scratch directories from halvo-test-dir, so two threads at one clock value get two directories, and graph::tests::a_closed_port_is_not_sent of halvo-connect holds the freed port under one lock with listen, so no other test of the binary binds it (U2.5).
- Changed: (capture run, app-2) The app is a client of halvo-control and links no store crate: every command is async and goes through the client behind four places in the order of the calls, a poller reads the status every 3 s for the page, the tray and on_status of every registered module, and the page shows the real state of the capture service (connecting, not running, not answering, a refused status, unsupported, and each capture and store state with its cause), never claims capturing, paused or an empty store without a status, deletes with the store id that the person armed with and says deleted, already deleted or refused in the owner's words, and holds the 2 px around the arming point (ruling PL-10 (4) (a)); the settings file and its path leave the app, and --check-page takes --data-dir and prints the service. Ruling AP-14: the app keeps the last delete's answer in memory alone, with the time of the answer, until the next delete or a new window main, and the page reads it by the thirteenth command last_delete after it loads again; every answer of a mutation that its command returns as Ok replaces the view, from all three writers, the tray's toggle among them, unless its call was made before the newest answer taken, which Views reads against the mark as it stood before the answer (rulings AP-17 G2 and AP-19 H1), and an Err moves the mark; a delete's answer always takes the view to Connecting; a delete answered deleted or already_deleted, or whose reply was not read as an outcome (a deadline, a failed channel, a broken or refused reply, and an error reply of the service other than busy, unsupported_version and unsupported_op, which the contract gives before anything is removed; ruling AP-17 G6), leaves the view Connecting with no store and no sizes and wakes the poller, its error carries outcome_not_known, as a cap Save's does from the same list (ruling AP-19 H3), and after control-2c the busy of a life that is ending and an already_deleted that answers no request of the call remove nothing and read as a request that the service did not take, a panic or an early return leaves no kept answer in flight, and no status is read inside the command; every draw of the tray and every on_status runs under one draw lock and reads the view at its moment; the macOS guard reads back, after the delegate was put in front, that the view's navigation delegate is the guard by pointer, answers for the navigation selector and not for its form with preferences, and that the store holds one proxy configuration; on macOS the tray shows one sentence for every cause that keeps the window closed (I-114 point 14). On Windows the start arguments map tauri.localhost and ipc.localhost to no address too: --host-resolver-rules holds MAP * ~NOTFOUND alone (ruling AP-14 L12). Each means of the engine guard is held by a test that fails when that means alone is gone (ruling AP-17 G3): the navigation handlers and the Windows request hook decide by one function each, the macOS install's reads of the engine (the store and the allowed hosts, the delegate and the proxy read back, the question before the proxy is set) go through one seam that the tests drive with a stand-in, and the compile call of the rule list, the proxy's endpoint, the reading at start and the LinkPreconnect preference are pinned at their one call; the bodies that talk to WebKit (each method of the real engine's side, set_feature with the value that it sends and its read-back, and the peer preference's read-back) stand word for word as src-tauri/src/engine-pins.txt holds them (ruling AP-19 H2); the configuration at creation, the content policy, the frame script and the Windows start arguments are held by text tests and hosted copies, with no test on a developer's machine.
- Added: (capture run, app-2) The check scripts/r1/ci.d/20-app-links-no-store requires halvo-control in the app's normal tree and fails on halvo-capture, halvo-sqlcipher, rusqlite or libsqlite3-sys, or when cargo tree fails; the self-test of 32-app-check-page holds the cut of the first line at 120 characters (ruling PL-10 (4) (b)); docs/baseline/ledger.d/app-2.txt states the 1,048,576 bytes of the client's replies in flight. Ruling AP-14: the check scripts/r1/ci.d/38-app-engine-routes for the jobs macos and windows, with its self-test: in a second copy of the tree the engine-alone copy reads 0 beside the no-layer copy above 0 on the ip forms of s03 and s18, and s20 on Windows, and s05 reads control 0; a control of 0 fails as not measured. Ruling AP-15: outside a hosted job (GITHUB_ACTIONS is not true) the check changes nothing, builds nothing and starts nothing, and skips with exit 77; in a hosted job on Windows it turns off only the firewall profiles that are on and turns those alone on again from a trap that also runs on a signal and at an early exit, and prints the state before and after; its self-test runs it with stand-ins that record their calls, without the hosted variable (no call) and stopped in the middle (the state put back). Ruling AP-17 G8: it fails when the archive, tar or the copy maker fails or a copy does not hold its change, builds both copies into a target directory under its own work directory, ends the listener and a running launch by their recorded process ids at a signal before the firewall is put back, prints no row of a step that the job does not ask, and fails a control of 0 written as above 0; its self-test rejects 19 failing fixtures. In main's driver of lanes it runs in the lane alone, because it turns the Windows firewall off, listens on ports of its own, builds two copies and starts the built app.
- Security: (capture run, app-2) The window main refuses every download through its own handler. It is created hidden at about:blank and loads the app's page only after every engine means of rulings AP-9 to AP-12 answered installed; otherwise it is closed before any load, and the app runs on without it while its tray item stands, with Open disabled and the tray line, on macOS for every cause, "Halvo's window cannot open on this Mac right now. You can still pause or resume here. Quit Halvo and start it again; if the window still does not open, a newer Halvo is needed to see or delete what Halvo has stored.", and on Windows "Halvo's window is not available. Quit Halvo and start it again.", or ends with exit 3 when there is no tray item; --check-page prints guard=installed. On Windows the app takes WEBVIEW2_ADDITIONAL_BROWSER_ARGUMENTS out of its own environment as the first step of main, before any thread exists (ruling AP-17 G9). The means on Windows: a handler of every resource request that refuses each address but http://tauri.localhost and http://ipc.localhost, and a request whose address it cannot read, with a refusal made at install so that no call that can fail stands before it (ruling AP-17 G9), the two navigation handlers, which also cancel when they cannot read the address, and start arguments that map every host name to no address, the app's two names among them (the page loaded and answered its four read commands in each of the 21 launches of the head), point the engine at a proxy of no address, take the proxy's implicit exceptions away by --proxy-bypass-list=<-loopback> (Chromium's net/docs/proxy.md, "Implicit bypass rules" and "Overriding the implicit bypass rules": localhost, *.localhost, [::1], 127.0.0.1/8, 169.254/16 and [FE80::]/10) and keep WebRTC off unproxied UDP by --webrtc-ip-handling-policy=disable_non_proxied_udp. On macOS: the navigation delegate in front of the runtime's, read back before the load (the view's delegate is Halvo's by pointer, it answers yes for the navigation selector and no for that selector's form with preferences, and the store reads back one proxy configuration), a content rule list that blocks every load but tauri://localhost and ipc://localhost, the data store's proxy at no address, the engine's peer-connection and link-preconnect preferences turned off and read back, a configuration at creation whose store keeps nothing and whose set of allowed network hosts is empty, read back on the built web view, and a reading at start, in a world of its own, that the engine offers no peer-connection constructor; every private selector is asked for before it is sent. The empty set of allowed network hosts refuses web addresses of other hosts for page loads; it does not cover a datagram or the connection of a turn: address over TCP. Beside them the content policy carries webrtc 'block' and a script in every frame takes the peer-connection constructors away; no measured 0 rests on either. Measured in hosted run 43632388796 for scripted steps, with the engine means in and the content policy, the runtime's rule and the script out, beside a control above 0 in the same job, with every listener bound, in 21 launches of the head on each system and one of each other copy. On windows-2025-vs2026 20261103.298.1 (WebView2 runtime 156.0.4314.58), to the runner's own IPv4 address or a name for it: 0 connections and 0 requests for a refused navigation with a query, a link clicked by script, a frame, a form post with a body, a download from another host, an image, a fetch, sendBeacon, a WebSocket, a worker's fetch and a navigation by an initialization script; 0 connections over https, and 0 connections and 0 requests with a port of the job, for a navigation to a form that the URL reader refuses; 0 datagrams for a peer connection to a stun: server, with a marked remote description, with a turn: address and a user name over UDP and from the constructor of a new empty frame, and 0 connections and 0 requests with a turn: address over TCP; 0 lookups for the name forms; 0 datagrams for a WebTransport connection, where the control sent 6, and 0 connections and 0 requests for a prefetch link, where the control opened 1 with 1 request; speculation rules: not measured, the control read 0. The request hook alone lets a WebSocket to that address through (1 connection, 1 request) and holds sendBeacon and a worker's fetch. To a loopback address of the runner, on Windows 127.0.0.1, localhost, [::1], and tauri.localhost and ipc.localhost with a port of the job, and on macos-15-arm64 20261104.0514.1 (macOS 15.8.2, WebKit 20621.3.11.11.3) the first three: 0 connections, 0 requests and 0 datagrams in the head and with the engine means alone for a refused navigation, a link clicked by script, a frame, an image, a fetch, sendBeacon, a WebSocket, a worker's fetch, a peer connection to a stun: server and a turn: address over TCP, beside a control above 0 with no layer for each; on Windows an image, a fetch, sendBeacon and a worker's fetch to the two app names with a port: not measured, the control read 0. On Windows the five loopback forms, tauri.localhost and ipc.localhost with a port among them, read 0 without the proxy, without --proxy-bypass-list=<-loopback> and without the map, where with no layer a refused navigation, a link and a frame to the two app names opened 1 connection, a WebSocket 1 with 1 request and a turn: address over TCP 2 with 4 requests. On macOS, to the runner's own IPv4 address or a name for it, and for the three links below also to an IPv6 address off the runner (to an IPv4 address off the runner the head's three cells were not measurable in that run, because the packet filter did not see a probe of the head's launch, and the engine-alone copy read 0 beside the same control): 0 connections and 0 requests for the steps above but the refused form, and 0 connections for a preconnect hint and a link with a target; the empty set of allowed hosts alone reads 0 connections for a link clicked by script, a link with a target and a download link on every form measured, off the runner among them, and hosted run 43541710304 showed that the early connection of the three links is removed by the link-preconnect preference alone and the request of the first and the last is refused by the rule list or by the delegate. No route found: window.open on both systems and a link with a target on Windows, because wry 0.57.1 refuses every new window when the app sets no handler (src/webview2/mod.rs lines 861 to 863, src/wkwebview/class/wry_web_view_ui_delegate.rs lines 288 to 290); a test holds wry 0.57.1 and tauri-runtime-wry 2.12.3 in Cargo.lock. The preconnect and dns-prefetch hints on Windows: with the private mode on the runtime made no connection for a hint, at load or by script. The dns-prefetch hint on macOS: the head's cell is not measured, the control read 0; with DNS prefetching on, which Halvo does not set, it was closed (hosted run 43585044597). The dns-prefetch hint to an address in numbers or to a hosts-file name has no count. Not measurable: the refused form over http at port 80 on Windows, because the system holds port 80 on the runner. Not measured: on macOS a navigation to a form that the URL reader refuses: the control read 0; row A2-1b: with no layer 0 of the 70 refused forms left the app on macos-15-arm64 20261104.0514.1 (WebKit 20621.3.11.11.3) beside both controls above 0 (hosted run 43585044597, log lines 6766 to 6769; ruling AP-12 point 2 (d) leaves the 70 forms out of later runs); what refuses them was not read, and Halvo's means had nothing to refuse. Not measurable: a link-local address on both systems; the entry that takes the exception for link-local addresses away is in (Chromium's net/docs/proxy.md). On macOS a WebTransport connection, a prefetch link and speculation rules: not measured, the control read 0; a link preview has no step, because no script can set one off. On Windows, to an address off the runner (198.51.100.0/24), the system's connection audit (Filtering Platform Connection, events 5156 and 5157), which counts connections of the engine's processes (msedgewebview2.exe and halvo.exe) and does not count a lookup by the system's resolver or a packet without a connection: 0 in the head and with the engine means alone for an image, a fetch, sendBeacon, a WebSocket over ws and over wss, a worker's fetch, an EventSource, a prefetch link, a frame, a peer connection to a stun: server, a turn: address over TCP, a WebTransport connection, a ping, a download, a link clicked by script and a refused navigation, beside a control above 0 with no layer for each; not measured, the control read 0: a preconnect hint, a link with a target, window.open, a form post, an image, a frame, a fetch and a navigation to a file: address on that host, and the start of a scheme registered under HKCU from a frame, window.open, a link with a target, a link and a navigation. Not measured on macOS 14 or on a macOS newer than 15.
- Fixed: (capture run, app-2, rulings AP-14, AP-17 and AP-19) The page's words after a delete follow the reply's six fields: it reads nothing left only when nothing_left is true, root_destroyed is yes, not_followed, files_left, entries_left and unknown_entries are 0 and no database file is left, with a line of its own for each field that is not clean (I-114 points 1 and 2); a sentence that says that something left cannot be read stands only beside root_destroyed yes, and beside no or unknown the not-followed text, the files line and the database line take points 16 and 17; a key file that no command names has a sentence of its own (point 3); the words after a delete and the clause on copies and backups are the owner's points 4 and 5. After a delete answered deleted or already_deleted the page loads again, offers no second delete during the load, and shows the kept result with its time, and with its date when it is not of today (points 7, 8 and 19), with the focus only at that load; a delete whose request was written and whose reply was not read as an outcome says that Halvo cannot tell (point 6), and a delete answered busy says that the press deleted nothing (point 13), with no second ask. An answer of a pause, a cap or the tray's toggle whose call was made before the newest answer taken moves nothing, and a delete's answer always takes the view to Connecting, so that in no order of a delete, a pause, a cap and the tray's toggle and of their answers does a deleted store come back before a poll made after the delete's answer. stored.js draws an inventory only for the store of the status that stands and only one sent after the newest answer, also at the release of a hold. A pause or a cap answered not running reads not running on the page and on the tray. A cap Save with no status keeps the typed value and says by the service whether the cap was changed (points 9 to 11); one that ends in an error after which its outcome is not known, by the same list as the delete's, shows its error words and then point 18; a value typed after Save is not overwritten by the reply; and the eviction line has the words of point 12 and is cleared by a status at or under the cap, a size or cap that is not known, another store, a no-status view, the next cap reply or a load.
- Security: Halvo's build of SQLCipher (crates/halvo-sqlcipher, capture run sqlcipher-1, rulings S-82, S-83, ST-40 and ST-42) zeroes, under secure delete, the gap of a page that the database builds again (a statement at the end of line 81262, in rebuildPage) or edits in place (a statement at the end of line 81517, in editPage), where the pinned source leaves copies of rows, and is built without SQLITE_ENABLE_STAT4, whose samples keep the text of removed rows; in the keyless draws of the crate's tests page_gap (tables and tables WITHOUT ROWID with indexes, rows of 8 bytes to more than a page, single and mass deletes, replacements in place and of the same length, rows that end in a zero blob, a copy by INSERT ... SELECT, a VACUUM, a delete that frees a second freelist trunk) no text of a removed or replaced row is left in the files and every unused byte up to the usable size of every page is zero but for two allowances (stale 2-byte offsets right after a page's pointer array, each at least its slot's offset plus 2 and at most the usable size less 4, and 4-byte values no larger than the page count in the tail of an overflow page and in a freelist trunk past its count), where the build without the statements leaves such text; the reserved tail of a page is the codec's and is not read, and the keyed draws get no search and no structural check: they read their rows back against a model and run the integrity check alone; in the crate's FTS5 draws a table of that form leaves no removed token, searched whole, by its tail from byte 6 and by its last 6 bytes, in any page that dbstat does not name the table's _idx tree after merges that end, and FTS5's own code keeps such a token as a key of _idx until a 'rebuild'. It cleans no file that an earlier build wrote.
- Added: halvo-sqlcipher's build stops, naming the pinned version, what it looked for and what to read again, when the sha256 of the pinned sqlcipher.c is not the one that was read or a changed line is not found once in its function; the copy holds eight compile-time stops after the pinned file's last line (the database's code compiled out, first, because MSVC ends at the first #error; STAT4 defined, a temp store other than 2, auto-vacuum, no codec, synchronous other than 2, SQLITE_DEBUG, a compile default of the page size other than 4096), each refusing the build by name whatever sets it (shown by a build with each flag in CFLAGS on this Mac and in named hosted runs), and then ends with a function of Halvo's own that answers 2, which `halvo_sqlcipher::page_gap_mend_linked` requires; the mutant rows rebuilt-page-gap-not-zeroed and edited-page-gap-not-zeroed take each statement out; ADR 0013 records the change, its table of the byte writes of btree.c, whose rows a test holds, with counts of the secured keyless draws, and what a move of the pin owes; the rules of the structural check stand in tests/page_gap/rules.rs, which another crate's tests can include, with a function that plants a byte in the places that its doc lists; for each line that the structural check or a search can print, a test of page_gap makes the fault in a copy or in a file and requires that line, a test fails when a line of rules.rs has no control, and ADR 0013 lists the parts of rules that have none; a test pins that a new database of this build has auto_vacuum 0; in every draw of page_gap the rows that the test did not remove read back equal to its model, by table and by index, and the integrity check passes, keyless and under a key, and every result was held against the pinned source without the statements on 64 drawn workloads at the stress step and at the gates, which no tracked test holds; a test pins what the build does on a leaf whose content start lies above live cells (the integrity check refuses it before the first delete, and a delete loses those cells), and a keyed test requires that one byte of a b-tree page changed on disk fails the read; the capture store's deletion proof changed with the build: a hardening test of ADR 0005's rule that a shred keeps the key row of its segment now judges the mutant row key-row-deleted, the two formal rows of a deleted key row are gone because under this build such a row left neither the whole wrapped key nor its middle 32 bytes in the files of the scaled cases in one hosted run (docs/verify/store-shred.md, Not covered), and a new formal row, formal-shred-never-committed, fails the oldest-first case with a shred whose transaction never commits.
- Fixed: On a Mac a key typed ahead of a focus move into a password field is no longer recorded when the program takes it inside the hold (capture run, macos-2b, ruling MA-12): the keyboard source holds every key down and modifier press with its own instant and the app of its arrival, and records it only when it is as old as the bound (30,000 ms, `HOLD_BOUND`) and every reading of the secure-input state from its arrival was off and ended no more than 30 ms (`GAP_BOUND`) after the read before it began; a reading of on or unknown, a gap, a key down, key up or flags change whose own reading of the state ended more than 30 ms (`LAG_BOUND`) after its OS time, or whose OS time is 0 or after its arrival, a key made before a wake of the Mac and a reading that finds the Mac woke (ruling MA-22 L1), a reading that could not read the two clocks together (the cause unclocked, a timing fault) and every key before the first close pair of clocks after a start whose pair did not come close (ruling MA-24), a tap that the system switched off, the daemon's own take of secure input, a stop, a refused emit, a session that is not at the console and a full queue of 4,096 keys drop every held key, which sets keys_withheld, except at a stop or a refused emit, and is in no count; a timing fault reads the source impaired with run_loop_stalled until a key is next released, and a wake is no timing fault, so a Mac whose two clocks cannot be read together notes no typing and reads impaired. After a tap notice, a reading that was not off, a wake or a late or untimed event, the next flags change is a reading alone (ruling MA-22 L2); after a stop no path takes a reading, a drop has the cause stop and sets no flag, a tap notice does not switch the tap on again (ruling MA-22 L3), and an arriving key decided after a stop is withheld with the cause stop and moves no flag and no count (ruling MA-24). The lag rule and the wake mark are shown on stand-in clocks alone: nobody has seen a key cross a sleep on hardware, and the key presses between a wake and the first reading after it are not noted. On a hosted macOS 15 runner the reading timer came up to 60 ms late, so there the guard records no key at the product's constants (ruling MA-16). Two cases stay open faults: a program busy for longer than the bound, a listed known fault of the check 51-macos-stall, and secure input that is on for less than the gap bound, or never, or that ends less than the lag bound after a key, held at the product's gap by a stand-in that is a listed known fault of 51-macos-stall, with the measured lines run on a scratch push and asserting nothing (rulings MA-19, MA-22 L1 (c) and L10). Its mechanism has been seen on the hosted machine at the test's gap in three of the four recorded long-form runs of the measured line: 3 of 124 rounds in run 43604085133, 2 in run 43627916230, 2 in run 43655810029, and none in run 43741572763. An on-time shorter than the spacing of two readings that are both in time releases the keys; at the product's gap of 30 ms no hosted round shows it, and the cause of the one hosted click round that recorded its letters is not shown (ruling MA-20). In the hosted rounds at 2 ms steps the keys were held under a reading of off and dropped by a reading that fell into the on-time while the program handled them; the on-time was not printed. The guard was shown with a test program on a hosted macOS alone.
- Changed: The macOS keyboard source records no key up and no release of a modifier; both stay readings of the hold. emit_metadata_only takes the instant of its record, so the record of a held key, its number and its id are made at the release with the key's own time; `InputSource::with_hold_bound`, which a build without debug assertions does not have, takes a shorter bound for tests in a process of their own, and `InputSource::with_seam`, which only a debug test build with the feature `stall-seam` has (turned on by the crate's own dev-dependency on itself, one line of Cargo.lock and no package), takes the bound, the gap, the timer's step and the session reader and counts the drops by cause; secure_input_keystrokes counts a key down that came in time under secure input alone, before a stop; a modifier press after a reading of on is a reading alone and is not counted (ruling MA-22 L2); the reading of the state has three values, unknown being a session that is not at the console or has no session dictionary, read at the start and every 250 ms while keys are held; and the rows of the per-event read are made again against the hold, with the rows of the reader and of the hold beside secure_input_macos.rs.
- Added: The stall fixture of the macOS keyboard source and its hosted tests (crates/halvo-capture/tests/macos_stall_fixture.rs, ignored, refusing to run outside a GitHub-hosted step that names the fixture), with the probe of MA-12 F4; the fixture's stamps on its main thread and the source's raw entries behind the feature stall-seam (times, values and causes alone, in memory), printed for every round; the measured line short_window (the click round as it was, and the leave that the fixture is asked to make 0 to 200 ms after the secure field became first responder, with the on-time as measured, since the set on-times were not reached) and the whole-password forms at a person's pace and at 2 ms steps as measured lines, which assert nothing; the Tab, click, focus-move and leaving-key phases keep the secure field for the test's gap plus 130 ms after the fixture's first stamp of on, count a round only when its entries show a reading of on in the span from the fixture's last stamp of off before the on-time to its first stamp of off after it and a drop for on made by that reading, read (D) only in the narrow span of the fixture's first and last stamp of on, leave a round uncounted as a late click only when every key that it recorded was released before the fixture's last stamp of off and at least the phase's bound after its own arrival, fail every other round that did not count (ruling MA-22 L6), and run under a cap of rounds, and the click phase counts 62 rounds at wait 2,000 ms (ruling MA-19); and tests that run by default with stand-ins or tables, which open no window and post no input: the launch path with five stand-ins for open (ruling MA-17), one fixture for the line at the product's constants (ruling MA-18), the stamps command, the rules of the verdict, of the line at the product's constants by each round's own cause and entries (ruling MA-22 L7) and of short_window's case, and the on-time with the fates of the held keys (ruling MA-19);
