the check scripts/r1/ci.d/51-macos-stall.sh of the macos job, which runs each stall phase in its own process at the gap of the test's own that input.rs decides (twice the widest spacing of measure_the_readings_of_the_hold, rounded up to 10 ms, from 30 to 500 ms, measured by the check when HALVO_STALL_GAP_MS is unset and once more above that cap), in the short form of the every-push job (every wait of each mended phase with one counted round, the return phase, one control round, the two-sided gap rule, both listed known faults and the proofs, with a line for each thing that it leaves out) or, with HALVO_STALL_FORM=long, in the long form that adds the line at the product's constants, short_window form (a), the loop and the whole-password lines (ruling MA-23), holds the known fault of a program busy for longer than the bound by its own assertion, holds D2 by a stand-in at the product's gap listed as a known fault, fails when the passing run of the long form that docs/baseline/ledger.d/macos-2b.txt records ran on other blobs of the hold's nine files than the head's (rulings MA-22 L10, MA-23), and says that a measured line asserts nothing, prints what the rounds of pushes 5 to 7 show and do not show, reads that 12-test ran the tests with stand-ins or tables alone of this file and passed them, and proves that a check build without debug assertions has no `with_hold_bound`, no print of a held key and no `with_seam`, the last also with the feature stall-seam (ruling MA-22 L8); and docs/baseline/ledger.d/macos-2b.txt with the line for the held keys at their cap and the run of the long form with its figures and the blob ids of the nine files; tests/source_rules.rs lists the path attribute that declares secure_input_macos_hold.rs (ruling MA-12 C2).
- Added: Three CI checks of the release (release-1): 60-release-dry-run runs the documented release command in a throwaway clone with no remote (the dry run on a named branch changes nothing, --execute is refused off main, and on main it makes one commit Release <next> and one tag v<next> with every package at <next> and the typed entries grouped under ### headings; on a release commit it requires that the dry run refuses with its no entry line), 61-version reads the one version of the workspace from cargo metadata and refuses a workflow or check that compares --version output with a literal on one line, in either order or by a case pattern on that line (a literal held in a variable is not seen), and on a tag ref a tag other than v<version>, and 62-build makes the unsigned release build of halvo and halvo-capture by calls that name no other package, feature or configuration, refuses a tauri package that cargo resolves from another source than crates.io, an untracked cargo configuration at any depth of the checkout, and a tracked one at any depth that its list does not name, which is empty (none outside the checkout is read), checks that both binaries print that version, that the build changed no file of the checkout and that no shipped file holds the stand-in or test-seam marker, and puts Halvo.app.tar (macOS) or halvo.exe and halvo-capture.exe (Windows) into its artifacts; scripts/merge-pr.sh reads with gh api the copies that the default branch of the repository that gh merges into holds of the file that $0 names (the file that runs, when sh runs a file), of scripts/check-commit-messages.sh and of scripts/commit-range.sh, stops before its other checks when one differs from the copy that the run uses, and hashes them again after the fetch that follows its merge call.
- Changed: The Build, Version and build-tree upload steps of both CI jobs are now check 62-build, and the workflow names no secret; the step Lockfile unchanged fails when HEAD is not the commit of the run, when a tracked file changed, when a file stands that no tracked .gitignore ignores, when a tracked file carries an index flag, and when an ignore line of an untracked file or of git's settings hides a file, with git's own variables dropped, the checkout's git directory and work tree named by flags, the file-system monitor and replace objects off, and git's time and stat checks at their defaults (a change of a file's mode is read on the macos job alone, and neither job sees a rename that changes only the case of a name or a git that a directory added to GITHUB_PATH puts first); scripts/check-changelog.sh refuses an Unreleased entry whose text starts with a type word and a colon; package.json pins the Tauri CLI to the exact version 2.12.4 of the lock file, and src-tauri/windows-tests.manifest is checked out with LF (release-1).
- Fixed: sh scripts/release.sh works again (release-1): every package of the workspace gets one shared version, the release commit's subject names it, src-tauri/release.toml holds the tag and the CHANGELOG.md and README.md rules, and the pre-release hook groups the typed entries of the release under Keep a Changelog headings; the wrapper refuses a bump level other than patch, minor or major, runs --execute on main only and, when the clone has a remote named origin, only on main's newest commit there, reads CHANGELOG.md as the lint does, refuses a dry run in a shallow clone, a heading of the next version that already stands and a commit message, tag name or tag message that would hold a template, all before cargo-release changes a file, refuses on --execute a tracked file that differs from HEAD or carries an index flag and a clone where git can name no committer, before the fetch of a shallow clone, puts the clone back to the commit it started from and deletes the tags of the run when cargo-release fails, and names index.crates.io only when cargo-release failed on a lookup or a connection; `sh scripts/release.sh --help` prints the two modes, what each changes, the read of origin's newest commit and the one case without it, the refusals before a release and the undo of a failed one, the default level patch and the steps that stay the owner's.
- Added: The native confirmation dialog of halvo-coord-app holds every control of each show for arm_ms after its window became the active window and visible to the person, decided at the press by the presenter's own clock, with a refused press starting the hold anew, and reports the show on screen no earlier than that moment (interface run, U4.5, rulings RD-91, RD-103 and RD-112); on Windows the report comes at the first reading at which the task dialog's window is active and visible, at an activation or at the window's own show, and never at TDN_CREATED while the window cannot be seen; on macOS the moment is read on the main thread at the two become notices, at a reading repeated about every 50 ms until the first moment and at each press before it is decided, a report that panics still starts the hold, as on Windows, and the alert's observers and repeating reading end on every way out of the show, a panic among them; the show call and the presenter's constructor carry arm_ms. The hosted test target guard_hosted holds the hold through the dialog of each system, on Windows by its own Show and on macOS by the moment's decision, and the check 46-r4-dialog-ran requires in each hosted job the end lines of dialog_hosted and guard_hosted with every case of that system run and none failed, no show helped, no report taken at a press, no report made before the window was visible and the count of slipped schedules (a case whose schedule slipped runs again, at most three times in all), and no inconclusive line and no line of the loop mode (rulings RD-94 (b), RD-107, RD-111 and RD-112).
- Changed: The native dialog's card takes each label and value as they are, where it turned control characters, line separators and direction marks into blanks, and the presenter refuses to show a card that breaks the composed form, over its bound (16 lines and 120 characters a line, a placeholder that the proven lists of U2.4l replace) or with an access-key mark in a control text, with no answer (interface run, U4.5, ruling RD-90 item 10).
- Changed: (routing run, U2.2c) The floor of the test formal_traces of halvo-route counts the drawn traces of formal/r6-spend/traces.jsonl that differ and refuses a file with fewer than 200 such lines, so six directed traces and one drawn trace written 200 times no longer pass it (contracts.md T7.6, ruling SG-51).
- Added: (routing run, U2.2c) formal/r6-spend/export.sh refuses a result with fewer than 200 drawn traces that differ before it writes --out, with `REFUSED drawn: <n> drawn traces differ, 200 are needed; draw again with a larger --count` and status 2; docs/r6/gateway/request.md gains a line for `sequences.jsonl` that names the three states the export does not reach and says that a replay reads the file from the tree and keeps no copy and no sum (contracts.md T7.5 and T7.6, rulings SG-50, SG-51 and SG-54).
- Changed: In the router's checks (routing run, U1.2e, contracts.md T13.1 and T1.5), 30-r6-table and 20-r6-gateway print `FAIL <NN-name> (setup)` and exit 1 where they cannot enter the repository or make a directory, where they exited 2 with no such line; 30-r6-table reads the outputs of its cargo commands from variables of the script and the committed specimen by a SHA-256 sum taken before its first cargo command, never reads back the files under its artifacts directory, and reads the generated specimen only when `route-gate generate` exits 0 (SG-48 F1), refuses a list in which a line stands twice with `required list=bad` (G20), keeps refusing a constant whose name starts with PINNED at the start of a line as `pins unreadable=PINNED` (H21), and its self-test puts a carriage return into the listing, the ignored listing and the run of the required tests alone (E16b); 40-r6-policy fails with `(flags)` on the plain name of a variable that starts with `CARGO_TARGET_` and ends with `_RUNNER`, and on that name written with the escapes that its scan decodes, in a file that its scan reads, in a comment too; a name built from parts, a name escaped another way (a backslash before a plain letter in sh, a caret in cmd, a backtick in PowerShell), a file that the scan does not read (an extension that its list lacks or one in upper case, an env file that a workflow loads) and a runner key given to cargo through `--config` pass it (the capture run's request of 2026-11-13 17:38Z).
- Changed: learn-gate routine takes the newest routine row of its play's workflow and agent by halvo_learn_store::newest_routine, so that of two rows of one rev the one of the later day gives the figures (the learning run's ruling LM-79 item 2, unit U2.7).
- Changed: In the windows job, where CI_D_JOB is windows, 30-r3-formal, 30-r4-formal and 30-r5-formal leave their invariant and state-count runs of TLC and their control copies to the macos job, print one line that names them, and keep the export and every check of the model files; 30-r5-formal prints small=- in its record line there, and 30-r4-formal's gate record is made again (run 20261113-halvo-r8-citime, stint 2, attempt a04).
- Added: A ci.d script raises its own time limit above the default of 1,200 s with a line `# limit: <n>` among its first ten lines, the driver's own cases have 1,250 s, and scripts/ci/README.md states the margin of both limits over the measured seconds; the driver scripts/ci/run-ci-d.sh refuses a script under a ci.d directory that git does not track, such as a file that a self-test left there for the next step, whatever the spelling of CI_D_SCRIPTS, and the variable RUN_CI_D_NESTED whenever it is set, the empty value included, which made the own cases stop after their first case and pass; the own cases hand their nested runs the switch on file descriptor 9 (capture run, driver-2).
- Changed: The driver scripts/ci/run-ci-d.sh tries the removal of a script's TMPDIR three times over about 2 seconds, ends in one process the groups that the self-tests left at the end of the step ci.d self-test, keeps its own files in a directory apart from the one that holds the scripts' TMPDIRs and gives each TMPDIR a parent of its own, runs its own git with no global git configuration and no GIT_ variable of the caller, on a PATH without the cargo bin directory, against an index of its own and, before the first script, against the checkout's index as well, hashes the settings that git reads for the checkout, files that the configuration includes among them, and at the own cases' time limit and at an interrupt ends the nested driver and the witness of the case that runs, each of which writes its own record after it has made its group and then reads the driver's mark; scripts/r1/ci.d/12-test.sh and scripts/r1/ci.d/15-gate-self-test.sh name the time limit `# limit: 1800`, at least 1.5 times their longest hosted time measured when the line was set (capture run, driver-2).
- Fixed: An interrupt of the step ci.d self-test ends the process groups of the self-tests that have already ended, as scripts/ci/README.md says, not only those of the self-tests that run; and the own cases of the driver scripts/ci/run-ci-d.sh for standard error written after the print, for a child that a script leaves and for a limit reached under a moved clock hold their order by marker files, where a sleep or the start time of a process decided it and a loaded runner failed the first of them (capture run, driver-2).
- Security: In halvo-runner (agent-setup run, U4.1's second pull request, contracts.md A9.4, ruling CP-178 (7)), a write step whose `file` is not the slot `workbook` is refused before any load, `prepare` or row: the run ends `failed` with the cause `workbook`, with no effect row and nothing written, where such a step was prepared and written in the file of its own slot.
- Added: In halvo-runner (agent-setup run, U4.1's second pull request, contracts.md A7.1, A7.4, A11.3, rulings CP-96 (b), CP-98 (4), CP-135 (3)), a file edit stores its checkpoint's row `planned` after the load before the edit and before `prepare`, and its row `made` with the SHA-256, the length and the name that `prepare` gives before the effect's `propose`; a later `prepare` of the run on the same workbook sends neither row again, also when it answers the checkpoint error because the person changed the workbook, so the `made` row keeps the SHA-256 of the copy that stands (CP-139 (b)), and such a later `prepare` runs only while the checkpoint stands with that row's length and SHA-256, so that a checkpoint that the person removed or changed is not made again under the old row (the check runs outside the lock of `prepare`: when the person removes the checkpoint and changes the workbook between the check and that lock, a copy of the changed workbook stays under the `made` row with the old digest, the run ends `failed`, and the library leaves that copy as `changed`); a row that the store does not take ends the run `failed` before `prepare` or before the `propose`, and the runner removes no checkpoint.
- Added: halvo-schema's executable::day::day_of(at, utc_offset_s), the day of an instant on a clock that many whole seconds east of UTC (the seconds of the instant by div_euclid, plus the offset, by div_euclid of a day; a negative day is 0), and UTC_OFFSET_S_MAX, 50,400 s; the runner stamps every day through it, and the store's host takes it at the join of U3.1's second pull request (CP-204 D2, D9).
- Changed: The runner requires --utc-offset SECONDS, a whole number from -50,400 to 50,400 in the form that i32 writes, refuses with the status 2 and one line on standard error before its first frame a start without it ("REFUSED usage: no --utc-offset"), with another value ("REFUSED usage: --utc-offset is out of range"), with it twice ("REFUSED usage: --utc-offset twice") and with it as the last word and no value ("REFUSED usage: a key with no value"), and stamps the day of every log line, effect row, checkpoint row and finish by day_of with that offset in place of the UTC day; halvo-agent-client's RunSpec gains utc_offset_s with no default, which Supervisor::command passes on, so a start whose offset the runner refuses answers not_started with the runner's exit (CP-204 D1, D3, D4).
- Added: (agent-setup run, U3.5 (a)) In halvo-schema, `executable::same_address`, `executable::reply_senders` with its answer `executable::Senders`, which only the reader makes and which holds another address against its senders by `same_address`, its error `executable::SendersError`, and `executable::is_sender`, the adapter's rule for a sender, moved from halvo-connect, which gives on `reply_senders`, `same_address`, `Senders` and `SendersError` under its own names; so the store and the policy crate can hold two addresses by the function that the adapter uses, and their calls are U3.5 (b)'s (contracts.md A11.2, CP-150 (1) (f), CP-153 (2), CP-162 (3)).
- Changed: (agent-setup run, U3.5 (a)) The reader of `reply_senders`, which stood in halvo-connect before this unit and stands in halvo-schema now, takes the white space that `char::is_whitespace` names from the ends of each piece, a no-break space and a tab among it, where it took the space alone; its rule for a sender refuses in the part after the `@` a dot at its start, one at its end and two in a row; and `SendersError` names the piece by its number counted from 1, `Twice` both numbers, with a value of its own, `NotAscii`, for a piece that holds a byte outside ASCII. `halvo_connect::reply_senders` answers `Senders` in place of a list of texts, with those numbered errors, and `ReplyRule::new` takes that answer, so a rule with no sender can no longer be made. In halvo-agent-gate, `join_senders` reads the joined list with that reader, refuses what it refuses with `SendersRefused::Read` and the reader's error, and binds the reader's trimmed senders, where it kept its own trim and twice rule (CP-165 (5) (b), (c), (e), CP-162 (3), CP-203).
- Removed: (agent-setup run, U3.5 (a)) In halvo-agent-gate, the variants `Empty`, `EmptyAddress` and `Twice` of `SendersRefused`; the reader's errors under `SendersRefused::Read` take their place (CP-203).
- Added: (agent-setup run, U3.1 b) In halvo-agent-store, the handlers of the checkpoint list: `checkpoint` stores a workbook's `planned` row and then its `made` row, `list_checkpoints` reads the newest `planned` or `made` row of every agent's checkpoints by run id with `ended`, `effect` and `superseded`, and `mark_checkpoint` stores the `gone` or `left` row of a `planned` or `made` checkpoint alone; and the start row: `start_run` stores one `agent_log` row `start_accepted` (CP-135 (5)); the public read `newest_playbook`, the agent's newest stored playbook number by the reader of `load`'s key `playbook` (CP-202 Addition 1); and `MemoryStore::with_utc_offset`, the memory store's local calendar.
- Changed: (agent-setup run, U3.1 b) In halvo-agent-store, `Now` holds the request's instant alone and `Host::day` gives the local day of an instant; a stored row's `day` is the day of its `at`, and a row after the day of the request or outside its kind's bound is refused with the new error name `day` (CP-122); every runner body of a run with no start row of its agent is refused with `binding`, and a `log` line with `seq` 1 with `effect` (CP-135 (5)); a `releasing` or `committed` row holds the approval of the effect's last `approved` row, `finish` refuses a record that the end's moves contradict, and a repeat is a row equal to the effect's last row in every key but `day` and `at` (CP-132); a start row stands from 93 days back to two days ahead of the request's day (CP-205 C1); a `checkpoint` whose planned row would make its run's checkpoints overflow one page of `list_checkpoints` is refused with `length` (CP-205 C2). In halvo-learn-store, `list_refinements` reads the agent's newest compile number by `newest_playbook` in place of a `load` of a probe run, which the start row's rule refuses.
- Added: The browser profile trial (U0.3c) prints one `egress_denial` line for each denial of item 3 with its protocol, its port and whether its host is loopback, never the address, and holds item 3 by a tracked run of the whole program against a stand-in check, the end of item 3 by rows for a signal and a missing inner line, run.sh's argument reader by a tracked test, the web trial's collector by 19 new self-test cases (33 to 52), and the hosted harness's put-back rules by 24 new self-test cases (11 to 34, of which 14 run on Windows); tools/r3-trials/web/hosted/fetch.txt names pypdf 4.3.1's wheel with its address, size and SHA-256.
- Fixed: The profile trial reads every fault of a browser's start, `no_channel` among them, as `cause=browser_start` in items 1 and 2 and in item 4's task shape; run.sh takes `--driver PATH`, refuses a file at `--browser` that is no program as `browser` and `--item 3` with another port as `port`; the hosted harness puts back a policy that stood before, takes away the key or directory that it made, does so from a trap when it is ended, checks each port on Windows, ends the preferences daemon and a policy page's browser by recorded number and start time, and installs pypdf only from the wheel that its line names.
- Fixed: (agent-setup run, U0.7d) The web trial's mark (tools/r3-trials/web, `checks::Mark`, `checks::end_runs_own`) takes a process as the run's own only when one of its arguments is exactly `--user-data-dir=<profile>` or `--database=<profile>/Crashpad`, compared as a path by the rule of each system, with the Windows command line split by the system's rule (`checks::bears_profile`, contracts.md A11.1, CP-189 G1), where it took any argument that held the profile followed by a separator, a quote or a space; `checks::tree_of` and the relay's guard still take the first process and every descendant by its parent and start time without a test of its arguments, and an adopted process only when it bears the mark. Every test target of the trial's crate runs with a fence on from its start (`procs::fence`, CP-161 (3), CP-215 G1: the crate's dev-dependency on itself gives its own tests the feature `test-fence`, and each target holds a test that the fence is on) at the point where `procs::kill` and `procs::ask_end` send their signal, which admits only a process that the test started, by number and start time, with its descendants, or a program below the test's own directory, and otherwise sends nothing and fails at the call; the trial's program fences itself when `R3_WEB_FENCE_BELOW` names a directory below its own temporary or target directory, and stays closed for any other value.
- Added: The case `filters` of the seam's conformance suite reads set D under three more ranges, up to the `at` of its seventh entry, up to the `at` of the keystroke of its step back, and up to the pointer's `at` with the kinds `pointer` and `keystroke`, and makes every ranged read of D from the zero position, from the pointer's position and from the position of every other entry up to the keystroke's (C5.13 item 8, U1.2f); conformance_filters_step_back fails eight more wrong servers by these reads, among them two that find the end of a read by a half-open binary search over `at`, with the midpoint rounded down and up, and one that drops a first line below the line before its start, runs seven servers that end a read again in Batches of two lines, and the test of set D's data reads D under loads that move every `at`; a test of suite_controls.rs builds the six pinned expectations from the counts and positions that the fixture transport serves.
- Changed: The seam's conformance suite gives each control a failure text of its own: an oversize reply frame names its message, an open reply that holds records or expiry entries names their counts, an expiry list names the rule it breaks (out of rising order, a segment with no record, a segment of the records with no instant), and an entry below the cursor's position is told from one that does not rise; the expectations of eleven controls in crates/halvo-seam/tests/suite_controls.rs follow (U1.2f).
- Changed: The refusals that run on a sealed tier name no file below their arguments: the `tier-manifest` refusal of halvo-sample's leak and input-copy names the TIER argument as given with ` mismatched=<n>` for listed files that do not hash to their entries, leak refuses a TIER argument that is a symbolic link with `tier-manifest`, also through a linked parent and a trailing separator, the `tier-files` refusal of input-copy names the TIER argument alone and the `output` refusal of input-copy, build and compose the DIR or --out argument alone, the `layers` refusal of input-copy the TIER or --layers argument alone, and the `site-data` refusal of compose, of the fixture server and of check-sites.mjs names a site file as `SITE_DATA <n>`, its number in byte order of the paths given.
- Changed: An ordinary push or pull request no longer runs the gate's self-tests (under sh and under dash): they run when the range changes a file of the gate's list, when the range cannot be read, and in the weekly run. So a break of a gate self-test by the runner's image, its tools or its load is found by the weekly run or by the next change of a listed file, not by the next push (jobtime-8; WS-6). Check 15-gate-self-test reads the range of a push from before to HEAD, of a push whose before is no commit in the clone from the merge base with the default branch (unreadable when HEAD is on that branch), and of a pull request from the first parent of the merge commit; its list is scripts/r1-gate but scripts/r1-gate/mutants.expected, the check itself, scripts/ci/run-ci-d.sh, scripts/ci/self-test-alone.txt, docs/baseline/r1-gate-bounds.txt, .github/workflows/ci.yml and the .gitattributes files of the root, scripts/, docs/ and docs/baseline/, each matched in any case of letters; scripts/r1-gate/mutants.expected and docs/baseline/r1-gate-main.txt are left out because no pass reads them and their own checks read them on every push (30-mutants, and the record line of this check). It prints `gate self-tests: run (<reason>)` with a line of the groups, cases and failing fixtures of each pass, or the two lines `gate self-tests: not run: ...` with the range and the list; it fails when a pass that runs lacks a group's PASS line, prints one twice, prints one whose count of failing fixtures rejected is not above 0, or prints a PASS line marked `[override:` or `[dirty tree;`, and the run.sh stub of its self-test now prints a PASS line with a fixture count for each group. The range read and the record reading run git with every `GIT_` variable of the caller cleared, with no global or system configuration, with replace objects and grafts off, and the diff with `--ignore-submodules=none` and git's own order of names, so no GIT_ variable and no git configuration of the caller, the user, the system or the checkout skips the passes. The record line is read on every run, as before.
- Added: Check 54-tag-needs-store4 (release-4) refuses a tag while any tracked file under crates/halvo-capture/src at HEAD holds a statement that creates the search index events_fts (CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE, with or without IF NOT EXISTS, in any letter case, across line breaks, the name quoted or after a schema), the index that keeps words of a removed event until store-4 takes it out; it reads the commit's own tree with replace refs off, passes every other ref, an absent one and a pull request merge ref among them, and a tree without that directory, and fails on every ref at a link, a submodule or a file that git or awk cannot read under it. On main it finds two such statements: db.rs line 1411 and the pattern of store/mutants.rows line 31.
- Changed: sh scripts/release.sh (release-4) runs check 54-tag-needs-store4 with the coming tag after its refusal on origin and before cargo-release: while a tracked file under crates/halvo-capture/src holds a statement that creates events_fts, --execute stops with the check's line, makes no commit and no tag, changes no tracked file and releases nothing, and --dry-run prints release.sh: --execute would stop here: <the cause> and exits 0. Check 60-release-dry-run asserts that stop on main while such a statement stands (the dry run's line, and --execute refused with the check's line and the clone's status, HEAD, tags and refs as they were), also at minor and major and in a clone whose origin holds HEAD as main, and its commit-and-tag case once store-4 has taken the statements out; its self-test copies into its fixtures every ci.d script that release.sh names.
- Changed: Check 26-daemon-owner-growth (capture run, daemon-1d) holds the bytes in use over all malloc zones: the all_zones_growth of the growth test's heap line (after Settle less at ready) must stand at or under 27,872 bytes, the largest of daemon-1d's hosted readings plus their range (ruling DM-11 (4)); a fall reads as no growth, and a heap line that is missing or holds no whole number fails the check. OWNER_GROWTH_MAX_BYTES stays 8,388,608.
- Changed: (capture run, control-2d) In halvo-control, paths::one_line, which puts a text on one line with each control character, U+2028 and U+2029 as "?" and cuts nothing, is public and built on both systems (ruling CT-14 K5). The status's late_events, on both systems, and a Mac's secure_input_keystrokes are a yes or no and no count, keys_withheld is a yes or no on a Mac too, and ctl prints the three as yes or no; the counts stay as digits in the debug timed run's report (ruling PR-30 PR10). The status reports its password guard's bound in password_guard_bound_ms, which no ctl line prints: a guard other than none must give a bound above zero and none gives none, or the status type refuses the status (rulings PR-30 PR10 and MA-12 G3). The instance lock's take, try_take and read take &DataDir in place of a path, so the lock opens only a path of the whole form that DataDir's constructors give it, and UnixConnector holds the DataDir (ruling CT-14 K1).
- Added: (capture run, control-2d) halvo-control's status carries data_dir_resolved, the folder of a data directory reached through a link, after parent and absent otherwise, on a Mac alone (a Windows status that carries it is refused); ctl prints it as data_dir_resolved=<folder> on one line through paths::one_line, and a name that is not UTF-8 reads U+FFFD (rulings CT-13 point 2 and CT-15). The protocol number stays 1. The page's texts gain keys_withheld on a Mac whose status reads keys_withheld yes, an item and no warning, with the team's default sentence texts::KEYS_WITHHELD_ON_A_MAC, which is the owner's to change (ruling PR-30 PR10). paths::RemovedBy gains AccountHelperDelete, the account helper's delete that removes a saved sign-in once the account service has answered, for the account helper's Keychain row and for ACCOUNT_HELPER_STORE, which now carries it in place of RemoveHalvo (the billing run's P7-104 item 4).
- Fixed: (capture run, control-2d) ctl's lines for a delete say what did not happen: the plain busy (exit 75) and an already_deleted that the client does not take for a delete (exit 1) print "this request deleted nothing" and never that nothing was deleted, and a delete with no reply within its 120 s says that whether it deleted anything is not known (contracts.md C2.6; ruling CT-14). ctl prints a cause at the data folder, a link that leads nowhere, an entry that is no directory or a folder that cannot be read, as that cause with the folder on one line and exit 1, never a raw OS error and never behind "the control channel failed:": the connector reads the instance lock before it answers an error of the socket's directory (ruling CT-15). A data folder whose path has a part longer than 255 bytes reads "the data directory or a folder on its path cannot be read" from the lock's read, the take, the check and ctl, as is_provisioned already read it, and no OS error (ruling CT-15). A folder that ctl can open and not search (mode 0600 or 0400) reads "cannot be read" when the lock is free, a held lock answers the in-use line after the deadline whatever the connect's error, and any error of a delete after its request was sent prints "whether this request deleted anything is not known; read the status" with its cause (ruling CT-16).
- Changed: When the store refuses a batch of captured events three times in a row, at the tries that its retry schedule made due, each time with one of SQLite's codes 1, 2, 12, 18, 19, 20, 21 or 25, Halvo now gives that batch up: its events are wiped, never stored in part, and counted among the lost events, and a timed run's loss line counts them. After three such batches in a row with no good write between them, Halvo gives up no further batch for these codes: it keeps the next one and tries it again until a write succeeds. While that lasts, capture goes on, and events beyond what Halvo can hold (2,560 events or 2 MiB) are counted as lost. A restart may lose up to three more batches. This version writes no log line for a given-up batch and does not stop capture. A store that keeps such a batch and still answers with one of these codes makes the count of lost events too high by that batch, never too low.
- Fixed: A timed run that lost events always prints halvo-capture: lost events=<n> first=<ts> last=<ts> error=<KIND>, also when a good write followed the loss or the system refused the secure-input hold.
- Fixed: The checkpoint and sheet crates close the edges that U2.3b's readers found (U2.4): a `replace` that misses, and the drop of a prepared copy, remove only the file that `prepare` made and that still holds the bytes it checked, and `replace` renames only that file, so a file that another party put at the copy's name stays; the removal finishes, or answers `left` for, what a removal killed between its move aside and its unlink left under the removing name, so no row says `gone` while the bytes lie in the folder; the removal unlinks only the file that it moved aside, and answers `left` with the reason `name` and removes nothing when another file stands under the removing name at the unlink (a limit of A11.3 stays: a second party that gives the moved copy another name in the folder inside one call keeps that name while the call answers `gone`); a row whose folder is absent, as while its volume is not mounted, is undecided and never `gone`; a row whose folder is itself a link is `left` as `link`, and one whose folder or path is relative or holds a parent step is `left` as `not_file`; and `prepare` refuses a relationship of a type that it reads whose namespace before the last slash differs from the format's in letter case alone.
- Changed: `halvo_checkpoint::remove` makes at most three passes, again only while the system refuses a step of the removal, and answers the new `Undecided::Folder` for an absent folder; `removing_name` takes the checkpoint's name alone and builds one removing name for each checkpoint (the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of that name), the same length as before; a `prepare` of a run after a refused `replace` of that workbook in the same process answers `Error::Checkpoint` and writes nothing, after the workbook's own refusals, when its checkpoint has the path that `prepare` built then (also in a directory made again at that path), the same name in the same directory, or the same file (another spelling on a volume that folds case); a `replace` whose miss finds the copy that `prepare` made but cannot read it answers `Error::CopyLeft`; and the scratch folders of halvo-sheet's tests and of halvo-checkpoint's identity scan come from `halvo_test_dir::make`, so 15-r3-test-dirs keeps 44 rows and 57 places.
- Added: A hook of halvo-checkpoint's own tests at the two moments of the races of `remove` (after the checks of the copy, and after its move aside), declared under `cfg(test)` with no public item, feature or variable of the environment, and a test in tests/races.rs that reads the crate's source and fails when the hook can be reached from another build; the seam's tests force a file moved into the checkpoint's place, a write in place and a pass killed before the unlink, beside the two timed race tests.
- Changed: (learning run, U2.6) In halvo-learn-store, the learning handlers read the `learn_edit`, `learn_run` and `learn_routine` rows of a request's workflow by the store's workflow key, through the new `LearnHost` call `for_each_under` (the keyed read of halvo-derived's `Rows::for_each_under`), under the key of the body's workflow or of the run's held start, and read the `learn_run` rows of every workflow only to find a `put_playbook`'s run by its id where they hold no start of the run (after a restart), so a row that its parser refuses stops what rests on its kind in the workflow under whose key the store keeps it and nothing of another workflow, and the interim reading of a refused row's workflow from its text ends (contracts.md L4.2, L4.4; the coordinator's ruling LM-58 (c)); the handlers' test host and learn-gate's memory store keep the workflow key beside each row, the memory store deletes a workflow's rows by that key and the test host bounds a row by the day kept beside it, the routine fixtures take the newest row through `newest_routine`, and learn-gate's two privacy test places take their directories from `halvo_test_dir::make`.
- Fixed: Refinements::parse of halvo-schema refuses a reply of more than CHECKPOINTS_REPLY_MAX bytes (261,067), counted with its white space and escapes before it reads anything, with the rule reply_bytes, where it read a reply of 261,121 bytes whole (the learning run's ruling LM-47, unit U1.5); the test T-C of tests/r5_kinds.rs homes the witness 1 under learn_edit's v, a Version, and reads its domain witness with SiteDomain::parse, so that a widening of the edit kind's entry fails it after a witness was also changed (ruling LM-48); 20-r5-rule's self-test empties the artifacts file of LM-77's two fixtures before each run and runs them twice over one artifacts directory, and its closed list holds 80 names (rulings LM-79 item 1 and LM-85).
- Changed: `route-gate import` refuses a driver block whose attempt lines do not match its `drive` and `reply` lines (routing run U0.2c): for every model, five attempt lines for each counted `drive` line and one `write` line for each `reply` line, else `source` with `driver:<line>`. Such a refusal means that the trial file lost or gained a line of a cell: rebuild it from the trial's raw files, never by hand. A `drive` cell of the verdict's driver or a `reply` cell of its writer that is missing whole is refused by the set; any other cell missing whole still imports with its rows fewer, and a cell that ends `notrun` after its compile with no `drive` line is refused until the agent-setup run answers whether it writes one. The golden case no_driver is now one whole cell (rows=5).
- Fixed: A run of the backend's node tests that is stopped half-way no longer leaves wrangler dev, its node child, esbuild and workerd running (billing run, unit 4.12, ruling P7-101 Addition 1 A4, round P7-118): the test kit (backend/test/ledger/process-group.ts) ends the process groups that its process started when that process gets TERM, INT or HUP, when its channel to vitest's main process closes and when it exits; it marks each group under backend/.tmp/groups with its owner and its checkout, and the node tests' new global set-up ends, before any test, the groups that an earlier run of the same checkout left after KILL, by their marks alone, never a group of another checkout, of the sweeping process or whose leader started before the mark's owner; endGroup and the handlers signal a group only while its leader has the recorded pid and start time, and KILL the members that its TERM left; test/contract/wrangler.node.test.ts starts wrangler dev through the kit; the kit sends wrangler TERM, on which wrangler removes its build under .wrangler/tmp beside its config (the stop case reads that for a config in its own directory; INT and HUP go to the vitest run), and backend/.wrangler/ stays ignored by git; test/ledger/stopped-run.node.test.ts stops runs half-way and counts what is left, and test/ledger/process-group-rules.node.test.ts holds the kit's rules on a recorded process table.
- Changed: backend/README.md says that 21-r7-backend-test runs the workerd tests, the node tests and then the records of check 50, and what a stopped run of the node tests leaves, where, and which limits the test kit has.
- Added: halvo-secrets has `Store::delete_if_holds`, which removes the item only while it holds the bytes that its caller read and keeps any other item, whose bytes its answer `Other` gives: from a file it reads the file under the name and compares before it claims, so other bytes answer `Other` and nothing is claimed, and with the caller's bytes it claims the file by one rename, compares what it claimed once more and names back a file that was replaced in between; from the Keychain it finds the item with its bytes and a reference, removes that item by its reference and looks again, because the library that the crate uses drops the status of the system's delete; on macOS it reads first and answers by the table of the delete, and a store that keeps its file alone (`Store::in_directory`, the DPAPI store of Windows) puts back a claim that a stopped operation left before each operation. It also has `Store::remove`, which at the person's word removes the item of one named store (`StoreKind::File` or `StoreKind::Keychain` on macOS) and then reads, so the state with other bytes in both stores, which answered `Locked::OtherStore` for good, has a way out from a build that can remove the item of the store that it names; between two test binaries on the hosted macOS image, a build without the feature `keychain` could neither read (-25293) nor remove (-25244) the Keychain item that a build with the feature made, so from that build every read answers `Locked::OtherStore` while that item stands; macOS documents two ways to remove such an item, by the build that made it or by the person in the system's Keychain Access, neither tested here, and this build does not try another way. A removal of the file at the person's word also removes a claim that cannot be named back and the temporary file of a killed write, and after it removed a credential it answers `Deleted` or a failure, never `NotStored`. Every operation that finds no file under the store's name, the removal and the move included, lists the store directory once more and answers `NotStored` only when neither the name nor a claim stands there, and otherwise looks once more and then answers `Fault(-1)`; a write to a file lists the store directory after it publishes and answers `Fault(-1)` when a claim stands there, so no write stores a second credential beside one that a claim held, but in the two windows that the doc of `Store` names (unit 7.8, rulings P7-56, P7-109 and P7-112).
- Changed: after `deleted` or `no-such-account` the account helper's delete makes one call of the store, `Store::delete_if_holds` with the sign-in that the run sent, in place of a second read and a delete by name, so a sign-in that another run stores after that read is never removed; its lines and exit codes are as before. Every operation of `Store::in_directory` and of the DPAPI store now lists the store directory first, so on macOS a store directory that can be searched and written but not listed (mode 0300) answers `Locked::Refused` with code 13 to a read, a write and a delete, where the read of unit 7.2 used the file (unit 7.8, rulings P7-63 D1 and P7-112).
- Fixed: halvo-secrets reads a file whose link count is 0 right after its open, one that another process removed while it was open, as a file that is not there and no longer as `Locked::HardLink`; ending the claims that a stopped move or `delete_if_holds` left passes over a claim that its owner removed or named back in the meantime, where it answered `Locked::HardLink` or `Fault` with code 2; in the build with the Keychain, a claim that the store cannot settle, one beside a taken name or one that cannot be read, is now the answer of a read, a write and a delete, which change nothing, where a read gave the Keychain item and a delete removed it beside other bytes in the file; and a write to a file no longer answers `Stored` beside a claim that holds another credential (unit 7.8, rulings P7-109 S2 and S3, and P7-112).
- Added: (billing run, F.6, ruling P7-96 points 2 and 3, ruling P7-116) The pnpm step of scripts/prerequisites.sh compares, on every run and before that pnpm runs, the file that runs for a pnpm in the step's prefix (~/.local/bin/pnpm or ~/.local/pnpm), followed through its links and through npm's shell shim, byte for byte with the binary inside the checked platform package; when they differ, npm installs pnpm over it and the step compares again, and it fails with one line that names both and the way on when the bytes differ, when the prefix holds no such file or when the path finds another pnpm. The compare and its lookup of pnpm use the PATH that the step was given without each entry that is the same directory as the prefix or its bin directory by the shell's same-file test, so no tool of the prefix takes part in it, and the step puts the prefix on its PATH only after the compare has passed. On a hosted runner of GitHub Actions (GITHUB_ACTIONS=true, RUNNER_ENVIRONMENT=github-hosted) the step fails when it finds a pnpm of the pinned version that the same run did not install, and --help prints the usage with the named limit that a pnpm of the pinned version on a person's PATH outside the prefix is used as it is.
- Added: halvo-derived (workflow run, U5.2): the privacy tests of the derived store, privacy_window, privacy_bounded, privacy_kept, privacy_scrub, privacy_redact and privacy_listed, the delete of everything with no database file and no key file left, the ciphertext check, a second process refused also with the lock file unlinked, own_origin_refused, a proposal for a stored workflow, a routine across the window and the summary ahead of a clock set back, with a keyless open and the delete of everything in the test host (feature test-host), the page-gap probe over the six checkpointed removals and the structural check of halvo-sqlcipher's tests on derived's own files with its two controls, each test on the list of 50-derived.
- Changed: halvo-derived (workflow run, U5.2): the test of the proposal check, m42, is named a_proposal_that_names_a_summary_after_the_clock_s_day_or_past_its_bound_is_refused and its doc comment states the check as the ruling DS-5 (a) sets it, and its line on the list of 50-derived follows the name; the open of the derived store refuses by name a linked database that is not Halvo's mended build (`halvo_sqlcipher::page_gap_mend_linked`), an `auto_vacuum` other than 0 and a page size other than 4,096 (ruling S-85, item 5); the check 15-r3-test-dirs holds no row for the two places of halvo-derived that U5.2 mended, with its pins lowered.
- Added: halvo-derived (workflow run, U5.1d): the index rows_read_workflow on the kind, the workflow column and the body for every family but `workflow`, so that the keyed row read walks the rows of one workflow alone, with the schema's number still 1 (S-67); the names `StoredFault::DroppedWorkflowKey` and `StoredFault::Family`; the checkpointed writer, which alone has the function that removes rows by a selection, so that a call of it under a plain write does not compile, while a support row that a new proposal drops is removed under the plain write, as S-80 allows (S-82, S-83); and tests: the guard for workflows with an agent in every kept state and under keys with and without a site, the open's refusals, the plans of the keyed read and its steps, the read-back of the open's settings, a count of the sync calls with the full flag, which holds `fullfsync`, a check of the crate's text that no source runs ANALYZE or PRAGMA optimize, and tests/replaced.rs, where a replaced value and a dropped support row leave no marker in the files after the next checkpointed call, the delete and the close (S-80, item 3).
- Changed: halvo-derived (workflow run, U5.1d): a proposal with the key of a kept workflow whose agent is set, in any state, no longer changes that workflow, its support rows or the revision and is counted in `unchanged`, so that only a decision moves the plan of a workflow that an agent does (C2.4, S-64); an open now refuses, by name and with no row changed, an entry that gives no workflow key for a kind whose stored rows name a workflow and an entry that gives a stored kind another family (C3.9); the open sets `synchronous` to FULL and `fullfsync` and `checkpoint_fullfsync` on and refuses a store where one of them or `temp_store` reads back otherwise (S-78, S-74 (e)); the open gives a bounded row with a day and no day of going its day plus the days of its entry (S-74, P2); and the open refuses a database that an earlier build made, whose schema lacks the new index.
- Fixed: The runner test a_close_of_input_after_the_edits_load_lets_at_most_its_propose_out closes the runner's input on the answer to the checkpoint's `made` row, the last request before the edit's `propose`: since the checkpoint rows came between the edit's load and its `propose`, the close after the load let the checkpoint's `planned` row out, which the test took for a forbidden frame and failed on hosted runners when that row won the race with the end of file; a failed close test now names each frame that came after the close.
- Added: The store's delete tail on a data directory with a closed layout, `EncryptedStore::delete_data_dir(dir, Layout)` with `Layout::DataDirectory` and `Layout::ScratchStore`, which opens the store directory and the roots directory from the handle of the directory that holds each by their fixed names; `RootsHandle` and `FileIdentity`, and the custody calls `wrap_store_key_in` and `unwrap_store_key_in` through a held roots directory; `RootsLeft::at_its_path`, and `RootsLeft::others_left`, an `OthersLeft` that counts by kind (`root_shaped`, `directories`, `other`), and names none of, the entries that stay in the roots folder at a name that is no root name and no partial name (store-3b).
- Changed: The store holds its roots directory for its life (taken at the open before the unwrap, and at the create after create_root) and keeps the identity of its root file; the unwrap, the wrap, the open's cleanup, abandon_create and the root step of delete-all go through that handle, a root moved inside it is destroyed where it lies when the name at which it stands lets the step write through it, and a delete whose held roots directory no longer stands at its path answers DeleteIncomplete; a root renamed there to a name that is no root name and no partial name while it has a second name outside the folder is removed with no write through it, the delete answers DeleteIncomplete with the root left, the tail after it answers Ok, and the file keeps its key at its outside name (ruling ST-48 U2). The made mark records the root file's identity, and a cleanup by it acts on the file of that identity alone and writes through it only where its name lets the step write; a made mark without one reads owed. On Windows the held roots directory cannot be renamed while a store is open (store-3b).
- Removed: `EncryptedStore::delete_store_dir(store_dir, roots)`, its walk of the roots path's parts (ruling ST-46 K2), and the source test of ruling ST-46 K8 in crates/halvo-capture/tests/source_rules.rs with its entry in check 30 (store-3b).
- Fixed: The handle's delete-all no longer destroys the root files of a directory put at its roots directory's old name and reads its own root destroyed while the moved root still unwraps (ruling ST-46 K6); a root file renamed aside after the step's listing is destroyed where it lies; a link put at the store name after the tail's look no longer leads the tail's sweep into another store's roots; an open's cleanup no longer destroys the root that store.key names under the name of the mark's root; a cleanup whose partial root file cannot be destroyed answers an error (the review's N7); a create refused at an entry of its root id publishes no root to pick its answer (the review's F1); a root write whose partial name another writer holds no longer zero-fills and removes that writer's file (ruling S-95 (f)); no delete answers Ok while a regular file that can be a root file stands in the roots folder at any name, but for a later delete-all on a handle that already answered Ok, which replays that Ok and reads no folder (ruling ST-45 H3), so that a file put into the roots folder after that answer is destroyed by the next tail and not by that handle: wherever the root step sweeps it removes every regular file there and zero-fills one at a name that is no root name and no partial name only when it has one name and a root file's length, which it reads by the name before any change of mode, so another party's file with a second name keeps its bytes and its mode (ruling ST-48 U1), removes a link as the entry that it is, and enters no directory, which keeps every delete DeleteIncomplete while it stands, and on Windows the listing reaches a name that ends in a dot or a space through the verbatim form of the held directory's path (ruling ST-47 T1); the scratch tail given a store path that is a link no longer removes the link and answers Ok while the store behind it reads, and a data directory, a store path or a roots path spelled `link/`, `link/.` or `link//` is refused as `link` is, not followed and with nothing removed (ruling ST-47 T2 and T3); a create over a live store answers AlreadyExists before the cleanup of create.pending, so a made mark written by hand no longer makes it destroy the live store's root (ruling ST-47 T4); a made mark written by hand no longer makes the next open, the tail or the next create write through another party's file at a name in the roots folder where the step would not write through it by that name: a name that is no root name and no partial name, or a root name when the file is not of a root file's length; at a partial name, or at a root name with a root file's length, it still does (the named limit of a planted file) (ruling ST-48 U2); and the answer of a delete counts by kind, and no longer names, what stays in the roots folder at a name that is no root name and no partial name (ruling ST-48 U3) (store-3b).
- Fixed: (capture run, daemon-1b) The two parse tests of halvo-capture's daemon that take a gate mode are built with debug assertions alone, so the lib's daemon tests pass in a release build (ruling DM-12 P5); a gate mode whose account gives no data directory to check DIR against exits 1 with a line that says so, where it exited 64 with a line about DIR and the usage text (P7); the binary installs the SIGINT and SIGTERM flag, and the umask of 0o077 that comes with it, for the timed run alone, so SIGTERM ends a verify mode by the system's default action, where the mode ran on to its end and exited 0 (P3); the release test that check 26 runs, a_release_build_refuses_the_three_gate_modes_as_usage, also holds that a release build refuses the gate flags --hold-secure-input, --switch and --marker and the scratch delete as usage with exit 64 (ruling DM-11 (5)).
- Added: (capture run, daemon-1b) A debug build's `halvo-capture --delete-all --store-dir DIR` (contracts.md C4 item 9): it refuses with exit 64 a DIR that reaches the account's data directory, also through a link, compared by resolved paths, takes no instance lock, and makes one call of the store's delete tail in the scratch layout on DIR as given; it prints `store_dir=<DIR>` and then `no_store` when the tail answered Ok, nothing named a root and no name was a link or an entry of another kind, or `deleted` with the seven keys whose values the tail's report holds, segments_shredded, keys_destroyed, root_destroyed, root_id, files_left, database_left and not_followed (a data directory's entries_removed, entries_left, unknown_entries and intent have no value in a scratch store directory and are left out); a fault of DIR's text (relative, or with a `..` part) is usage with exit 64 before the account is read; before the tail's call it refuses with exit 64 and one line on standard error a DIR that holds a data directory's layout, an entry at the name store beside one at the name roots, read through DIR as resolved, a link included, and without following a link at either name, and opens, changes and removes nothing in it, so that a gate trap given a wrong folder cannot cost a person's store its root (ruling DM-22); a DIR in which those two names cannot be read is refused the same way with a line of its own (ruling DM-22 Addition 1); it exits 0 only when the tail answered Ok, 1 when the delete is not finished, with what stays on standard error (a root file by its name in the folder roots, any other entry there by kind alone), or, when the tail could not open DIR, a line that says so and claims nothing about what stays, and 75 while another process holds the store; a DIR that is a link or a file, in any spelling (`link/`, `link/.`, `link//`), is refused by the tail, nothing is removed, and it exits 1 with `halvo-capture: <DIR> is a link or a file, not a folder; nothing was removed; give the path of the folder itself`; after a run that destroyed its own root while its store.key stays, as `--verify-store` and the timed run do, the first delete removes the store's files and exits 1 with `root_destroyed=no`, because no tail answers destroyed for a root that the call did not find and remove (ruling ST-46 K1), and the next prints `no_store` and exits 0; the row scratch-delete-keeps-the-root of crates/halvo-capture/src/daemon/mutants.rows answers without the tail's call and is caught by daemon::tests::a_scratch_delete_destroys_the_root_through_its_one_tail_call.
- Fixed: Check 51-macos-stall prints, for a test that exits 101, its panic line and the lines after it, and keeps each test's whole output under tests/ of its artifacts directory, which the macos job uploads. In tests/macos_stall_fixture.rs a reply of the fixture that does not come within its wait, or a fixture output that ended, makes the round a round not driven: its round line names which, it never counts and is never a pass, a key recorded in it still fails its phase, letters landed in the secure field fail nothing by themselves, and the two marker releases of a round whose last reply was lost after its markers record nothing; in the short form a phase passes with one held round and no failed round, and its rounds not driven are printed by wait; in the long form every wait of each mended phase needs a held round (the return phase keeps its three-way verdict of ruling MA-23), or that wait reads not driven and the ledger takes no record from that run, because on the hosted runners a round is often not driven through no fault of the product and a short form that failed on that turned two landing jobs red; a phase whose every round was not driven reads not driven, never a pass; the round lines and 51's lines print each phase's counted rounds and rounds not driven (rulings JB-36, PR-32, MA-27). refusal_or_none of the macOS hold builds under cfg(any(debug_assertions, test)), so the release test build of halvo-capture's library no longer warns that it is never used (ruling JB-37).
- Changed: Check 51-macos-stall's ledger (docs/baseline/ledger.d/macos-2b.txt) binds the lines of crates/halvo-capture/Cargo.toml that name the feature stall-seam, each with its table and with a features list read as its stall-seam alone (so win-3's added test-seam beside it holds), in place of that file's blob, so a change of another line of the manifest owes no long-form run; the other eight files of the hold stay bound by their blobs.
- Security: `scripts/r1/ci.d/19-home-paths.sh` (hook-3, inbox entry 121 (d)) also runs the copies of `scripts/check-home-paths.sh` and `scripts/commit-range.sh` that the default branch holds, taken from the commit that `refs/remotes/origin/<default branch>` names by its object id, or on a push to the default branch from the commit before the push, on the same range beside the copies of the branch, so a pull request that weakens its scanner or its range file no longer passes its own home-path check; the list of allowed paths stays the one of the scanned commit, so an entry that a pull request adds holds for both copies, and a full clone of a workflow run without the default branch fails. A pull request whose head commit holds another `19-home-paths.sh` than the default commit, by object id, is also read by the default branch's copy of the whole check, which runs after the default copies of the scanner and before the branch's scanner; the default copies are read and run before the branch's scanner, and the default branch's copy of the whole check runs the branch's scanner itself, as its own, after its own default copies have run, with replace objects off, and the run fails when the clone holds a replace ref or a grafts file or when the default ref or HEAD moved while the branch's scanner ran; a shallow clone fetches the commit before a push by its id. Named limits: 19-home-paths, the driver and the workflow run as the pull request holds them (a pull request that changes the lines of 19 that start the default copies passes, and so does a run whose workflow or driver sets HOME_PATHS_CHECK_OF_THE_DEFAULT_BRANCH); the branch's code of the step before, its self-tests, runs in the same clone first and can leave a process behind or write the repository, and the branch's 19 runs as the work tree holds it, which the branch's scanner can write while 19 runs; a default commit without 19 skips the default branch's copy of the check with a line that says why; and a change that weakens the scanner or the range file without adding a home path passes both copies and then is the default branch's copy; the review of their diffs is the guard.
- Fixed: On a push to the default branch, `scripts/r1/ci.d/19-home-paths.sh` (hook-3, inbox entry 121 (b)) reads every commit of the push, from the before commit that the event file of `GITHUB_EVENT_PATH` gives, as the step Commit messages does, and no longer the newest commit alone; it reads every commit of the head when that before commit is all zeros or not in the clone, and fails when the event file is missing or gives no object id. A shallow clone, as the windows job checks out, still reads the head commit. On a run that a pushed tag started, the ask and the fetch of the tag at origin each end after 120 s without an answer (inbox entry 121 (e)): the check ends the git process that it started, by its pid, and fails with tag=remote-could-not-be-asked and a line that says to run the job again or to fetch and scan the tag on a clone, in place of holding the job to its own limit. A scheduled run, whose event holds no repository, takes the default branch from GITHUB_REF.
- Fixed: (capture run, mutants-6) The mutant row `late-key-not-counted` of crates/halvo-capture/src/macos/mutants.rows changes the late-key count of `admitted` in crates/halvo-capture/src/macos/input.rs, the line that its test reaches; since the stop's own count in `withheld_at_a_stop` came above it (MA-24 S2) the row had changed that line, which its test does not read, and main's budget run of 30-mutants failed on it. The new row `ma24-late-key-at-a-stop-not-counted` changes the count in `withheld_at_a_stop` and is caught by `a_late_key_at_a_stop_still_counts_as_a_late_event`. The row `lag-judged-by-the-gap-bound` of the same file, whose pattern also matched a line of the stop's own branch in `on_tap_event` first, changes the lag bound of the normal path alone, the line that its test reaches.
- Changed: An account delete ends the plan at the provider once no hold of a request is open (a hold lasts at most 30 s) and no send of the usage is in flight, makes one cancel call at a time for one account while the call is inside its bound, stores the call in flight (its id and begin, in a new table cancel_call) and then the answered cancel and then the decision of the removal, and asks again from 60 s to one hour with no end while the cancel goes unanswered; status answers closing until the cancel is answered. Five new log lines name no account: cancel-before-send, one for each interval not yet acknowledged when the cancel is made, written again at each cancel try, and a counted interval may still be on the final invoice; cancel-unanswered, at most once an hour while the pause is capped or a subscription stays unended; cancel-call-unsettled, when a cancel call outlives its bound; removal-overdue, at each failing run while the removal is past its bound; destroy-error, in place of alarm-error, for an error of a module call or of the due time that destroy catches. An interval that cancel-before-send counted may be counted again by usage-not-billable when it is closed at the removal's bound.
- Fixed: A begun account delete is finished by the account object itself also when a step of the alarm or of the delete throws: under the mark the alarm never stops and pauses from 30 s, doubling to one hour, between failing runs, with the count of failing runs restarting at a delete, written by a statement of its own and kept in memory, so that the pause stays at 30 s (at most 2,881 runs a day, fewer when a cancel try is owed later) while the count of failing runs cannot be stored, because its one statement fails or every read of the skeleton fails, and the object restarts at each run; outside the mark five alarm runs that catch an error of the due time end with alarm-stopped, while the entries still answer; when the run's read of the mark at its end fails as well, the alarm does not stop and runs on at the pause; the stored data is removed when the usage reports settle or at the latest 24 hours after the answered cancel for the account's current subscription id, and within a minute after a removal that failed or stopped; and check 70 counts the 33 samples of the wire.
- Fixed: An account delete ends every subscription on the person's customer record at the provider, not only the one that Halvo stored: one port call cancels the stored subscription, reads the customer, lists its subscriptions with no status filter and cancels, page by page, each one that has not ended, and the delete ends only when the customer's read shows it deleted at the provider, or on a whole list read after the last cancel; when that list has more than one page, its first page is read again and must show nothing that has not ended. The stored subscription goes first on every try until a call ends it or reads it ended, or until a second try finds it unknown at the provider; an unknown answer counts only in a try whose read of the customer showed the key to be the right one, the first such answer answers failed and the object keeps it, and the second, in a later try, settles the id and writes cancel-stored-unknown; no later try makes a request for a settled id. An id that cannot be sent in a request gets no request and throws nothing. A fault of the provider or of the list, a customer that the provider does not know or shows in the other mode, and a customer id with no provider address or key answer failed and keep the data. One call makes at most 20 requests and starts none with less than 500 ms of its 10 s left. A failed call counts as ended only the cancels that its last whole list no longer shows, and a call that stops before a last whole list counts every cancel that it made; it also counts the subscriptions that it saw and could not end; a failed call that ended a subscription tries again after 60 s; cancel-unanswered is written at most once an hour while the pause is capped or a subscription stays unended, and new skeleton columns hold the settled stored id, the first unknown answer and the time of the last such line; the record of the call in flight is one per account; manage answers unavailable while the account closes; the log gains cancel-customer-unknown, cancel-list-fault and cancel-stored-unknown.
- Added: `AgentRun::new` in halvo-schema is the one maker of the stored `agent_run` record for tests and tools: every count 0 and `playbook`, `model` and each key of the run's list null until the caller sets it, its text written by the record's own serializer in the order of the golden lines. The product's two writers (crates/halvo-agent-store/src/end.rs and crates/halvo-runner/src/run.rs) build the type by a struct literal, which a new key stops compiling. The tests of halvo-agent-store and halvo-agent-client build their records with it. The check `25-r3-run-record` fails for a record built by hand in the forms that it reads, which its --help lists with the forms that it does not read; the learning run's four files and the workflow run's frozen entry are known rows, and a known row passes when its pins rise in the same diff, where the review of the diff is the gate.
- Changed: The supervisor library asks its caller's `Secrets` for a saved sign-in with the agent of the run that asked, as the library started that run and never a value of a frame (`Secrets::read(agent, name)`), so that the app can answer that nothing is stored for a name that this agent's own set-up does not list. The refusal itself is the app's `Secrets`, which is not built yet: this change carries the agent to it, and until it is built nothing stops one agent from reading a sign-in that the person linked for another. The test stand-in records the agent of every read, and a test with two live runs that each ask two names, where one run holds a value under both and the other under one alone, shows that every read carries the agent of the run that made it.
- Changed: Agents of the categories `utility_bill_payment` and `shop_order` have 40 steps, 300 s and 0 model tokens, the values that the verdict line `driver=none path=B writer=none` of `docs/r3/trials/driver.md` gives when the file holds no `limits` line, and the test `limits_from_trial` of halvo-policy computes every row of the table `LIMITS` again from that file; `check_playbook` refuses a step that writes a file other than the slot `workbook` with the cause `workbook` and the step's number, so that no such step is stored and the runner ends such a run at its load.
- Fixed: (agent-setup run, U0.6b) The driver trial's tools (tools/r3-trials/drive): the reply rule counts no body that names a host or a name with a port followed by a path or glued to other text before it, a dotted name with a port, a host in letters of any script, with combining marks or with an xn-- last label, an IPv4 or IPv6 address, or an at-sign in its small, full-width or written form, also with a character of the Unicode categories Cf, Mn or Me inside it or with a dot or hyphen look-alike that the trial's README names; `verdict.py --check` refuses every pair of files that `route-gate import` refuses, with the importer's detail, and its self-test holds the importer's own cases; the reply rule reads the senders of `reply_senders` through halvo-schema's reader and selects nothing for a list that the reader refuses; and every test target of the trial's crate runs with the fence of r3-web on, with a test that fails when it is off and a lint that refuses a target or a manifest table that it cannot read. The crate's tests still run in no job of main's workflow (CP-173's named limit).
- Added: The card of an agent shows an entry for a committed effect that it cannot show (run.effect.<class>.unshown, with no field and no kept value) and one for each thing not done (run.effect.<class>.not_done, or .refused where the run's own ledger holds the person's no for that effect and digest); a confirmation names the agent that asks and its place among the page confirmations; the row of an agent whose workflow is gone stays while its run lives, stopping with no control, or line.working.kill_stuck; the words lint refuses two controls of a confirmation class that differ only in case or spacing; and the words of the round (U2.4d).
- Changed: A run's line on the card takes the cause of the coordinator's own run_ended row where only the coordinator gives it (lapsed, cancelled and its other stops), and a declined run with no refusal of the person has a neutral line; the choices of a second step say what follows each; the line of the hold failures says how to try again; agent_history's effects carry their effect id and digest (U2.4d).
- Security: Origin::parse refuses the number forms of an IPv4 address that a browser reads as another address, and the coordinator declines, with no item, a second step or an account-link handback for an http site outside 127.0.0.0/8 (U2.4d).
- Changed: In halvo-coord (interface run, U2.4p, rulings RD-97 item 4, RD-100 and RD-102 item 2), the drive files' items that the comment passes of U2.3b and U2.4b marked RESHAPE carry names and types that say their rules (for example `newest_closing_row`, `log_or_reread_once`, `MoveContext`, `answer_row_closes_item` and the enum `ItemLapse` of a stop), and a Skip on the ready card of a queued agent drops its queued start in the same move, also where the Skip is answered `stale` because the owner stored the Start's answer with a lost acknowledgement; `Report.dropped` names the agent.
- Fixed: In halvo-coord (interface run, U2.4p, ruling RD-96 item 5 (b)), a raise of an item closes, in its own body and before its `item_raised`, the open items of its kind that it finds for the agent from two sources: the raises of `ready`, `setup` and `check`, and the check that an answer raises, write the `item_lapsed` that the board owes for an earlier life's item of that kind, and the raise of a run's item writes the `app_ended` lapse of each open item of its kind that another run raised, as the ledger's events and reads show it; two open items of one kind of the raising run itself stay as they are.
- Fixed: In halvo-coord (interface run, U2.4f, rulings RD-64 1.4, 1.8, 2.1 to 2.3), recovery reads each agent alone and sets aside an agent whose history the owner refuses, so one unreadable record no longer leaves every agent with no board; a start whose run_started was refused with a name other than seq asks for no further defer until a store of the agent is acknowledged; the items' ledger, the debts and recovery's body send by the refusal's class, keeping, sending one by one, taking off and counting, or keeping whole, a binding of a workflow that the owner's list shows dismissed being class B in each of them (RD-122 C1), recovery's body cut between debts by records and bytes; a waiting record's day is set again from its instant before each send; an owed run_ended or item_lapsed whose log meets seq reads through read_back, so it closes the lost starts that its read brings (RD-96 item 5 (c)); and a run that a tick stopped with deleted and whose workflow is listed again before its end stores no run_ended deleted, a row that halvo-schema refuses (U2.4c's point 2); a proof write refused with no_workflow drops both records instead of keeping them waiting; and recovery, a tick and a new life's first creation move raise no check and no setup for an agent set aside or a dismissed agent kept with no event read, so that nothing is logged for an agent whose history was never read (RD-121); a dismissed agent kept with no event read is logged for by no tick and no move of the person, and an agent set aside whose dismissed workflow answers binding is kept unread (RD-122 C1, C2); a proof takes off only the record that it was set for, and a proof write refused with a name of class D holds the place while one that fails with no refusal counts nothing (RD-122 C3); and a slotted agent whose record is stuck by its own refused starts is tried again by a tick, so that it starts once the store takes rows again, the stop for the life staying where a place is held or the refusal cannot pass in this life, until a store of the agent is acknowledged (RD-123, RD-124 W2); and a start that the owner refuses with no_workflow stops the tick's start for the life and counts nothing toward the stuck line, so that no line shows (RD-125 (1)).
- Added: In halvo-coord (interface run, U2.4f, rulings RD-64 2.5 and 2.6), the limit record_stuck_ticks (3) in Limits and formal/r4-coordinator/limits.json, the board's count of each agent's records taken off with the names of the last one, its count of ticks in a row with a refusal of class C or D, and roster::status_of with the line problem.record for a stuck record, which no page shows at this head: compose still calls roster::status, and the unit that lands second wires the line, its words and the dropped count; and, by rulings RD-86 item 7, RD-119 and RD-120, the two defer values of each run on its run_ended, defer_launch (whether the defer of its launch was acknowledged) and defer_ticks_failed (the ticks at which it stood on the board and no defer was acknowledged), both null where this life did not see the run whole.
- Changed: In halvo-coord (interface run, U2.4f, rulings RD-73 7 and RD-104 (a)), Limits::PRODUCT takes 20 records and 16,384 bytes for one body, equal to halvo-schema's REQUEST_RECORDS and REQUEST_BYTES, and Limits::from_json refuses a file above them, formal/r4-coordinator/limits.json holding the same 20 and 16,384 (RD-122 C4); Owner::history returns the reply's last, which the three senders take as the stored last, also where it is lower (RD-64 3.2); a confirmation's answer of either presenter reads the owner's events before it goes to the runner, and where the read shows a stored switch-off the run stops first and gets no answer of that press, and where the read fails nothing is sent (RD-101 item 5 (b)); and halvo-coord's row text and halvo-schema's writer write defer_launch and defer_ticks_failed after origin on every coord_event row, null where unset, and the coord_event kind entry lists both, so the store's commit refuses a row of the eighteen keys of before (RD-120).
- Removed: In halvo-coord (interface run, U2.4f, ruling RD-69), the Clock::today overrides of Watch and of six test wrappers, which no code of the crate called.
- Changed: (capture run, jobtime-10) The hosted test of a stale Keychain reference between two processes (account/crates/halvo-secrets/tests/hosted.rs, `a_stale_reference_between_two_processes_keeps_the_later_item`) makes the parent's search before its delete once, with no retry and no pause: in every round of every hosted run all 20 tries of that search were refused with -67701, and its answer is printed and decides nothing. The looks that decide a round, the child's, the control's and the one after the delete, keep their 20 tries, with no pause after the last. The 200 rounds took about 2.7 s each, and the hosted target took 540 to 546 s of 10-r7-account on hosted macOS; the rounds, the child per round and every assertion stay.
- Fixed: 30-mutants names its own time limit of 1,900 s with a `# limit: 1900` line (capture run, jobtime-11, ruling WS-7), so that a push to main, which runs the budget pass (it starts no row after 240 s) after the apply-only pass, no longer fails it at the default limit of 1,200 s, as main's push run 44151327847 did at 1,208 s; the driver's own case limit-lines-of-the-repository and scripts/ci/README.md name three scripts with a limit line.
- Changed: Give each CI job a 150-minute timeout in place of 120 minutes (capture run, jobtime-11, ruling JB-52): the macOS job has stood within minutes of its limit of 7,200 s for five landings, and one slow runner or one dropped and rerun job costs two hours each time; this buys room while the seconds of the checks are cut, and it changes nothing that is checked. The bound ci_job_timeout_min in docs/baseline/r1-gate-bounds.txt, the pinned bounds of check-cost's self-test and ADR 0011 read 150 with it.
