- Added: In halvo-agent-store (agent-setup run, U3.4, rulings CP-130 (a), CP-135 (1a), CP-141 (2), CP-143 (2), CP-153 (6) (d), CP-154 (2) and (3), CP-160 (3), CP-163 (1), CP-171 (10), CP-178 (3)), the handlers of reply_row and load_replies: reply_row stores a message's reply rows of one agent in the closed order of A7.4 and refuses with effect every other row, and a begun row while another agent holds begun, committed or unknown for the message; load_replies answers the agent's newest row of each id asked and, by the same function, the ids that another agent's rows block, never a shorter list; a run's end carries each begun reply row of the run to unknown in the commit of its record; create_agent stores the agent's start row, which load answers under start; MemoryStore::set_clock and MemoryStore::remove_workflow for tests.
- Changed: In halvo-agent-store (agent-setup run, U3.4, rulings CP-143 (4) (ii), CP-205 (b), CP-218 (c), CP-227), put_playbook answers a write to a file that is not the slot workbook with playbook instead of class; effect refuses a releasing row when the newest configuration gives refuse; start_run stores no second start row for a run whose start row lies past the log's bound, on a host whose read still hands out that start row, and a machine whose clock moves by days (a clock read three days back or 93 days on) can let start_run open a run again, a named limit of the class of the effects' clock limit; a clock set back more than two days past a run's end lets a begun reply row of the ended run be stored and stay begun, a named limit; a kept row that holds day is held to the day of its at and to the request's day.
- Fixed: In halvo-agent-store (agent-setup run, U3.4, ruling CP-205 (b)), checkpoint stored a made row whose instant was before its planned row's; it refuses it with effect now.
- Fixed: More tests of halvo-runner fail on every run where a chance order or the time hid a fault (U4.10c): the test of the close on the checkpoint's `made` row, now named for it, first serves a run with the link open and asserts that the propose is the frame after that row's answer; a new test closes on the answer to the edit's load and lets at most the `planned` row out with nothing made beside the workbook; every close test of tests/sequences.rs asserts that the runner ends by itself with its line `run link=closed` within 5 s of the close, so a runner that holds its request until the grace exit fails, and names each frame that came after the close; two handback tests assert the finish before what stands under it; a test of src/file.rs calls the file step with a write to a slot other than `workbook` and sees it refused with the cause `workbook` before any frame; the scratch folders of tests/support come from halvo_test_dir::make, and 15-r3-test-dirs drops their row; and the wall tests of tests/gates.rs and tests/fill.rs no longer need the run within 1 s of the runner's start. Six one-rule copies of the runner that break the close of A6.1 in a race window still pass every test (the review's b-01, b-02 and a-04, a replace outside the lock of `settle`, and a-01, b-03 and b-04, an effect row sent by a plain `ask`); they are placed as a unit of their own.
- Changed: In the account helper (billing run, unit 7.9, ruling P7-84), account/schema/ holds the four contract files again as backend/ of the same tree has them, with the status answer closing; account/schema/vendored.txt names each file by its path under backend/ with its sha256 and no commit line, `sh account/schema/vendor.sh` takes no commit and no R7_BACKEND_DIR and reads backend/ by path, its --check names each file that differs, and 10-r7-account runs that check in both jobs so that a later change under backend/ fails it.
- Fixed: In the account helper (billing run, unit 7.9, ruling P7-84), `halvo-account status` reads the status answer closing and prints `result=closing` with exit 0 and a sentence of its own on standard error that names no time, where it printed `error=failed-call` with exit 1; status maps each result of the route by name, so a result that a later vendoring adds does not compile until it is mapped.
- Added: In halvo-coord (interface run, U2.4i, rulings RD-67 3.3, 3.4 and RD-81 8.2, 8.4), the port method Owner::replies, which reads the agent_reply rows of one run with ReplyRow and ReplyState, a row that its reader cannot read kept as not known, and its read for an agent of the category email alone, in every move that computes that agent's holds while the store has ended its newest run and no answer to a check names it: a row unknown or unreadable makes the hold check stand beside a record of 0; once a check card was raised for that run its hold stands until the person's answer, whatever a later read of the rows answers or fails to answer and through a roster read that did not answer (ruling RD-134), in that life and, because the stored item_raised is read, in the next life as well, so the card keeps both choices and the row offers no Run now beside it (for an agent of another category the open card holds within its life alone, the named limit of the Fixed line); a read that did not answer starts nothing and raises nothing (the start answers state, the row waits for its last run to be closed); a roster read that did not answer leaves no reply rows or key entries of an earlier move in the facts. Facts holds the move's answer as Replies. The memory owner answers the read from rows that a test gives and refuses no_workflow and binding as agent_history does, and Watch, halvo-coord-app's Wired and every test wrapper of Owner pass it on.
- Fixed: In halvo-coord (interface run, U2.4i, ruling RD-81 8.1, 8.5 and 13.5), the hold check also stands when the agent store ended the newest run with a record of 0 while the roster entry's key unknown of the same read names an effect of that run, as the agent store's end_run leaves a releasing effect; Facts holds those entries, taken where last is taken (refresh, the creation's facts and recovery's); the memory owner's end_run now stores such a record and lists the effect as the store does (Ports::release), tests/run_recover.rs's refusal_interrupted_no_retry turns to that form, and formal_traces maps the model's run of unknown outcome from the record's count or the roster's key, the model unchanged. By ruling RD-130, for every category an open check card on the board keeps the hold until the person's answer, whatever a later read of the key gives, so Run now is refused with hold and both choices are taken; named limit: for an agent that does not answer mail and whose key entry left the roster, after Halvo is opened again the card of the earlier life has lapsed and the agent may start or show its ready card without that answer. By RD-81 8.3, the check card takes its kept fields from the facts of the same roster read that made the hold stand and makes no second read of the roster when the page is built (tests/coordinator_moves.rs's pinned call list turns from [Agents] to none).
- Added: The Windows keyboard source records a key only through a clearance of its password gate (capture run, win-3; contracts.md C13.8): `RawInputSource::keyboard` takes a `PasswordGate`, whose detector reads the focused field through UI Automation on a thread of its own while the source runs. A key is cleared only when the state at its input time, every mark since and a read of the detector's own that began after the key's 120 ms hold all read Clear, every read of the detector's own from the key to that read, that read included, found the element of the read before it (ruling W3-11), no Tab, Enter, Escape, Windows key or click before it still waits for a read that shows the focus moved, a mouse registration routed the clicks to the gate for the whole hold and, while the pointer source runs, its pump has passed the end of the hold by one tick step, and the same thread is in front (ruling W3-7). The gate keeps its latches, the times with no click route and the pointer's progress apart from its ring of 256 marks, compares ticks only as offsets of at most 250 ms from a key, reads a mark older than 24 hours as Unknown, and withholds a key whose own marks left the ring (`Rule::MarksLost`) or whose clicks the pointer's pump has not passed (`Rule::ClicksNotForwarded`) (ruling W3-17 Part 1). Only the native edit, Chrome and Edge, each told by two things that the gate reads, can read Clear; a field once read as a password field stays one until a read of the detector's own after a focus move; a focus event never clears a key; and a key not cleared within 250 ms of its OS time is withheld and counted nowhere, so `late_events` and `keys_withheld` are each a yes or no. What the gate costs (rulings W3-5 B9, W3-7 Q2 (f) and correction (2)): a key typed while the program in front does not answer the detector's read within 250 ms is withheld; a Tab, Enter, Escape or Windows key that moves the focus into a password field, or into a field of a kind that is not on the list, is withheld; after a click or a focus key that moves no focus, every key is withheld until a read shows a move; no key typed in a program of another kind is recorded; and a field whose text is shown reads as an ordinary field once the focus has left it. What the rule of the same element costs (ruling W3-11 B6): the key that moves the focus, when the program acts within about 120 to 150 ms, and any key typed within that time before a move that a read shows, are not recorded; in a form filled with Tab in a program that acts at once, nothing is recorded; one key after each failed read is not recorded; while the gate reads Unknown (a start while a window of a kind off the list was in front, a later read that found one, or a read that failed) and no focus event reaches it, every key is withheld, because a withheld key asks for no read: the reading ends only at a focus event that reaches the gate, a focus key (Tab, Enter, Escape, a Windows key, and so Alt+Tab), a click, a desktop switch or a new start, and no timer, poll or read ends it; a click or a focus key ends it but opens a latch, and since no latch leaves the gate by count or by age, the time with no key recorded ends only at a focus move that no key and no click of the person made, or at a new start, and never ends by itself, and the same holds while the gate keeps a Password reading (rulings W3-14, W3-15 B1, W3-17 X1); a control whose focused element changes with each key records nothing. What the rules of ruling W3-17 cost (X2): after a Tab or a click into a program that acts at once, and after any focus key or click that moves nothing (Enter in a chat box, a click into the field that is being typed in), no key is recorded until the program or the page moves the focus by itself, once for each such latch, where before the ring of marks forgot a latch after about 254 reads, which was the leak, and now nothing ends it; a held focus key adds one such latch for each repeat; while the pointer source runs, a key waits until the pointer's pump has passed the end of its hold, and a pump that is behind withholds keys; keys around a restart of the pointer source, or typed while the keyboard source starts, are withheld; a Password reading lasts until the next focus event or latch; and a desktop hook that cannot be set, or a first read of the detector that does not answer within 1 s, refuses the start. A mouse registration that fails when the pointer source stops holds the gate closed until the keyboard's window registers the mouse again, and a start after a stop of the detector that timed out waits for the old detector and is refused while it runs. The source contract gains the reasons `writer_gone` and `item_closed` (ruling PL-2 (6)), and the Windows functional check gains the native dialog and the Chrome, Edge and Firefox fixtures of phases A to F, the held-pump test, one `shutdown_not_ok source=<name> count=<n>` line for each source, the stall phases of the native fixture as a process of its own, the phases of the fixture page in Chrome and Edge (stalls, type swaps, a burst whose first letter makes the swap, a letter and a Tab out of a field that a timer made a password field, a shown password field, the cold and warm orders) and renamed copies of Firefox and Chrome, and as known defects a focus move that no latch sees (by the program or the page itself) and a type swap on a page that holds its main thread, each of which fails without its marker when a key is recorded in another way, and a text input that a page masks by style alone, the one stated exception to "password field": a box that a web page only draws as dots, without telling the system that it is a password box, is not recognised, and what is typed into it is recorded like any other typing; nothing that UI Automation reports tells it from a text input, the row fails without its marker on the day it does, a Mac is alike as far as the team has read, and this row alone does not hold back the switch-in of typing on Windows (ruling W3-20). The phases count keystroke events at the sink against the seam's verdicts and the page's letters key by key, and the stall and page controls ask for 5 of 5; a page control of the stall and swap tests that reads short is typed once more only when its own prints show every missing letter withheld at 250 ms with no latch open, the gate Clear through them and its read asked on time and answered late, and a run of the check that takes more than one such second control fails (ruling W3-18); a control whose page moved its own focus only after the first letter reached it is printed as schedule slipped, what it recorded is not read, and it is typed again, at most three times (ruling W3-19). The four page tests of the stall and swap phases start the source with their page in front, and the state after a start behind another window has a test of its own in Chrome and in Edge: when the seam's values show that state (the gate Unknown before the letters, each letter withheld by the state at the key, no latch, no event or own read since the page came to the front, the handler's counts unchanged across the page's own moves), it asserts that nothing was recorded and keys_withheld reads yes, then opens a new page whose five letters must be recorded, and the check prints after_start_unknown=<met>/<runs> (ruling W3-19, its amendment). Under the closed word HALVO_WF_FORM=short of the every-push job every test runs the fewest rounds that run each of its phases and waits once (one round where a round is a repetition; the burst keeps its five), split into two ci.d scripts, 50-windows-functional for the native group with the two registry and packaged-app known defects, which must run before the empty per-user Uninstall key appears in both registry views, as it does on the hosted runner about an hour into the job, after the native tests and before the browser tests, with no test of the unit as its maker (run 43817919408, ruling W3-27 (3) (b)), and 52-windows-browser for the page tests, the fixtures and the other known defects, each with its own functional line, seconds and ledger check; the known defects of the held swaps and of the stall self-focus run in the long form alone and each push prints their names with the ledger id, so a form of theirs that a browser or system update on the runner closes is seen only at the next long-form run; under long, on a scratch push and outside the driver, every test runs its full rounds (20 at each wait but 2,000 ms, 5) with the loops by exact name, and the ledger records both scripts' lines (rulings W3-24, W3-25); in the short form the check refuses a head whose tree of crates/halvo-capture, without the paths that only a macOS build compiles (src/macos, src/secure_input_macos.rs and src/custody/macos.rs, each declared under the macOS cfg; src/secure_input_macos_hold.rs, declared by #[path] in the macOS-only src/secure_input_macos.rs; tests/macos_stall_fixture.rs, which starts with the macOS cfg; and tests/macos_stall_fixture, which holds no Rust file in a crate without a build script; each named in the check with the declaration that the check reads again at the head, and no file that the Windows target can build naming one of the last three, ruling W3-27 (2)), whose check, or whose windows lines of Cargo.lock differ from what the passing long-form run that docs/baseline/ledger.d/win-3.txt records ran on (rulings W3-17 S2, S4, W3-23). A change in the crates halvo-control and halvo-sqlcipher, which the Windows build of the capture crate compiles, is not bound by the ledger and passes the short form on an old long-form record (ruling W3-26). Every functional test prints the per-user Uninstall key of both registry views before and after itself, and a test makes the empty key and shows that c5-watch's precondition removes it and goes on, its guard putting the key back as it found it in every exit (ruling W3-27 (3)). In the cases that stand as known defects, a key that lands in a password field is still recorded, and no product build starts this source. A focus notice that the system does not deliver changes nothing, which the hosted phase shows, and a focus event's Clear never clears a key, which a test holds through the detector's own focus callback (ruling W3-9 (3)). The renamed copy of Firefox is a limit of the hosted proof: the gate reads it as a program of another kind, Unknown, and withholds every key while it is in front, whichever element the keys reach, but on the runner its keys do not reach the page, so the phase does not show that a letter landed in the page's password input (ruling W3-9 (4)). The tests read each key's class and verdict through the feature test-seam, which only the crate's dev-dependency on itself turns on; that dependency is the one line of this unit in Cargo.lock, and the tree check 21-tree-bans holds that no other manifest names the feature (ruling W3-8).
- Fixed: The Windows capture sources name the app in front from the first event on and a hosted app by itself, emit a foreground record only when the process in front changes, keep a title only for the foreground window's own title, count one keystroke for each key-down transition, refuse a raw-input buffer shorter than its record, and answer Ok from `shutdown` only when every removal call succeeded; the keyboard source removes its Raw Input registration on the calling thread before it answers `Timeout` (capture run, win-3; ruling PL-3 parts (2) and (8)).
- Added: scripts/r1/windows-prints-rule.sh holds the closed forms of what the Windows functional tests and the print tool may print (number forms, words of the vocabulary and key=value of those; an address, a path or a URL fails), with the vocabulary read from the Windows view of halvo-capture that the ledger of check 50 binds and a fixed list of words, each with its reason; the new check 53-windows-prints reads the literal text of every print call and the format string of every format!, write! and writeln! of tests/windows_functional.rs, the modules stall and browser and tests/windows_prints.rs against it, and its self-test refuses a planted key, title, text, address, path and URL by name, in a print call and in a test's log. The function wpr_check_log of that file reads one test's log against the same forms for 50-windows-functional and names each line that breaks them by its number, its length and its first failing token, never by its text.
- Fixed: (capture run, win-3c; ruling W3-26) In the short form, 50-windows-functional and 52-windows-browser also refuse a head whose trees of crates/halvo-control and crates/halvo-sqlcipher differ from what the passing long-form run that docs/baseline/ledger.d/win-3.txt records ran on: the Windows build of the capture crate compiles both crates, and the functional tests execute code of halvo-control (`Modality`, through the events that every source emits and every test counts). So a change in either tree, as a change in the capture crate already did, owes a long-form run of the windows job (HALVO_WF_FORM=long on a scratch push) recorded in the ledger before the every-push job passes; the earlier limit of win-3, that such a change passes the short form on an old long-form record, no longer holds. The two whole trees are bound, also the paths that no Windows build reads (the macOS-only modules of halvo-control, the tests and notes of both crates).
- Changed: (capture run, win-3c; ruling W3-29) 50-windows-functional, and so 52-windows-browser, which execs it, reads the log of every Windows functional test after the test ran with wpr_check_log of scripts/r1/windows-prints-rule.sh (win-3b), with the vocabulary that wpr_vocabulary writes once per run before the build: a log with a line that breaks the rule, or one that the rule cannot read, fails the check, the check prints the rule's prints-rule lines (the test, the line number, the length and the token, never the line's own text) and a count line `prints_rule broken_lines=<n> withheld_logs=<n>`, and it prints no line of such a log and stops before any later step reads one. The rule file is bound in the ledger beside the check, so a change of the rule owes a long-form run too. The rule's standard error is read with its standard output, and the rule, the verdict and the printed lines read one copy of each log taken when its test ended, so a child that still holds the log adds nothing between two reads (ruling W3-31). The logs of the long form's loops are not read by the rule, a limit that the check's rule line names. The rule's vocabulary reads this check's lists macos_only_paths and expected_tests in their here-document form, and a self-test case of the check runs the rule over a stand-in of those lists and fails when either leaves that form.
- Changed: release-2 (capture run): scripts/prerequisites.sh installs the prebuilt cargo-release by copying it to a temporary name of its own run, made with mktemp in the cargo bin directory, and renaming it into place, checks its version again after the install, and falls back to cargo install only after a failed download or on a platform with no prebuilt; a temporary directory that cannot be made, a failed unpacking, a failed copy (a directory at the final name among them) or a version other than the pinned one stops the script with a line that names the step. A version check that fails after the install leaves the new binary in place, and the next run's presence check installs again. On Windows the archive's path reaches PowerShell as data, through the environment. rust-toolchain.toml lists the components clippy and rustfmt, the rustup line of scripts/prerequisites.sh passes one --component flag for each component listed there, and both jobs of .github/workflows/ci.yml lose their Rust components step. .gitignore ignores the capture store files store.db*, store.key, create.pending, writer.lock, daemon.lock, daemon-state.json, delete-all.intent and *.seg at the root of the checkout only. Named limit: from this commit a store file that a check leaves at the root of the checkout is ignored by a tracked rule, so the step Lockfile unchanged and the driver's read of tracked files do not flag it; a store file deeper in the tree still shows. README.md describes the tree as it is under its four fixed headings: Git Bash on Windows, the tool check, the supported systems, the test, lint and gate commands, the one version of the workspace, and in Release the crates.io lookup, the stop before store-4, the UTC date and the owner's steps.
- Security: release-2 (capture run): scripts/prerequisites.sh pins the sha256 of the three prebuilt archives of cargo-release 1.1.7 (aarch64-apple-darwin, x86_64-apple-darwin, x86_64-pc-windows-msvc), downloads with --proto =https --tlsv1.2, and checks the digest before it unpacks; an archive with another digest stops the script, which then neither installs it nor falls back to cargo install.
- Added: release-2 (capture run): check scripts/r1/ci.d/63-prerequisites.sh, in both jobs and the free lane, holds the pinned cargo-release version and its three digests against scripts/prerequisites.sh, each as a whole line, requires clippy and rustfmt in rust-toolchain.toml and the line that passes them to rustup, fails on rustup component add in a tracked workflow file of any ending, and requires each store-file rule of .gitignore anchored at the root, with no line that is the bare name or **/ and the name. Check scripts/r1/ci.d/64-readme.sh, in the macos job, fails when a fixed heading of README.md (Prerequisites, Build, Version, Release) goes, repeats or changes order, when the version anchor of src-tauri/release.toml does not match once under ## Version or names another version than sh scripts/r1/ci.d/61-version.sh --print, when the Prerequisites section does not say that the command uses no sudo, when a path that ends in .sh, .js, .mjs or .py is not a tracked file, or when a check name in a one-line command sh scripts/ci/run-ci-d.sh macos|windows [--self-test] NN-name has no tracked script; it reads no other form of a script or check name. scripts/prerequisites.sh checks for curl, node and npm, and on macOS for a Command Line Tools directory that exists, with xcode-select -p, before any download, and names each one that is missing; on Windows it warns that it does not check for the Visual Studio C++ build tools.
- Added: In halvo-coord (interface run, U2.4l, rulings RD-77 part 12, RD-89 and RD-90), items::native_card and Coordinator::native_card, which compose the native dialog's card from the held confirmation (its title and lines as the state's native item, one paragraph "<label> (<source>): <value>" per field from the new words entry confirm.field, and the page's two choices), the table of I4.3 with each field's admitted source and marking, the closed character lists at Unicode 13.0.0 (items::chars), and the refusal at the raise, with the end kind, of a confirm request whose deciding value is not its composed form, whose field states a source that A-A9.3 does not admit, or whose native card breaks its bounds, or that lacks a deciding field of its class, holds a field twice or stands out of A-A9.3's order, or whose native card holds more than three marks of the category M in a row, more than one mark of a below class in a row, or a mark with no base before it, all counted on the canonical decomposition (ruling RD-131 item 1, ruling RD-132 item 2, ruling RD-137); a card of two payments, or of a payment and a send (15 to 17 lines against the proven 13), is refused at the raise with the end kind, so that no card that a person cannot read whole is shown; halvo-coord-app's hosted target dialog_hosted reads each bound on a screen of both systems (a card of CARD_LINES lines of CARD_COLUMNS columns of the widest letter, of wide characters and of letters with a combining mark, whole inside the visible area and the smallest supported screen), reads on macOS the ink of a line with three stacked marks against its line box, and asserts that the ink of a line with three stacked marks stays in its field's room (its line and half of each empty line beside it), reaches no other line and draws over no control (ruling RD-136 P1); its one try at the largest text size changed nothing on either hosted system (NSAlert does not read NSFontSize, and TextScaleFactor takes effect only at a new sign-in), so CARD_LINES rests on the normal text size, a named limit (ruling RD-136 P2). Two more named limits (ruling RD-139): the count of 3 for marks above rests on this Mac's reading, because the hosted font draws no ink for a fourth mark on a tall letter, and marks drawn below that lie outside the five combining classes 202, 218, 220, 222 and 233 (for example Greek U+0345, Hebrew U+05B0, Arabic U+0650, Thai U+0E38 and Tibetan subjoined letters) still take the count of 3.
- Changed: In halvo-coord-app (interface run, U2.4l, ruling RD-90 items 7 and 9), the native card's bound is halvo-coord's proven CARD_LINES and CARD_COLUMNS in place of U4.5's placeholder of 16 lines and 120 characters, and a line, a title or a control text is counted in columns of display width (a wide character two, a combining mark none), so that 120 wide characters break the form and a letter with a combining mark takes one column.
- Fixed: In halvo-coord-app (interface run, U2.4l, rulings RD-107 and RD-113 item 2), the hosted target dialog_hosted waits at its start until its app reads active on macOS, as guard_hosted does, and otherwise runs no case and ends with its inconclusive line, in place of the run in which all four cases printed that the alert was never read.
- Added: In halvo-coord (interface run, U2.4m, rulings RD-80 3.4 and 4.1 to 4.6, RD-84), the mark of a move or a tick that did not return: the first call that takes the coordinator's lock after such a move publishes with seq raised also when nothing differs; a tick that does not return rings the bell, and every publish carries the new note note.out_of_date until a tick returns, and run_now, switch_on and the start of a ready item answer no_service meanwhile; the words entry and the fixture state out_of_date, which scripts/r4/words/lint.mjs lists; and the settle of a confirmation that an answer's move left answered (ruling RD-84): the first move after it lapses the item with the new cause answer_lost and stops its run with the stop cause answer_lost, in the order of I4.5, with one Runs::cancel and no decision, and the run ends failed, while a move whose settle cannot read the clock answers no_service and does none of its own work; until a tick of the life returns, the page gets the stand-in and agents_card answers no_service; the formal models take the cause (Confirm4's Causes, LapseOk, the stop set of Lapse and HAfter; Schedule's bound on the stop causes, answer_lost in Schedule.cfg and Schedule-store.cfg, ending failed), with the traces exported again, the replay of tests/formal_traces.rs, the 33 rows of the README's table of wrong designs run again, and gate-record.txt made again.
- Changed: In halvo-coord (interface run, U2.4m, rulings RD-80 4.1, RD-122 and RD-133), a run that stood on the board through a tick that did not return writes defer_launch and defer_ticks_failed null in its run_ended, not known, so that a count that the row holds is no longer a lower bound; by ruling RD-133, a tick held by a settle that waits for the clock counts one failed defer for each run on the board, a queued start waits in the queue while the mark of a lost tick stands and is started only by a tick that returns or a move after the mark, only the moves that take the waiting item or answer for its run wait while a settle waits for the clock, and the person's Stop and Switch off are refused by no read of the clock: the stop goes on the guard's table and the board at once with Runs::cancel in the same move, the waiting agent's own Stop lapses its item answer_lost and ends the run cancelled, and the stop's time and its rows wait for the next read that answers, which ends RD-101 (g)'s named limit (a row lost when the clock fails) for these two paths alone; a Stop or Switch off of a queued agent takes it out of the queue at any time, a runner's end that no read let through is made again at the next read with the person's cause, a held tick counts only for the runs that no stop has reached, and the hold_set of a Switch off under a failing clock is stored at once with the last time that this life read the clock, so its time and day may be older than the press; with no read yet in this life the hold_set waits with the other rows, and a new life before the next read loses it; by ruling RD-138, on that path the stop's record (the run's stop on the board with no time, the switch-off's hold and its hold_set) is written before Runs::cancel, so a cancel that does not return loses neither the Switch off nor the Stop's grace, a tick that reads no clock keeps the mark of a lost tick, a held tick that changed the state publishes, a quit under a failing clock leaves a waiting settle's item to the settle, and a panic in one agent's settle does not undo another agent's Stop or Switch off in the same move; by ruling RD-140, the same holds for a panic in another agent's waiting rows, kept end or switch-off that the move takes up first, whose work is kept for a later move.
- Fixed: Check 55 (billing run, unit 5.9 (i), ruling P7-94 N4) bounds each job: a seed of orderings at SEED_SECONDS (1,800 s), a setting of the fault-sequence search at SETTING_SECONDS (2,400 s), a setting of the bounded plan at ONCE_SECONDS (120 s) and the memory job at MEMORY_SECONDS (120 s), each lowered by --job-seconds N; a job past its bound ends its worker, fails the line with a sentence that names the job and the bound, and a worker that does not end within KILL_WAIT_SECONDS (10 s) of that fails it too; a worker ends itself within 500 ms of its parent being gone, from a thread that reads the parent while a job holds the event loop, and says so on standard error; each job line gives the worker's resident and peak resident memory; check.mjs --parent-gone-self-check shows the worker's end, with a real kill of its stand-in parent on a GitHub runner alone; and gate fixture bad-setting-runs-without-end holds the bound. A worker that sends no ready message within READY_SECONDS (120 s), or ends before it, fails the line by name; a result that arrives after its job ran past its bound does not replace the bound's failure; the worker's watchdog ends it also when its line cannot be written; and a seed or a setting named twice is refused (gate fixtures bad-worker-never-ready, bad-worker-ends-before-ready and bad-setting-named-twice). Gate fixture bad-rule-d2-window-48h, stale on main since makeMetering refuses a margin under the uniqueness window less LATE_READ_SPAN_MS, raises LATE_READ_SPAN_MS with the window, so that its first failure is again one identifier recorded twice.
- Changed: scripts/r7/ci.d/22-r7-backend-records.sh (billing run, unit 5.9 (iv), contract 9.8) refuses the record docs/r7/gate/55-metering.txt unless its line begins "line: PASS metering: plan=whole" and states fake-checks=22 of 22, orderings=100000 of 100000 and settings=41 of 41, each once and each with no further "of", and one plan= token alone, numbers that it reads from contract 9.8 and not from check.mjs, names contract 9.8 and the token in its FAIL line, and reads one candidate line with --metering-line FILE for the stamp script; its self-test gains 14 cases, an empty line given to --metering-line among them. 24-r7-wall's list of the files under scripts/r7/ that read backend/ names scripts/r7/ci.d/27-r7-metering-self.sh. The good scope of check 55's self-test (unit 5.9 (iii), entry 68 (g)) runs seed 1 with GOOD_SCOPE_ORDERINGS (1,000) orderings again, and its checkout line must read orderings=1000 of 1000 unless METERING_SELF_TEST_ARGS narrows it. test/metering/cancel.node.test.ts gains a named pin for each of contract 3.2's cases (i) to (iii) of a stated cancel whose event arrives after its own end (ruling P7-97 A1).
- Added: scripts/r7/ci.d/27-r7-metering-self.sh (billing run, unit 5.9 (ii), rulings P7-95 E10 and P7-97 C2), in the alone part of both jobs: check 55's memory self-check (MEMORY_SETTING 25, MEMORY_ROUNDS 4, at least 484 databases, at most MEMORY_LIVE_BOUND 2 live) and its parent-gone self-check each exit 0 with their PASS line, every bad fixture of backend/gate/fixtures/ applies to the files of HEAD, read from git, and no tracked file differs from HEAD after them; its self-test runs copies of HEAD's backend/ with the unmended statement cache and with no watchdog, a bad fixture of another check than metering whose text is not at HEAD, and a memory self-check that prints its PASS line and exits 1. scripts/r7/stamp-gate-records.sh runs the memory self-check first and stamps nothing when it fails, refuses to write any record unless check 55's line passes 22-r7-backend-records.sh --metering-line, and gains --self-test with eight cases, a memory self-check that prints its PASS line and exits 1 among them, which script 27's self-test runs; both self-tests refuse with a cause of their own where git has no name and email for a commit.
- Changed: citime-w1 (CI-time run): the step ci.d self-test of scripts/ci/run-ci-d.sh runs in two parts: first the driver's own cases, as two jobs of about half the cases each, and every self-test that does not run alone, up to CI_D_SELF_TEST_JOBS at a time, those that the new list scripts/ci/self-test-first.txt names (the longest of the windows job) starting first in its order; then each self-test that runs alone, one after the other with nothing beside it. The output stays in the order of the run, and after the own cases' two jobs the driver prints the case own-cases-in-two-jobs-print-each-case-once-in-the-order-of-the-run and one verdict line for both jobs. Each job of .github/workflows/ci.yml has timeout-minutes: 180 in place of 150 (ruling WS-9), with the bound ci_job_timeout_min of docs/baseline/r1-gate-bounds.txt and the case bounds-of-the-unit of scripts/r1-gate/check-cost.sh.
- Fixed: 10-r7-account admits a file that a build script of account/ wrote into its out directory in both layouts that cargo gives that directory, build/<package>-<hash>/out and build/<package>/<hash>/out with a hash of 16 lower-case hex digits and a package name of ASCII letters, digits, _ and -, which the Windows runner image windows-2025-vs2026 20261116.315.1 (provisioner 20261111.635) gives and which the check read as outside, so every Windows job on that image failed at includes-inside; as a named limit, in the new layout a package whose name ends in - and 16 hex digits is refused; its failure line now prints the file made real, the out directory as the dep-info file names it, as the shell holds it and made real, each test of out_dir_of with the name that it compared and each test -ef with both operands, the out directory found, and the walk of below up to and including the target, none above it; an operand that resolves outside the target reads outside the target, while the out directory that the dep-info file names is printed as spelled, also where that spelling lies outside the target and resolves into it, and the offending file is printed made real, and where the repository sits under a home directory the line shows that directory.
- Fixed: billing run, unit 4.13: the backend's node tests start the mock upstream of test/ledger/gateway-chat.node.test.ts, stripe-mock (startStripeMock of test/billing/servers.ts) and the node child of test/metering/stripe-meter-load.node.test.ts through the test kit (startGroup and endGroup), so that a run stopped half-way ends them, and the new test/ledger/server-stops.node.test.ts counts what a run leaves when vitest's main process alone gets INT while each of them is up, and after each of four stops of a run that started a group through the kit: INT or KILL to vitest's main process alone, TERM by pid to every process of the run, and vitest's own KILL of a fork; after a failed start, startGroup reads ps again and sends KILL to the child's group only when that read shows the child with a start time, except when ps cannot be read: then it sends KILL to the group of the child that it still holds, the one signal of the kit with no start time, which backend/README.md names; startGroup's errors name the command by its file name, and a command that does not start throws that error alone, with node's own error event of the child caught.
